# Requirements Traceability

## IR-123 · Disclosure notice describes external AI processing (FR-M6-02)

The shared notice describes manuscript extraction, external Voyage AI embedding
and reranking, and configured language-model answers and whole-paper summaries.
The consent label names external AI processing; the expanded terms show pending
CIT-U DPO / legal review. Modal clipboard output is derived from the displayed
sections instead of maintaining separate wording.
Settings identifies the text as the current notice, rather than as historical
terms accepted at registration (`SettingsPage.test.tsx`).

**Evidence:** `components/compliance/DpaConsent.test.tsx` renders the submission
notice and exercises Copy content in the modal. The old notice fails both
regression cases. Final command results are recorded in the PR.
**Partial:** institutional approval, the withdrawal decision and consent
re-collection remain outstanding. Existing consent stamps are not renewed.
See [the processing inventory and review checklist](../compliance/IR-123-processing-review.md).

## IR-538 · Announced email verification in the shared entry frame

`EmailVerifyPage` uses `AuthLayout`'s verify variant. A status region remains
mounted while loading and announces the result together with its reason; success
explains that RDCO approval follows. `ButtonLink` shares the button's variants and
size steps while preserving link navigation. Decorative status marks are hidden
from assistive technology, and the page leaves all three palette allowlists.

**Verification (2026-10-30):** `EmailVerifyPage.test.tsx`, `Button.test.tsx` and
`palette.test.ts`: 33 passed, including axe in loading, success and failure and
link destinations. Full-suite and browser geometry/contrast evidence is recorded
on the IR-538 PR. The initial full local suite was stopped during host memory
pressure after unrelated tests exceeded their timeouts; that attempt is not a
passing suite result.

**Browser evidence (headless Chrome, 2026-10-30):** success and failure at
320, 360, 768, 1280 and 1745 px, with an 800 px viewport height: no horizontal
overflow; the result ends at or above 530 px. Navigation targets are 52–54 px
tall. Primary contrast is 12.34:1 at rest and 9.53:1 hovered; outline contrast is
10.27:1 at rest and 9.84:1 hovered. At 360 px, Tab reaches the first action with
a visible 2 px maroon outline. There is exactly one h1 at every tested width.
Screenshots and computed geometry were captured locally; the IR-538 PR records
the check and CI results.

## IR-368 · Actionable API rate-limit messages

`lib/errorDetail.ts` formats HTTP 429 as "Too many requests" with an upward-rounded
wait from Retry-After, then DRF's detail, then "try again later". Other server
details are preserved and callers retain their existing fallback wording.
The fetch stream preserves status, retry header and detail just like Axios.
Ask IRIS, Paper Chat, Discover, Publish (the current Add Record flow), My Reviews
and the review/document-request dialogs use this shared formatter. Publish keeps
its field-error handling. The former Evaluation screen has been replaced by
My Reviews and the record review dialogs; no retired screen is recreated.
Error toasts have alert semantics and Paper Chat's transcript is a polite log.

**Evidence:** `lib/errorDetail.test.ts` covers retry-header precedence, detail-only
waits, seconds/minutes/hours, missing/malformed waits, non-429 details and existing
fallbacks. `api/ai.test.ts` checks real fetch Responses including non-JSON 429s;
`RAGChatPage.test.tsx` and `PaperChatDock.test.tsx` render throttling through the
accessible alert/log. The focused chat/API/Publish run passes (110 tests before
the final additional Paper Chat/API cases); TypeScript passes. Full-suite,
lint/build and final CI evidence is recorded on the PR.

## IR-371 · Configurable API request budgets

The anonymous, authenticated and Ask IRIS query rates come from
`API_THROTTLE_ANON`, `API_THROTTLE_USER` and `API_THROTTLE_AI_QUERY`.
Unset values retain `100/day`, `1000/day` and `60/hour`, regardless of DEBUG.
Invalid or nonpositive values refuse settings startup and name the key.
Only the development example opts into higher values; production configuration
and the separate overview budget are unchanged. The development guide includes
a verified cache-inspection dry run and narrowly scoped local reset instructions.

**Evidence:** `backend/apps/tests/test_throttle_configuration.py` imports fresh
settings without reading local env files: original defaults in both DEBUG modes,
independent overrides for all three rates, and startup rejection of malformed
values for every key. This file and `test_settings_validation.py` pass; Django
system checks pass with the existing axes deprecation warning. Full-suite and
CI evidence is recorded on the PR. No live cache entries were deleted.

## IR-417 · Proposal continuation and lineage

**Design:** [ADR-032 §6 and §12](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md).

An owner of an approved Proposal may create one Thesis / Research or Project draft through `POST /records/<id>/continue-as/`. The service locks the Proposal and the unconditional `record_one_continuation` database constraint prevents duplicates, including after soft deletion. It copies title, abstract, keywords, classification, PSCED, authors, owners and Adviser without saving the Proposal or copying its manuscript, review or consent. Each lineage link is filtered through `visible_to`. The route is declared in the cross-role access matrix. Historical migration fixtures deliberately use models from the actually applied migration state: reversing another app may also reverse dependent Record migrations, while a target migration's dependency closure may omit Record columns still present in the database. Their backfill assertions are unchanged.

My Library and Paper View offer the action from server-computed availability. The child opens the existing Publish dialog. Keywords and owner emails are editable in its shared Details form; owner changes require active existing accounts, retain the acting owner, exclude the Adviser, and are draft-only. A continuation requires its own manuscript before submission and enters normal adviser-first review. Direct submission remains available.

**Evidence:** `apps/records/test_continuation.py` covers preservation, editable metadata, authorization, lineage visibility, manuscript upload/submission and concurrent requests (one 201, one 409, one child). `test_capabilities.py` pairs offers with endpoints. `test_my_research_list.py` includes the new availability field and constant-query check; its exact-field assertion was deliberately updated for the expanded contract. Frontend: `ContinueProposal.test.tsx`, `PublishDialog.test.tsx`, `MyLibraryPage.test.tsx`, and `PaperViewPage.test.tsx` exercise the accessible controls, refusal, Publish reuse and both lineage directions. Focused backend: 22 passed; library API: 13 passed; focused frontend: 170 passed; TypeScript passed with the lockfile's dependencies. Final full-suite and copied-database migration results are recorded on the PR.

**Purpose.** The single mapping from requirement to evidence.
**Owns.** Requirement → design → implementation → test → evidence → status, for every SRS requirement.
**Does not own.** The requirements themselves ([`../SRS.md`](../SRS.md) is the only baseline) · test strategy ([`TEST_PLAN.md`](TEST_PLAN.md)).
**Authority.** **The only traceability mechanism in IRIS.** Do not create a second one.
**Update when.** Any change implements, alters or descopes a requirement — in the same PR.

## IR-576 "Not interested" with Undo, and a reset for my interests

**Design:** no ADR covers personal discovery. The behaviour follows the IR-557 spec text (user stories 6 and 7, metadata recipe step 1), building on IR-567's For you feed. Jira is not a requirements authority, so this gap is recorded here: an ADR for discovery feedback is still owed.

- **Not interested:** `POST /records/<id>/not-interested/` stores one `DiscoveryDismissal` per reader and Record (migration `records/0019`). It goes through `get_object()`, so a Record the reader cannot read is a 404, the same as a missing one. A repeat answers 200 and adds nothing.
- **Removed at once:** `_catalogue()` in `apps/records/discovery.py` leaves the reader's dismissals out, both when a snapshot is built and on every page. A page already frozen in a snapshot drops the Record and its cursor still works. A dismissal deliberately does not bump the interest revision: it removes one item and reorders nothing, so IR-557's "preference changes invalidate the snapshot" is applied to Undo and reset, not to dismissal.
- **Undo:** `DELETE /records/<id>/not-interested/` removes the dismissal and bumps the interest revision. It does not look the Record up: it deletes only the requester's own row, so it confirms nothing about the Record, and Undo still works after the Record became unreadable. Every old cursor goes stale (409), so the Record comes back under a fresh snapshot rather than an old one. It answers 204 whether or not there was a dismissal.
- **Reset my interests:** `DELETE /records/interests/` clears the chosen Areas and every dismissal, deletes the reader's snapshots and bumps the revision.
- **Saves are separate:** saves, folders and stars stay in this browser's library (`lib/recordLibrary.ts`). Dismissing never writes there, and removing a paper from a folder never dismisses it. Feedback is stored per account on the server, so two accounts on one browser never share it.
- **Interface:** For you cards only get a "Not interested" button. Discover then says the paper won't be recommended again and offers Undo for the latest dismissal; a failed dismissal puts the card back with an alert. Undo waits for the dismissal request to finish, and a page loaded while it is in flight leaves the card out, so a fast Undo or Load more cannot undo the removal. The Areas dialog has "Reset my interests" behind a confirmation that says saved papers and folders stay.

**Tests:** `apps/records/test_discovery_feed.py` covers removal mid-pagination without a stale cursor, Undo under a fresh snapshot, the 404 for an unreadable Record, reset clearing Areas and feedback and staling cursors, and feedback private to each account. `DiscoverPage.test.tsx` "Not interested, Undo and reset (IR-576)" covers removal and Undo, a failed dismissal, no control on Latest, folders and stars untouched by a dismissal, removing from a folder not dismissing, and reset with confirmation and on failure.

**Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tested. Not reviewed by a person yet. Saved Records do not yet feed For you; that is IR-575, which waits on ADR-044.
## IR-561 Paper Chat default reuse and idempotent sends

**Design:** [ADR-019 implementation note](../adr/019-persisted-unified-conversation-history.md), implementing IR-552 story 13 while preserving IR-569's deferred creation.

**Implementation:** one default Conversation per reader/Record, elected atomically at first Send; explicit additional Conversations remain available. A UUID submission claim precedes any model call. Duplicate pending submissions return 409 with Retry-After; completed requests replay the same permission-checked Turn; queued duplicates follow their existing AskRun. Partial Turns retain the identity. No claim is automatically reclaimed after an unknown provider outcome.

**Tests:** `apps/ai/tests/test_chat_identity_http.py` (real concurrent PostgreSQL connections, JSON/SSE and public-protocol replay, ownership and partial answers); `test_chat_identity_migration.py` (non-empty history survives migration); `test_durable_ask_http.py` (queued identity and cross-transport retry); `frontend/src/features/records/paper-view/PaperChatDock.test.tsx` (repeated empty opens, double Send and another tab's history); `frontend/src/api/ai.test.ts` (request identity and default lookup wire contracts).

**Verification (2026-10-29):** 109 focused backend tests pass (107 conversation/answer/queued/identity regressions, followed by the corrected intentional-disconnect test and non-empty migration test); 145 affected frontend page/API tests pass. TypeScript and AI-app flake8 pass; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` finds no missing migration (its database-history check warned about a transient Docker DNS failure). The full frontend run has three unrelated Sidebar failures caused by a Node/jsdom AbortSignal mismatch after fixing the affected Paper Chat mock. The full backend run could not complete: one attempt failed loading the harness with `Cannot allocate memory`, and the retry exited without a result.

**CI follow-up:** [PR #309's initial full run](https://github.com/moisturicer/IRIS/actions/runs/40795166357) passed Frontend (lint, tests, build), Security and Docker Compose. Backend ran 4,278 passing tests and found one vocabulary-guard collision: submission-response `pending` is not a workflow request status. The guard's existing documented allowlist now excludes `ai/submissions.py` for that separate meaning; no workflow value or behavior changes. Migration 0033 was also rolled back and reapplied successfully on a restored development-database copy, preserving its 19 Records; that copy had no Conversations, so the separate non-empty history fixture supplies Conversation/Turn preservation evidence. The disposable database and snapshot were removed.

**Status:** implemented and focused tests verified; CI rerun and independent human approval remain outstanding. Deterministic provider tests establish call counts, not production vendor performance.

## IR-626 workflow analytics: time in stage, open and overdue, throughput (ADR-043, ADR-011)

`GET /api/v1/reviews/analytics/` replaces the 501 stub, and `/review/analytics` (linked from My Reviews for the four offices) shows it. Figures replay IR-144's workflow `AuditEvent`s with AUDIT_EXPORT.md's clock rule (`apps/reviews/analytics.py`); definitions, thresholds and the relation to ADR-011 are in `docs/evaluation/WORKFLOW_ANALYTICS.md`. The access matrix row moves from `STUB` to `OFFICE_ONLY`. This is not FR-M7-01's deferred KPI dashboard, which stays deferred.

**Tests:** `apps/reviews/test_analytics.py` — exact figures from fixture event logs (median, nearest-rank p90, record-type cells, revisions and reassignment not restarting a clock, restart-all's second clock, unmeasured turns never zero, Manila week boundaries, the date range, revision cycles per scope, open and overdue now) and, against PostgreSQL, the endpoint's office scope, RDCO's narrowing, the 403 for another office, malformed filters, no reviewer identifiers in the payload, and the empty-log state. `apps/reviews/test_analytics_access.py` now expects 200 for the offices. `frontend/src/features/review/ReviewAnalyticsPage.test.tsx` (data start date, too-few cells, elapsed-not-effort, office filter for RDCO only, load failure, axe); `lib/access.test.ts`.


## IR-473 the document-access PIN ceremony is removed (NFR-S4)

Verifying a PIN unlocked nothing: no permission, download or view read it. `POST /reviews/pin/generate/` and `/pin/verify/` are gone (404), and so are `RecordAuthPinViewSet`, the `RecordAuthPin` model (`reviews/0020` drops the table), `core.utils.generate_pin`, both access-matrix rows and `reviewsApi.generatePin`/`verifyPin`. **The audit types `PIN_GENERATED` and `PIN_VERIFIED` are kept as `AuditEvent` choices**, and kept in `frontend/src/types/audit.ts`, so rows written before the removal still display. Nothing writes them any more.

**Tests:** `apps/reviews/test_pin_removed.py` covers three things. Both routes 404 and neither URL name reverses. Rows of both PIN audit types display their labels. `reviews/0020` applies over a table that still holds PIN rows, both unused and used. This file replaces IR-635's `test_pin_visibility.py`, below, whose endpoint no longer exists.

## IR-635 PIN generation respects Record visibility (NFR-S4)

**Superseded by IR-473**, above: the endpoint is removed. Kept as history.

`RecordAuthPinViewSet.generate` resolves through `visible_to(request.user)` before creating a PIN or emailing a title. Hidden and missing Records return the same 404. `apps/reviews/test_pin_visibility.py` asserts both the refusal and absence of PIN/email side effects, plus the owner's positive path. The IR-616 PIN defect markers are removed without changing expected access. Database execution is pending CI because local Docker's engine is unavailable; no pass is claimed.

## IR-636 analytics retains its office-only gate (NFR-S4)

The manually bound `reviews-analytics` URL now passes its declared `IsAuthenticated`/`IsStaff` permission classes into `as_view`, rather than falling back to `IsReviewer`. `apps/reviews/test_analytics_access.py` asserts Adviser, Student and role-less superuser 403, anonymous 401 and all four office roles reaching the declared 501 stub (**superseded by IR-626**, above: the offices now get 200 and the matrix row is `OFFICE_ONLY`). The IR-616 defect marker is removed without relaxing access. This does not implement analytics. Database execution is pending CI because local Docker's engine is unavailable; no pass is claimed.

## IR-616 authorization coverage across every app (NFR-S4)

**Design:** IR-611 slice 4, ADR-009, ADR-019's private Conversations and ADR-022's hidden-object convention. This extends IR-615 without changing application authorization.

- `apps/tests/access/table.py` declares accounts, reviews, AI, notifications, audit and opportunities in addition to records and documents. The pending/partial app mechanism is removed. Every route and method must have an entry; Django admin and the development toolbar retain IR-615's explicit namespace exclusions.
- Owned Conversation, AskRun and ResearchRun routes are exercised as all nine role/relation columns, including role-less superuser. Body-addressed Conversation and PIN requests also probe missing ids. A Conversation fixture contains a private Turn.
- `apps/ai/tests/test_access_matrix_bodies.py` checks readable evidence is present and unreadable evidence absent from JSON answers, live SSE and semantic-search passages/cards. Reopened history and queued replay/reconnect recheck visibility; queued checks cover both legacy and public-1 protocols. Only deterministic provider fakes are used.
- Notification tests observe unread lists before and after individual/bulk mark-read, including a shared broadcast and another reader's direct notification. Profile-edit tests prove a user cannot grant themselves a role, staff/superuser status or office-coordinator status.
- Source review found two mismatches, filed and linked separately: **IR-635**, PIN generation resolves a hidden Record and emails its title; **IR-636**, the manually bound analytics route drops its declared office-only permission. Expected rules remain intact under `KnownDefect` markers until the separate fixes land. The declared analytics stub has an explicit 501 expectation; ordinary permitted routes still reject every 5xx.

**Verification in progress (2026-10-29):** [CI run 40870491466](https://github.com/moisturicer/IRIS/actions/runs/40870491466) executed the full backend suite: **4,422 passed, 5 failed, 1 xfailed**. Frontend, Security and Docker Compose passed. The combined matrix/body-check duration was **16.025 seconds across 40 tests**, including test setup/teardown (JUnit sum, not total CI wall time). Backend flake8 passed. The five failures exposed test-definition errors, corrected deliberately: `role_name` rather than a nonnumeric `role`; legacy protocol requested by omitting the parameter, not unsupported `protocol=legacy`; ADR-019 retains transcript questions/answers and drops unreadable citation pointers, rather than deleting the Turn; IR-121 permits an Adviser to edit their own call but not RDCO's, now tested with both fixtures. No intended access rule was relaxed. A fresh CI run must verify those corrections.

Local Docker failed with `OSError: Cannot allocate memory` importing migrations from the Windows bind mount, then engine API hangs/500 errors. The container-local retry was also blocked. Compilation and `git diff --check` passed; no local database pass is claimed. CI reports the combined matrix/body-check JUnit durations in Backend logs and its job summary.

**Status:** implementation under verification, not Done. Independent approval and separate defect-fix PRs remain required.

## IR-615 every route is declared in the authorization matrix (NFR-S4)

**Design:** IR-611's spec (slice 3), [ADR-009](../adr/009-authorization-model.md), and the 404-versus-403 convention of [ADR-022 §Amendment 4](../adr/022-explicit-document-requests.md), including its one recorded exception.

- **Enumeration:** `apps/tests/access/routes.py` walks `get_resolver()` recursively. Nothing is hand-listed. A route is keyed by its app and URL name, and the methods are read from the view.
- **The table:** `apps/tests/access/table.py` declares one outcome per role (anonymous, other user, owner, assigned Adviser, ITSO reviewer seated and unseated, ITSO coordinator, RDCO, role-less superuser) for every records and documents route and method. The other six apps are `PENDING` on IR-616. Django admin and the debug toolbar are declared out of scope, with the reason.
- **A route with no row fails CI**, and so does a row with no route or the wrong methods.
- **Refusals:** a refusal of anyone who cannot see the object must be byte-identical to the response for a missing id. This is checked from `visible_to()`, not from the table. The only waiver is the `authorize_record_documents` exception, and the table cites it by name.
- **Defects found:** IR-627. Three upload and file routes answered 403 for a hidden Record and 404 for a missing id. The table kept the correct outcome under `KnownDefect("IR-627")`; IR-627 fixed the routes and removed the markers.

**Tests:** `apps/tests/test_access_matrix.py`, 16 tests in three classes:
- `RouteDeclarationTests` (7, no database): every route is declared, methods match exactly, the shadowed `api-root` really is unreachable, and the pending markers are live. A dummy route two `include()`s deep in a test URLconf is detected by name.
- `AccessMatrixTests` (3, Postgres): every records and documents cell, fired as all nine roles, each inside a rolled-back savepoint.
- `JudgeTests` (6): the judging rules on synthetic responses.

Run 2026-10-29 in the backend image against the dev Postgres: 16 passed. `apps/tests`, `apps/documents` and `apps/records` together: 521 passed, 13 skipped.

Two mutations were applied and then reverted, to prove the matrix fails:
- Dropping the owner gate on `submit/` failed 5 cells, each naming its role.
- Turning `uploads/<id>/download/`'s 403 into a 404 failed the `KnownDefect` cells ("remove its KnownDefect marker"). It also flagged the Adviser, who can see the Record and should still get 403.

**Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tested. Records and documents only. The other apps wait on IR-616.

## IR-624 the Review section shows whose turn it is and what each version kept or reset (ADR-003)

**Design:** Jira IR-624, building on IR-139's server-only `preserved`, IR-412's timeline and IR-619's `deadline_payload`.

- **Field:** `carryover` (`preserved` | `reset` | null), `carryover_version` and the server-worded `carryover_label` ("Cleared on v1, preserved", "Reset by v2") on each clearance in Record detail and the tracker, and on each tracker party row. The rule is `clearance_state.carryover`: reset means `pending` and rewritten by a revision version written after the row was created, so it survives a later revision that left the row alone. Preserved names the version the office cleared. The client infers neither state.
- **Visibility:** carryover is an outcome, so every tracker reader gets it. Seats, and so reviewer names, stay `null` to a viewer who may not read the review (IR-479).
- **Frontend:** `ReviewStatus` sits above the timeline: one turn line ("Waiting on IERC: 1 of 2 seats done"), then each asked party's state, outcome, carryover label, seats and due date. Overdue, paused and extended dates are stated in words.
- **Scope:** only ITSO, IERC and KTTO hold clearances. When the Adviser or RDCO asks for changes, its seats show "in review" again; no reset label is drawn for it.

**Tests:** `apps/reviews/test_clearance_state.py` (the rule) · `apps/reviews/test_tracker_carryover.py` (two offices through one revision cycle, owner and non-participant) · `paper-view/ReviewStatus.test.tsx` (rendering, a11y, withheld seats).

**Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tested. Not reviewed by a person yet.

## IR-625 the submitter sees where their record is and what waits on them

**Design:** Jira IR-625, building on IR-534's My Library dot and IR-342's rule that the frontend words no status itself.

- **Field:** `submitter_status` on My Library's list and on Record detail, sent to an owner only (`null` otherwise). It holds `line` (one sentence: *With your Adviser*, *In specialist review: IERC, KTTO*, *Revision requested by IERC: action needed*) and `waiting_on_you` (one item per party with an open revision or document request). Record detail adds `history`: each version, when it was submitted and each party's verdict on it.
- **Agreement with the dot:** `waiting_on_you` is non-empty exactly when `workflow_state` is in `OWNER_ACTION_STATES`, the states IR-534 counts.
- **No reviewer identity:** a party is named by its label. Why it asked stays in the revision banner and the document-request panel, under their own read rules.
- **Frontend:** `SubmissionStatus` heads Overview. Each Waiting-on-you link focuses its panel in place. A My Library link arrives with a `#fragment`, which `useFragmentFocus` lands once the panel renders.

**Tests:** `apps/reviews/test_submitter_status.py` · `apps/records/test_my_research_list.py` · `paper-view/PaperViewPage.test.tsx` · `features/library/MyLibraryPage.test.tsx`.

**Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tested. Not reviewed by a person yet.

## IR-587 the adviser picker shows no account state (NFR-S4, FR-M6-06)

**Design:** the decision recorded on Jira IR-587 (2026-10-14), following the IR-540 data notice.

- **Fields:** `GET /users/advisers/` returns exactly `id`, `first_name`, `middle_initial`, `last_name`, `email`, `college_name` and `department_name` (`AdviserOptionSerializer`), which is what the IR-540 notice tells registrants an Adviser shows to signed-in users. It used `UserSerializer`, which also sent `role`, `is_staff`, `is_superuser`, `is_verified`, `is_locked`, `consent_given`, `date_joined`, `is_office_coordinator` and `course_name` to every signed-in user.
- **Who may call it:** unchanged. Any signed-in user still needs the list to name an Adviser.
- **Frontend type:** `AdviserOption` replaces `User` on `listAdvisers` and its callers, so the type no longer claims fields the server does not send.

**Tests:** `apps.accounts.tests.AdviserPickerTests` asserts the exact key set a Student receives, so any added field fails it and forces a check of the notice. `PublishDialog.test.tsx` and `EditDetailsDialog.test.tsx` cover the callers.

**Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tested. Not reviewed by a person yet.

## IR-410 My Library absorbs My Workspace

**Design:** [frontend redesign spec §4.9 as amended 2026-10-11, and Appendix F F4](../frontend_redesign_spec.md), following [ADR-032](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md) and [ADR-003](../adr/003-clearance-aware-resubmission.md).

- **Defaults:** authors open on their own research; everyone else on Want to read.
- **Grouping:** an author's research is grouped by who has each record now (Waiting on you first, then the Adviser, the offices, RDCO, drafts, finished work and a collapsed Archived), using only server fields. The status line is `workflow_state_label`. The next step appears only for the owner-action states `awaiting_resubmission` and `awaiting_document`.
- **Office review on the card:** each office that cleared the record is shown, marked *kept* when it survived a resubmission.
- **Actions:** Continue on a draft opens Publish. Respond opens Paper View at Review or Files. `/workspace` redirects here.
- **Retired:** the case cards, numeric counters, filter rail, client stage derivation and the Workspace components.
- **Reading:** saved folders, likes and history stay browser-local in a "Your reading" panel beside the research.
- **Product context:** redesigned with the `impeccable` skill, which added `PRODUCT.md` (design context only; the ADRs remain the authority).

**Backend (Jira IR-410 comment 10547, settled 2026-10-07):** `GET /records/mine/` serializes with `MyResearchSerializer`, newest change first. It replaces Record detail, which cost about 15.6 queries per record there.

- **Fields:** id, title, type, authors, adviser name, `pipeline_status`, `workflow_state` and its label, `holders`, `cleared_offices` and dates.
- **Flat query count:** `tracker.workflow_facts(records)` gathers each workflow fact once for the whole list. It hands the facts to the same `derive_workflow_state` and reuses `clearance_state.is_preserved`, so the query count does not grow with the list's length.
- **Side effect:** IR-415's `my_seats` and `is_participant` leave the list.
- **Frontend type:** the rows are `MyResearchItem`, not `RecordDetail`.

**Tests:**

- **`frontend/src/features/library/researchSections.test.ts`:** the grouping rules (waiting first whoever holds it, the decider before the offices before the Adviser, drafts, finished, deletion and archived by stored status, empty sections omitted).
- **`frontend/src/features/library/MyLibraryPage.test.tsx`:**
  - author and non-author defaults, and the server's status wording;
  - draft Continue into Publish, and Respond into the right Paper View section;
  - holder grouping with cleared and kept chips;
  - no numeric counters, Archived collapsed, search and no-match;
  - the empty, error and retry states;
  - the reading panel's this-browser note and folder navigation, and `?section=saved`;
  - axe on three states.
- **`backend/apps/records/test_my_research_list.py`:**
  - the exact field set, and the seat and review blocks gone from the list;
  - `workflow_state` and its label equal to Record detail's for a record in every state;
  - holders for parallel review, a submitted record and a draft;
  - a kept clearance agreeing with Record detail's `preserved`;
  - ownership, the `pipeline_status` filter and ordering;
  - **the flat query count** (one record in every state against five of each).
- **`frontend/src/router/redirects.test.tsx`** covers the Workspace redirect, **`frontend/src/lib/access.test.ts`** navigation access, and **`frontend/src/test/palette.test.ts`** guards the converted page.

**Retired, with the reason:** `apps/records/tests.py::MineEndpointTests::test_mine_includes_clearances` pinned the full `clearances` block on this list for My Workspace's office pills. My Workspace is deleted, and the list now carries only `cleared_offices`. `MyWorkspacePage.test.tsx` went with its module.

**Evidence (2026-10-11, local):**

- **Backend:** `test_my_research_list.py` and `test_tracker.py` in a one-off backend container, **19 passed**; flake8 clean.
- **Frontend:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; Vitest on `features/library`, `components/shared`, `router` and the palette guard, **57 passed in 9 files**; eslint clean.
- **Browser:** verified at 360, 768, 1280 and 1745 px against a canned-API preview with the app shell, with no horizontal scroll at any width.
- **Full suites:** the evidence is CI on the IR-410 PR.

## IR-144 workflow audit events (ADR-011, ADR-003, ADR-004, ADR-022)

**Design:** [ADR-011](../adr/011-evaluation-framework.md) needs per-party and total turnaround
and the preserved-clearance count, and requires the instrumentation before the Week 11 pilot.
`apps/audit/workflow.py` writes one `AuditEvent` type per workflow act, called from the
services (`reviews.routing`, `seats`, `office_review`, `decisions`, `revisions`, `new_version`,
`withdrawal`; `documents.requests`) inside each act's transaction, after the per-record lock.
`create_audit_event` now isolates every insert in a savepoint and logs a failure at `ERROR`, so
an audit failure can never abort the workflow transaction (the IR-216 guarantee). Migration
`audit/0004` adds the types and widens `event_type` to 40 characters. Keys and the metric
recipes: [`../evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md`](../evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md). Display, filters and
CSV export are implemented by IR-529 (below).

### IR-529 audit viewer and CSV export

The RDCO Audit Log declares every backend type in grouped filters and shows actor,
timestamp (including time), Record link, and labelled metadata. Record, actor, event
and inclusive date filters share one validated API query with the CSV export.
`GET /api/v1/audit/export/` exports all matching rows in insertion order, with the
17 fixed columns mapped in `AUDIT_EXPORT.md`; no write route is introduced.
Deleted subjects retain their IDs; absent metadata and missing events are explicit.
Actor roles are current lookup values, not invented historical snapshots.

**Tests:** `apps/audit/tests/test_csv_export.py` covers RDCO-only access (including
other roles with `is_staff`), filter parity, all rows beyond pagination, CSV escaping
and formula protection, retained deleted IDs, invalid filters, empty exports,
read-only methods, and 7,200-second turnaround plus one preserved clearance computed
solely from CSV. `frontend/src/features/audit/AuditLogPage.test.tsx` checks every
backend type against the selectable options, labelled details, actor and Record,
filter/export parity, error feedback, and automated accessibility.

**Deliberate test correction:** IR-166's route-inventory assertion equated no detail
endpoint with exactly one collection route. IR-529 adds a read-only CSV collection.
The test now exercises GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE on a specific event URL and requires 404;
both collection routes separately reject writes. The immutability guarantee remains.

**Executed evidence (2026-10-30):** the Docker PostgreSQL run of `apps/audit`
passed all 56 tests using a separate `DB_NAME=ir529_verification` test namespace
(the pre-existing default test schema had a duplicate-column migration error).
The four Audit Log page tests passed, including axe checks. With locked frontend
dependencies installed, the full frontend suite passed 1,282 tests in 103 files;
`tsc --noEmit`, changed-file ESLint, the production build, audit flake8 and
`manage.py check` passed (existing Axes configuration warning). Full backend and
PR CI results are recorded in the linked IR-529 pull request.
The export is also declared `RDCO_ONLY` in the endpoint authorization matrix;
its route declaration and every role are checked by `apps/tests/test_access_matrix.py`.

**Recorded deviation from the card:** its "Clocks" rule starts a clock at `RESUBMITTED` for
`reset_parties`. A party that asked never stopped its clock, so none restarts for it. The
author's revision time is measured as `RESUBMISSION_REQUESTED` → `RESUBMITTED` instead
(AUDIT_EXPORT.md §Per-party turnaround). **Amended by IR-632:** under `restart_all` a reset
office that had completed is reopened, so `RESUBMITTED` starts a second clock for each of
`rereview_parties` with none running (`MetricsFromTheExportTests::test_a_reopened_office_gets_a_second_clock_under_restart_all`).

**Tests:** `apps.audit.tests.test_workflow_events`, read back through `GET /audit/` as RDCO:
every type with its metadata; `PUBLISHED` only on publish; `RESUBMITTED` under both policies
with one `CLEARANCE_PRESERVED` per kept office; document-request and revision events kept apart;
rolled-back and refused acts write nothing; a forced database error on the insert leaves a
routing act and a document request successful with an `ERROR` logged; and one Thesis's whole
life, from which per-party turnaround, total turnaround and the preserved count are computed
from the rows alone and checked against the assignments.

**Evidence:** the IR-144 PR's CI. Deployment smoke test and a sample export are recorded on
IR-144 after the Oct 17 deployment.

## IR-517 who is told when an owner deletes a record (ADR-032 §10)

**Design:** [ADR-032 §10, IR-517 Amendment of 2026-10-11](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md).
`perform_destroy` sends a notice per act after commit. On a withdrawal, each open-seat
holder is told in-app (no record link), including their party's document request when one
was open. Every other owner is told in-app and by email on every delete. On a delete
request, RDCO is told in-app and by email, with the record linked. No office pool is
told. `reviews.withdrawal.withdraw_review` also withdraws open document requests, and
`open_review_work()` reads whose work ends from the same rule.

**Tests:** `apps.notifications.test_delete_notifications`, through `GET /notifications/`
as each user, with outbound email patched where it leaves the system: seat holder,
Adviser at entry, pool not told, other owner told and acting owner not, document requests
withdrawn, sole-owner draft tells no one, and RDCO plus the other owner told with a link.

**Evidence:** the IR-517 PR's CI.

---

## IR-508 who deletes a record, and what a delete does (ADR-032 §10)

**Design:** [ADR-032 §10, IR-508 Amendment of 2026-10-11](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md).
`DELETE /records/<id>/` is `IsRecordOwner` (any owner; no office, no Adviser).
A draft or rejected record is soft-deleted now. A record in review is
soft-deleted now and its review withdrawn (IR-274). Accepted work becomes a
delete request for RDCO. A record in `pending_delete` is refused with a 400, so
a second delete can no longer skip RDCO's decision. Record detail offers
`delete_record`, `withdraw_submission` or `request_deletion` (`core.permissions.DELETE_CAPABILITY`)
to owners only. `IsOwnerOrStaff` is deleted.

**Tests:** `apps.records.test_delete_policy` (who; each status's act; the
bypass closed; any owner is the requester; the offer by status and viewer);
`apps.records.test_capabilities` probes each key in the statuses it names.

**Evidence:** the IR-508 PR's CI.

---

## IR-507 who edits and submits a record (ADR-032 §10, FR-M6-02)

**Design:** [ADR-032 §10, Amendment of 2026-10-11](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md).
The record update and `submit/` are `IsRecordOwner` (any owner; no office, no
Adviser). An owner edits only a `draft` or a record awaiting their revision
(`versions.details_editable`), matching the `edit_details` offer. Once a
record leaves `draft`, `versions.SUBMISSION_FIXED_FIELDS` (Adviser, type, IP
flags, routing hints) stay as submitted: unchanged values pass, a change is one
400 naming every field. Edit details shows those fields read-only and leaves
them out of the PATCH. Consent (FR-M6-02, IR-226) is therefore always given by
an owner. `destroy` is unchanged and is IR-508's.

**Tests:** `apps.records.test_edit_policy` (who edits, who submits and whose
consent is recorded, when an owner edits, the fixed fields one by one and all
at once, an office's IP tag surviving the owner's save, and what a changed
Adviser would strand); `apps.records.test_capabilities` with no waiver;
`apps.records.tests.SubmitOwnershipTests.test_staff_cannot_submit_someone_elses_draft`
(reversed deliberately from `test_staff_can_...`); three tests in
`apps.reviews.test_new_version` updated for the refusals (see the PR);
`paper-view/EditDetailsDialog.test.tsx` (the revision state).

**Evidence:** the IR-507 PR's CI.

---

## IR-418 capabilities payload (ADR-032 §10)

**Design:** [ADR-032 §10](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md)
and [frontend redesign spec §4.8](../frontend_redesign_spec.md). Record detail
returns server-computed `capabilities` for the requesting user. The frontend
adapter passes that list through; Paper View sections retain their existing
participation and paper-presence checks. Every action endpoint keeps its own
authority check.

**Tests:** `apps.records.test_capabilities` is ADR-032 §12's one table-driven
test: 9 states (draft, with the Adviser unopened and open, ITSO's pool, ITSO
seated, a revision ITSO asked for, RDCO seated, published, a Proposal with its
Adviser) × 6 viewers (owner, Adviser, ITSO, IERC, RDCO, a student stranger).
Each row pins the exact offer, and calls every capability's own endpoint inside
a rolled-back savepoint: offered => never 403/404; not offered => never 2xx.
`features/records/capabilities.test.ts` (pass-through, and the sections table),
`paper-view/reviewActions.test.ts` (Add reviewer's assignment), and
`test/roleGuard.test.ts`.

**Evidence (2026-10-11):** the table passed in a container against its own
test database, every subtest; the known-gap test below is a strict xfail that
fails with an AssertionError, as pinned. The table caught two drifts, both
fixed: `attach_file` offered to an Adviser, whom the upload endpoint refuses
(`IsStaff`); and `add_reviewer` never offered to a seated RDCO, whom
`add-reviewer/` accepts. Frontend tests and typecheck passed locally; full
suites: the IR-418 PR's CI.

**Decided by the project lead, 2026-10-11:** `tag_ip` is offered to office
staff on every record they can see, matching `tags/`, rather than on published
records alone; `add_reviewer` is offered on any open seat of an active office
assignment, RDCO's included, and Paper View's dialog takes that seat's
assignment.

**Known gap, closed by IR-507 (below):** the record update and `submit/` were
`IsOwnerOrStaff`, wider than any offer. The table's waiver of rule 3 for
`edit_details` and `continue_draft` is removed, and
`test_edit_and_submit_refuse_what_is_not_offered` lost its strict xfail.

**`decide`, resolved:** ADR-032 §10 (IR-270 amendment) kept `decide` "the
link to the current decision form for legacy records until IR-274". IR-274 has
merged and removed it from the frontend, so the ADR and the code agree with no
amendment: the server never sends it (the table asserts so), and Paper View's
header defers its primary to the Review action bar whenever any review action
is granted, which is what `decide` stood for.

## IR-260 adviser-first cutover (FR-M5-01, 2026-10-10)

**Design:** [ADR-032](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md)
§§1, 5 and 13. **Implementation:** `RecordViewSet.submit` now calls
`routing.enter_at_adviser` for every record type; it rejects absent or
self-owned Advisers and no longer accepts `declined`. The entry service writes
the Adviser assignment, entry seat, routing event and v1. `reviews.0014`
withdraws in-flight intake assignments for records with an eligible Adviser,
preserving historic reviews. `list_unassigned_intake` names records requiring
a human assignment. The frontend already requires an Adviser for every type;
retired intake history is labelled `Intake (retired)`. `seed_demo` drives the
new services, including specialist hand-back and a clearance-aware v2.

**Tests:** `apps.records.tests.SubmitOwnershipTests`,
`apps.reviews.test_intake_retirement`, `apps.records.test_seed_demo`, and
`apps.reviews.test_resubmission_regression`. The IR-233 strict xfail is
removed: a metadata-only revision now submits v2 and preserves peer
clearances. **Local evidence:** focused container tests for submission (7),
intake retirement (2), demo states (1) and the metadata regression (1) passed
against isolated PostgreSQL; frontend `tsc --noEmit` passed. Full-suite and
CI evidence will be recorded on the PR. Operational backup and restoration
steps are in [the cutover runbook](../engineering/IR-260-cutover.md).

## IR-138 transaction integrity (2026-10-06)

**IMPLEMENTED, awaiting PostgreSQL execution evidence.** In
`apps/reviews/services.py`, all five decision services now own an atomic
transaction from validation through writes and notification registration. A
record row lock serializes decisions before inspecting stage/clearance state.
A stage changed since the caller's read is refused; a same-stage office decision
rechecks its pending clearance under the lock. Notifications run on commit and
retain a snapshot of the decision's destination. Existing callers still observe
the updated Record instance.

`apps/reviews/test_transition_transactions.py` exercises the HTTP review and
resubmit endpoints: deferred delivery, outer rollback for all five actions,
failure after workflow writes, failure between clearance inserts, two office
approvals, duplicate office decisions, and a PostgreSQL lock-wait/stale-stage
case. Existing workflow characterisation and seed-demo tests remain unchanged.
Workflow `AuditEvent` creation remains IR-144; the boundary includes future
audit writes, and rollback assertions check that existing audit rows are unchanged.

Local evidence: Django configuration check passed with the existing axes
deprecation warning; Python compilation passed. Both the focused database test
and the full-suite attempt (`pytest -q --reuse-db --maxfail=1`) stopped at database
setup: the configured role cannot create `test_iris_db`. These attempts are **not
passing behavioral evidence**. PR CI supplies an isolated PostgreSQL/pgvector
service; its result must be recorded before claiming verification.

Contradictions recorded: IR-138's preserved original text excludes row-level
concurrency control, while its current acceptance criteria require concurrent
decisions to be safe. The minimal per-record lock implements the latter without
adding optimistic versioning or distributed transactions. W-03's proposed-state
sentence says audit events run on commit, but its scope and IR-138 correctly
require audit writes **inside** the transaction. The current source hierarchy in
AGENTS.md names SRS/SDD above ADRs while CLAUDE.md calls those documents frozen;
this change does not resolve that documentation conflict or change routing policy.

## Status vocabulary

| Status | Means |
|---|---|
| **VERIFIED** | Implemented, tested, evidence recorded and linked |
| **IMPLEMENTED** | Code exists, **no test evidence** — not complete |
| **PARTIAL** | Some of the requirement is met |
| **STUBBED** | Placeholder exists, does not function |
| **NOT STARTED** | No implementation |
| **DEFERRED** | Formally descoped, with an ADR or SRS amendment |
| **BLOCKED** | Cannot proceed — reason recorded |

> **A requirement is never marked VERIFIED because source code exists.** IMPLEMENTED is the ceiling until a test demonstrates it and the evidence is linked.

---

## Current position

**Almost nothing in IRIS is VERIFIED.** A pytest harness landed with IR-82 and the `apps/ai` extraction and chunking work now carries tests, so the blanket "there are no automated tests" that stood here is no longer true. Tests now also exist outside `apps/ai` — `apps/accounts`, `apps/documents`, `apps/opportunities`, `apps/records`, `config`, and the authorization matrix in `apps/tests` — so the "everything outside `apps/ai` has no test evidence" that stood here is corrected. This table records the honest position; it is not a to-do list disguised as a status report.

> **CI evidence dated before 2026-09-06 does not mean the test ran.** The workflow executed `pytest` with no database service, and `conftest.py` skips every `TransactionTestCase` — which DRF's `APITestCase` is — when Postgres is unreachable. Every database-backed test in the repo was collected, skipped, and reported green. IR-165 added the service; IR-163 made a wholesale skip fail the run rather than pass it. **Treat any "CI green" evidence in the rows below that predates those two as unverified**, and re-establish it from a run that provably executed. This is recorded rather than swept, per the source-of-truth rule.

> **This document is itself out of date in places** and its header still names `../SRS.md` as the baseline, which [`../../CLAUDE.md`](../../CLAUDE.md) has since frozen in favour of `docs/adr/`. Recorded rather than silently reconciled, per the source-of-truth rule. Rows below are corrected as the work that touches them lands, not in one sweep.

defaults are unchanged, so ADR-016 is not amended | `apps/ai/extraction/docling_client.py`: `DoclingExtractor.__init__` takes `do_ocr`, `table_mode`, `do_formula_enrichment` (validated against `TABLE_MODES`) and builds `_convert_options` per instance instead of reading a module-level `_CONVERT_OPTIONS` dict every deployment shared. `config/settings/base.py` reads `DOCLING_DO_OCR`, `DOCLING_TABLE_MODE`, `DOCLING_DO_FORMULA_ENRICHMENT`, refusing to start on an unrecognised `DOCLING_TABLE_MODE`; `apps/documents/tasks.py::_build_extractor` passes all three through. **Measured first, against a live docling-serve container on the dev stack (2026-09-30), one born-digital paper ("Nested Parallel von Neumann Architecture and Nested BSP", 158 text elements, 21,524 chars, no tables):** timings for the same `do_ocr=true, table_mode=accurate` option set ranged from 10.5s to 124.8s across repeated calls with no code or setting changed; `do_ocr=false` (`table_mode=accurate`) measured 363.8s on first use and 513.3s on an immediate identical repeat — ruling out "switching options is what's slow," since the second call changed nothing from the first and was slower, not faster; `table_mode=fast` (`do_ocr=true`) measured 451s, slower than three of the four `accurate` runs above it. **`do_ocr=false, table_mode=fast` was not run** — recorded as a gap, not silently skipped. `docker stats` showed `iris-docling-1`'s memory climbing across successive calls (46%→84% of its 8GB limit) independently of which flags were set, and its logs showed a full model-reload burst (RapidOCR, layout, TableFormer, CodeFormula) on requests that were not the container's first. **The individual flags are not the dominant cost on this container** — `DOCLING_SERVE_CONCURRENCY=4` and per-request model-loading variance swamp any signal from `do_ocr` or `table_mode` alone at n=1 per combination, so no combination reliably beat `true`/`accurate`. **Chunk counts** (AC1) were not separately measured: the test paper produced byte-identical extracted content (158 text elements, 21,524 chars, 0 tables) across every combination tried, because it is born-digital (OCR made no difference to a present text layer) and carries no tables (table_mode had nothing to act on) — so a post-chunking count would have been the same number five times over, not a fifth data point. Per AC2's rule ("defaults that keep today's behaviour unless the measurement justifies changing it"), **the defaults are unchanged** — `do_ocr=true`, `table_mode=accurate`, `do_formula_enrichment=true` — with OCR staying on because ADR-016 has no fallback extractor for a scanned submission with no text layer. **AC3 ("auto" OCR)**: not built.
