Non-staff refusal stays in `apps/records/tests.py` | 2026-10-29, local Docker: production and development images built from this branch both import `pyexcel==0.7.6` (`pyexcel-io 0.6.8`, `pyexcel-xls 0.7.1`, `pyexcel-xlsx 0.6.1`). The old `iris-backend` image reproduced the defect (`0 != 1 : pyexcel is not installed`); in it the new module now **fails** — 3 failed, `ModuleNotFoundError`, and `manage.py check` exits 1 — instead of skipping. In the rebuilt image `test_excel_import.py` + `records/tests.py`: 50 passed | **IMPLEMENTED** — `.xls` parsing is not exercised (no writer for the format is installed). Seen and not fixed here: the downloadable template's notes row sits above `END OF RECORDS`, so importing it unedited creates a record titled "Required", and its sample Record Type `Thesis/Research` does not match the stored `Thesis / Research` |
| **IR-637** | An Excel import row is all-or-nothing: its Record, primary owner and authors are saved together or not at all | Jira IR-637 (no ADR; decided 2026-10-29: rows stay independent, no whole-file validation) | `RecordViewSet.import_excel` saves each row inside its own `transaction.atomic()` savepoint. Before, the three writes ran one after another, so a failed owner or author write left a **published** Record the log called skipped. A failed row now leaves nothing, the log names it (`Row N: skipped — <title> — <reason>`), `skipped` counts it, and the rows around it are still created | `apps/records/test_excel_import.py::ImportRowIsAllOrNothingTests` (3 tests through `POST /records/import_excel/` with a real `.xlsx`): an author write forced to fail on the middle row leaves no Record, owner or Author from it while the rows before and after are created; a real Postgres error (an author name longer than its column, no mock) is rolled back with its row and the next row is still written; a forced owner failure leaves no published Record. All three fail with the savepoint removed. A write failure is logged on one line (`✗  Row N: not saved — <title> — <first line of the error>`), and a created row now names its sheet row too | 2026-10-30, local Docker (rebuilt backend image): `apps/records` 258 passed | **IMPLEMENTED** |
| **IR-638** | A cell value the system cannot accept skips its import row and says why | Jira IR-638 (no ADR; decided 2026-10-29) | `apps/records/services.py`: `parse_excel_import` only reads the sheet, and `check_import_row` decides. Title is required. A year is blank or a whole number from 1900 to next year (read strictly: a spreadsheet's `2021` or `2021.0`, not anything Python's `float()` accepts), and Year Completed is not earlier than Year Accomplished. Record Type, Classification and PSCED Classification must match an existing name ignoring case (`ImportChoices`, looked up once per file); blank Classification and PSCED stay blank, and a blank Record Type is Project (a database without Project says so rather than quoting a value nobody typed). Before, a bad year became blank, any number passed, and an unknown name was saved as no Record Type, Classification or PSCED. Every problem in a row is reported on one log line, naming the column and the value, and for names the accepted ones. The importer docstring no longer claims an unknown Record Type "defaults to Project". The template's sample Record Type is the stored `Thesis / Research`, and its notes row (IR-315's "Required" record) is now skipped, since "e.g. 2024" is not a year | `apps/records/test_excel_import.py::ImportRefusesValuesItCannotAcceptTests` (8 tests, real `.xlsx` through the endpoint): non-numeric years (`20O4`, `2_024`); years outside 1900 to next year, with 1900 and next year accepted; Completed before Accomplished; unknown Record Type listing the accepted names; unknown Classification and PSCED listing theirs; case-insensitive names with blanks kept; one row with five problems reporting all five while the next row imports; and the downloaded template importing its sample and skipping its notes row. Created and skipped counts are asserted in each. The pre-existing missing-Title test is unchanged | 2026-10-30, local Docker, on top of IR-637: `apps/records` 266 passed; 2026-10-31, after moving IR-563's Origin and Publication Year checks into `check_import_row`: `apps/records` 300 passed | **IMPLEMENTED**. Not changed: the template's sample Classification and PSCED names exist only where someone created them, so on a database without them the sample row is skipped with the accepted names listed |
| FR-M3-02 | Semantic indexing | ADR-013 (§Decision "Retrieval scope," amended 2026-09-08), ADR-007, ADR-015 | `apps/ai/chunking/` (domain, strategies, context path, normalizer, regions, re-chunk diff) · `apps/ai/repositories.py` (ChunkSet persistence; atomic swap behind the `one_active_chunk_set_per_record` partial unique index; incremental re-chunk with vector carry-over and `deleted_at` tombstones) · `apps/ai/ingestion/` + `models/ingestion_job.py` (lifecycle transition table, hash-derived staleness, job idempotency key) · `apps/ai/ingestion/pipeline.py` + `ai.tasks.chunk_extraction`, queued through the single `documents.tasks._queue_chunking_if_manuscript` guard that both extraction tasks call — IR-89 A–H, rescoped to the manuscript only by IR-195, and keyed on `PdfExtraction.kind` rather than on which task ran by IR-239. **IR-131 / [ADR-036]** add grounded answers with citations. Adapters key on the **wire protocol, not the vendor**: `OpenAICompatibleAdapter` serves Groq (development), OpenRouter (production), OpenAI, vLLM and Ollama alike, so switching vendor is a `.env` change with no new code. **Anthropic is not used** — it had been chosen in an import statement rather than a decision record, was never declared as a dependency, and so silently degraded every environment to extractive answers; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `AI_LLM_MODEL` are removed. Prompt assembly numbers sources deterministically and `apps/ai/answers/citations.py` parses the markers back to chunk, record and page, **dropping any marker outside the supplied range** — a citation pointing at nothing reads as evidence. The marker pattern accepts the CJK lenticular (`【1】`) and fullwidth bracket forms as well as `[1]`, and re-emits `[n]`: the configured default model, `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, cites in the lenticular form, and against the original ASCII-only pattern **every** citation on the real vendor resolved to nothing — zero citations, `is_grounded` false, and the raw marker rendered into the answer. `LLM_MODEL` itself was `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, which Groq has withdrawn, so every call returned 404 while `/api/v1/ai/status/` still reported generative mode; the model id is corrected here. **IR-252** is that reporting gap, re-scoped 2026-09-21 once IR-285 deleted the extractive lane it was originally filed against (`GroundedAnswerService`'s `UNAVAILABLE` state on vendor failure was already covered by `test_service.py::VendorFailureTests`, unchanged here) and **closed for `/api/v1/ai/status/`** on 2026-09-28: `CompositionRoot.generation_configured()` no longer reports `generative: true` from a key string alone. Past `is_configured()`, it asks the new `apps.ai.resilience.llm.any_provider_reachable()`, which reads the IR-321 circuit breaker already kept for each configured provider — a breaker only trips after a real `generate()` call actually failed against it, so an open one is evidence the vendor is down rather than a guess from the key. The one configured provider's breaker has to be tripped for this to report `False` (IR-385 deleted the second-vendor entry this used to walk). The disclosure gate runs before the prompt is built, because an LLM call always leaves the deployment; a vendor failure returns an explicit unavailable state with the sources still listed, never a fabricated answer (ADR-008). **IR-132** adds the resilience stack in `apps/ai/resilience/`, composed *around* the ports rather than inside the adapters — an adapter that retries or breaks its own circuit cannot be tested for the failure it hides. A circuit breaker with an injected clock (every transition testable without sleeping), bounded retry with backoff that never retries a rate limit, and a **Redis** token bucket rather than a process-local counter: the reference implementation ADR-015 borrows from counts in process memory, which is correct with one process and wrong with four. Separate ingestion and query budgets are the bulkhead, with the query lane given the larger share because a person is waiting on it. Query embeddings cache on normalized question text **plus the embedding space id** — mandatory, since a vector cached under a retired space and served against a current index is a cross-space comparison that survives a redeploy. `apps/ai/retrieval/degraded.py` degrades an open circuit or spent budget to chunk-level FTS through the *same* `visible_to` predicate, not `publicly_visible()`, so the answer does not depend on whether the vendor was up; the result carries `degraded` for a caller that wants to say so — as a field on the frozen `RetrievalResult` since **IR-279**, not on the `list` subclass it originally rode on, which every decorator stripped. **IR-320** classifies a vendor failure by *kind* (`auth`, `rate_limit`, `network`, `timeout`, `context_overflow`, `unknown`) rather than by exception type alone -- `LLMUnavailable`/`VoyageError` stay the one exception type per vendor (the anti-corruption boundary is unchanged) but now carry `.kind`, read from the vendor's own HTTP status or SDK exception class first and a closed message-heuristic fallback only when the vendor gives nothing better. `DegradableRetriever`'s `degrade_on` became a predicate rather than a hand-maintained exception-type tuple, because a single `VoyageError` can be either an outage worth degrading past or a bad key that fails identically against full-text search -- `composition._vendor_failures()` degrades only on `rate_limit`/`network`/`timeout`, never `auth`, so a misconfigured key surfaces as an error instead of a quietly worse answer. `retry_with_backoff` gained `give_up_on_kind` for the same reason, unwired to a call site yet (retry counts and the circuit breaker's threshold are the next ticket's). **IR-321** is that ticket: `apps/ai/resilience/llm.py` wraps `LLMProvider` in `RetryingLLMProvider` (retries only `network`/`timeout`, bounded to 2 attempts total -- not `retry_with_backoff`'s own default of 3, because a person is waiting on this call) and `CircuitBreakingLLMProvider`, composed the same way `apps/ai/retrieval/degraded.py` composes around `Retriever`. The breaker is process-lifetime and keyed by provider identity (`breaker_for`), not built inside `CompositionRoot.llm()` -- `composition_root()` returns a *new* `CompositionRoot()` per call when nothing is installed, so a breaker built there would never accumulate a failure past the request that saw it. `FallbackLLMProvider` switches to the next model in the list on `rate_limit`/`network`/`timeout` and gives up immediately on `auth`/`context_overflow`/`unknown`, recording which model actually answered on `.last_model_used` -- read by `GroundedAnswerService` onto `GroundedAnswer.model`, since ADR-023's recall measurement assumes one model per run and a silent fallback to a different one is exactly the confound that cannot survive undetected. **The contradiction IR-321 recorded here is resolved by IR-376**, which amended both ADRs on 2026-09-28: [ADR-008](../adr/008-ai-degradation-to-fts.md) §Amendment permits a fallback list of models **inside one vendor account** and confirms cross-vendor failover stays rejected, and [ADR-036](../adr/036-openai-compatible-inference-provider.md) §Amendment replaces "one provider per environment" with one adapter per protocol and the **vendor chosen per Inference task** — two vendors serving two different tasks is not failover. **IR-385 made the code match**: the `LLM_FALLBACK_*` second vendor is deleted rather than disabled -- settings, `cross_vendor_fallback_config()` and the config it built are gone, and setting one of those variables now refuses startup (`apps/ai/inference/startup.py`'s retired-namespace rule). What remains is a Profile's ordered `LLM_<TASK>_FALLBACK_MODELS` on one account, and who walks it is the vendor's dialect's to say: IRIS loops one request per model for Groq, and hands OpenRouter the whole list in its native `models` field for one request (`VendorDialect.resolves_fallback`). **IR-386 changes what `breaker_for` is keyed on**: `build_task_llm` (`apps/ai/resilience/llm.py`) replaces `build_resilient_llm` for a Profile's own list, wrapping the whole thing -- one model or several -- in a single breaker keyed on the Inference task (`InferenceTask.breaker_key`) rather than on `base_url::model`, so two tasks pointed at the same vendor and model no longer share one; a busy `summary` run tripping its own breaker leaves `answer` untouched. Because the breaker now sits above `FallbackLLMProvider` rather than around each candidate inside it, a switch-worthy failure handled by moving to the next model never counts against the breaker -- only every model in the list failing within one call does, which is the ticket's "not the first model's failure" rule (`apps/ai/resilience/tests/test_llm.py::FallbackExhaustionOpensTheBreakerTests`). `reset_llm_breakers()` needed no change to keep clearing every task's breaker between tests, since it already clears the whole registry regardless of key shape. Exhausting the list raises, which the answer path renders as the explicit unavailable state with the sources still returned (ADR-008). Evidence: `apps/ai/inference/tests/test_fallback.py` (the Groq walk, the kinds that stop it, the OpenRouter delegation, and that no second vendor is reached when every model fails). **IR-388 (M · Contract) deletes the untasked accessor those tickets built around**: `CompositionRoot.llm()` and its `_default_llm` cache are gone, so `llm_for(task)` is the only way any caller reaches a model. `build_resilient_llm()` and `_wrap()` (`apps/ai/resilience/llm.py`) go with it -- `llm()` was their last production caller, and every Inference task already reached its model through `build_task_llm` instead (IR-386); `_configured_providers()` stays, because `any_provider_reachable()` still reads it for the flat account `answer` inherits. `apps/ai/tests/test_llm_access_is_tasked.py` fails if the accessor comes back, via `hasattr(CompositionRoot, "llm")`. Django had **no** `CACHES` at all before this, so `django.core.cache` was per-process local memory — the same defect the rate limiter exists to avoid. **IR-130** wraps retrieval in `RerankingRetriever`, a decorator rather than a change to the retriever, so IR-129's visibility guarantee stays a property of one place. Recall is widened to ~100 candidates before reranking, because reranking what stage 2 already narrowed improves nothing. The **disclosure gate is applied before the reranker is called, never to its output** — filtering afterwards means the text has already left the deployment — and only when the reranker actually transmits, declared as `Reranker.transmits_externally` rather than inferred, since `NoOpReranker` makes no outbound call and gating it would cost recall to protect nobody. Rerank results cache on question plus candidate id set, so the same question over a changed corpus is a different entry; the Redis-backed replica-safe cache is IR-132's. **IR-129** adds the `Retriever` seam in `apps/ai/retrieval/`: stage 1 ranks records on the summary-level `RecordEmbedding` **with `Record.objects.visible_to(user)` applied before anything is scored**, stage 2 ranks chunks belonging only to the records stage 1 kept. Retrieval runs in Django, never the gateway (ADR-014 precondition 4), and reuses the one visibility predicate `RecordViewSet` already applies on every action (IR-153) rather than growing a second — ADR-014 rejected that on the grounds that two definitions of who-can-see-what will drift, and the drift is a confidentiality breach. The headline suite asserts at the seam that a chunk the asker cannot read is never returned, across every ground `visible_to` distinguishes (stranger, owner, office staff, assigned adviser vs. the adviser role alone, public catalogue, anonymous), plus that a permitted record ranking beside a forbidden one does not carry it along — the case that catches filtering *after* scoring. It runs against a deterministic offline embedder, because a security test that only runs where a paid API key is configured does not run. **IR-280** makes the Embedding Space the only thing in IRIS that names an embedding model: the `AI_EMBEDDING_MODEL`/`AI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` settings family is deleted, and with it the third disagreeing opinion about one number — 1536 in settings, 1024 in `VOYAGE_EMBED_DIMENSIONS`, and `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` in `.env.example`. Both vector columns were `vector(1536)`, a literal frozen into migration 0002 and then copied into 0004 — the hardcoding ADR-015 introduced `EmbeddingSpace` to remove, reintroduced by the newer migration. **`voyage-context-4` emits 2048, 1024, 512 or 256 dimensions and never 1536**, so every write of a real vector would have failed. Migration `0007` writes the `voyage-context-4`/1024/cosine space first and then reads the column width back *off that row*, so nothing in it names a dimension twice; the one number the schema still has to declare in Python lives as `apps.ai.models.VECTOR_COLUMN_DIMENSIONS`, because a column width is a schema fact and cannot follow an environment variable. The superseded space is **retired, not deleted** — `ChunkEmbedding` points at a space with `on_delete=CASCADE`, so deleting one takes its vectors with it. Vectors from a superseded space *are* deleted, deliberately and irreversibly: a 1536-vector cannot be cast into a 1024 column, and a vector from a model IRIS no longer calls is not comparable with one from the model it does. That is free today, when neither table holds a row, and a full re-index after IR-281 — which is why this is sequenced ahead of it. `VOYAGE_EMBED_DIMENSIONS` goes too: what the adapter asks Voyage to emit and what the column can hold are one number, and while they were two settings `VOYAGE_EMBED_DIMENSIONS=512` would have produced 512-vectors for a 1024 column — the same bug one hop along. **Two contradictions recorded rather than reconciled.** First, migration 0003's `RunPython` body is edited, against CLAUDE.md §Migrations' "never edit an applied migration" and `docs/engineering/DEVELOPMENT.md` §94's "supersede it": the settings it read are deleted, so left alone it raises on every fresh database, and the frozen literals reproduce byte-for-byte what it seeded. No applied database re-runs it. Second, `ai.tasks.embed_record` still posts to the gateway route ADR-024 records as non-existent; IR-280 changed only the `model_name` label it writes, because the setting that supplied it is gone, and replacing the call with the `EmbeddingProvider` port is IR-281's work. **The deletion of superseded vectors is deliberate, irreversible and more than the ticket asked for — it wants a reviewer's explicit sign-off.** **IR-281** computes the first chunk vectors IRIS has ever held, and takes the indexing path off the AI gateway (ADR-024). Two defects, both of which had hidden behind the fact that nothing on this path ever produced a vector. The adapter posted to the flat `/embeddings` endpoint, which serves the *standard* models: `voyage-context-4` is a **contextualized** chunk embedder served from `/contextualizedembeddings`, and its entire value is that a chunk is embedded with its sibling chunks from the same document in view — flatten that and "this approach reduced error by 12%" stops meaning anything once retrieved alone, while the vector itself still looks perfectly well-formed. `EmbeddingProvider` grows `embed_document_chunks`, which takes chunks **grouped by document** and returns vectors in the same shape; `embed_documents` and `embed_query` now route through the same endpoint, each text as its own one-chunk document, so there is one wire format rather than two to keep in step. The batching unit becomes the document — `batch_documents_by_token_budget` never splits one, because half a document's chunks in view is not the context the model was chosen for. `tasks.embed_record` stops posting to `{AI_GATEWAY_URL}/api/v1/ai/internal/embed/`, a route the gateway never registered at an endpoint whose `EmbedResponse` carries no `embedding` field, and embeds in-process through the port; a test walks every module under `apps/ai/` and fails if any reads the gateway URL, so the deployment promise is asserted rather than trusted. `apps/ai/indexing.py` is the new seam both tasks and IR-282's backfill share: it checks the active `EmbeddingSpace` against the vector columns **and** against the provider's own width, applies the disclosure gate, and confirms there is something left to embed — all **before** a metered call, since a vendor call that turns out to have been unnecessary is the cheapest bug to prevent and the hardest to spot afterwards. Vectors are keyed by chunk **and** space; "already has a row in this space" *is* the unchanged-text skip, reusing the carry-over the repository already does rather than inventing a second definition of unchanged. `ai.tasks.embed_chunk_set` runs it on the `embedding` queue and `chunk_extraction` queues it whenever a run wrote a new active chunk set, so an upload reaches vectors with no manual step. **One limitation recorded rather than reconciled:** the disclosure gate refuses **every** record today, because `Record` carries no embargo field and an undetermined embargo is treated as an embargo (IR-250) — the correct failure direction, and the reason a real corpus cannot be indexed until that ticket lands. Nothing here works around it; the way past a fail-closed gate is to supply the missing fact. A second, smaller trade: an incremental re-chunk sends only the changed chunks, so their context is the changed passages rather than the whole document, because restoring full context means paying for every chunk — `force=True` chooses the other trade. **IR-282** makes indexing a corpus an operator command with the bill shown first, and makes promotion a separate, refusable decision. `manage.py backfill_embeddings` walks Records, prints a pre-flight estimate of chunks, tokens and approximate cost, and **refuses to start** when the estimate exceeds `AI_EMBEDDING_TOKEN_CEILING` — a live risk rather than a theoretical one, since `AI_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` counts whitespace words and undercounts real BPE tokens by roughly 44% (IR-243). It is **resumable and idempotent by construction, not by a checkpoint file**: what it embeds is "active chunks with no vector in this space", recomputed each run, so a crash at record 3,000 resumes there and bookkeeping cannot be lost in the same crash that made it necessary. `--queue` hands each record to `ai.tasks.index_record` on the `embedding` queue with one `EmbeddingJob` per record, so a failed record records why; inline, a record's failure is reported and the run continues, leaving it pending for the next one. **The 4,910 placeholder uploads are skipped by construction** — sixteen bytes of `%PDF-1.7 fake bytes` never extracted, so they have no chunk set — and are *counted and named* in the report, because five thousand silently absent records look exactly like a broken run; nothing deletes them. `manage.py promote_embedding_space --space N` is the second half and is deliberately a separate command: it **refuses while any active chunk lacks a vector in that space** and lists which records are short and by how much, retires the superseded space rather than deleting it (vectors point at a space with `on_delete=CASCADE`), and promotes nothing automatically. `--space` on the backfill fills a **pending** space so it can be promoted once complete; **recorded limitation** — that mode writes chunk vectors only, because `RecordEmbedding` is one row per record with no space key, so a summary vector cannot exist in two spaces at once. **Review pass on IR-281/IR-282** closed four gaps the two-axis review found. A record whose chunks are all embedded but whose **summary vector** is missing was never walked again — stage 1 ranks records on `RecordEmbedding` before stage 2 ranks chunks within them, so such a record is invisible to retrieval however complete its chunks are; the plan now counts summaries owing, gated on the record having chunks at all so the 4,910 placeholders do not walk straight back into the run the stub skip keeps them out of. An **inline failure is now written to an `EmbeddingJob` row**, not only printed: a terminal that is then closed is not a record of anything, and the ticket asks that a failed per-record job say why. **Promotion refuses a retired space and a space of the wrong width**, not only an incomplete one — `indexing._checked_space` already refused to *write* to a retired space and the two disagreed about the same row. The partial-context trade-off on an incremental re-chunk is now carried as `EmbeddingOutcome.partial_context` rather than living only in a docstring, because partly-contextualized vectors are indistinguishable from fully contextualized ones. "A record's active, non-deleted chunks" was written out four times and is now one `indexing.active_chunks()`; four copies of a predicate that decides what a run pays for is a bill, not a wrong number. **IR-128** adds the query lane's vendor seams in `apps/ai/providers/`: an `EmbeddingProvider` with `embed_documents`/`embed_query` as separate methods (ADR-015 rule 3 — a boolean flag makes the wrong asymmetric call possible), a `Reranker` port with `NoOpReranker` as the production default so IR-133 can measure reranking's value by configuration rather than by code path, and the Voyage adapters for both. Batching enforces ADR-015's **token** budget rather than an item count; since **IR-287** it counts with the same tokenizer the chunker uses rather than a words-times-1.4 factor, so there is one definition of a token on both sides of the seam. Deterministic hash-based fakes keep the whole lane testable without a vendor account, which matters because IR-129's retrieval tests are the security tests. The empty `cohere_reranker.py` and `vector_retriever.py` prototype stubs are deleted — ADR-015 rejected Cohere on one-vendor grounds. **IR-249** aligns the two timeouts governing one Docling conversion: the worker waited `DOCLING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=900` while docling-serve, with `DOCLING_SERVE_MAX_SYNC_WAIT` unset and so at its own default of 120, abandoned the request and returned 504 — the server's cap being the binding one. A 45-page submission converts in about a minute alone, so every single-document run passed and **bulk ingestion failed entirely**, with the API returning 201 either way. `apps/tests/test_docling_timeouts.py` reads both Compose files and fails if the two values disagree, because the extraction tests run against a fake extractor and cannot observe container configuration — the blind spot that also let IR-240 ship green. **IR-248** stops an unnumbered heading evicting the numbered section it belongs to: Docling promotes a great deal to `section_header` that carries no number — 61 of 92 headings on a real SRS — and at level 1 each displaced its parent, so `1.3` and `1.4` were trailed to a bullet-list lead-in rather than to `1. Introduction`. An unnumbered heading now sits one level below the deepest numbered entry open. Recorded trade-off: a trailing unnumbered section such as an appendix is nested under the last numbered section instead of beside it, which the numbering-sequence check IR-242 also wants would resolve. Depth on record 30 moved from 54/20/1 across depths 2/3/4 to 8/18/34 plus 15 at depth 5, with record 29 improving too and not regressing. **IR-314** teaches the outline reader the two numbering styles it did not know: roman-numeral sections (`I.`, `IV.`) and lettered subsections (`A.`, `E.`), which is how most scientific papers number and which matched nothing, so every heading in such a paper reported "no depth" and the whole document flattened — all 717 chunks of the corpus at path depth 2. The recognition moves into the domain as `chunking/numbering.py`, which `docling_mapping.py` now imports rather than duplicating by shape, and which reads the whole heading *sequence* rather than one heading: `I`, `V`, `X`, `L`, `C`, `D` and `M` are simultaneously letters and roman numerals, so `C. Committors` between `B.` and `D.` is a subsection while the same string opening a paper is section 100, and only the run it does or does not continue tells them apart. A letter's depth is relative to the numbered section open above it — depth 2 under `I.`, depth 3 under `3.2` — or it would evict its own parent. Dotted decimals are unchanged and asserted so, and the decimal false positive IR-242 recorded (`3.5 kg of Feed`) is neither widened nor narrowed: both new styles require a separator a measurement does not have. **IR-241** folds a heading-only chunk forward into the section it labels: `_sectionize` opens a section at every heading, so a heading whose first child is another heading formed a section of one element that section-scoped merging could not reach, and a real 47-page submission produced six such 2–3 token chunks. The fold is the one narrow exception to “never merge across a heading” (IR-89 user story 21) and is scoped to chunks carrying no content — a label rather than a topic; two sections that both have a body still never merge. Because a chunk can now span a heading, `context_path.py` takes the trail at a chunk’s *last* word rather than its first, without which the fold would have silently made the path less specific than before. `PdfExtraction` keys on `upload` *or* `record` (`resolved_record_id`) and carries an explicit `kind` (manuscript/supplementary, defaulting to supplementary so an unmarked row stays out of the corpus); both creation sites — `SubmitDocumentView` and `RecordViewSet` — set it explicitly **IR-247** detects running furniture by repetition across pages rather than by the extractor's label: Docling labelled the same footer `page_header` 18 times and `paragraph` 27 times in one document, and the 27 survivors reached 20 of 75 chunks. Eligibility is deliberately narrow — short `PARAGRAPH` elements only, counted by *distinct pages* — so a repeated table header, which IR-111 works to preserve in every fragment of a split table, can never be swept up. **IR-241** folds a heading-only chunk forward into the section it labels: `_sectionize` opens a section at every heading, so a heading whose first child is another heading formed a section of one element that section-scoped merging could not reach, and a real 47-page submission produced six such 2–3 token chunks. The fold is the one narrow exception to “never merge across a heading” (IR-89 user story 21) and is scoped to chunks carrying no content — a label rather than a topic; two sections that both have a body still never merge. Because a chunk can now span a heading, `context_path.py` takes the trail at a chunk’s *last* word rather than its first, without which the fold would have silently made the path less specific than before. `PdfExtraction` keys on `upload` *or* `record` (`resolved_record_id`) and carries an explicit `kind` (manuscript/supplementary, defaulting to supplementary so an unmarked row stays out of the corpus); both creation sites — `SubmitDocumentView` and `RecordViewSet` — set it explicitly **IR-245** emits the captions Docling hangs off `#/pictures/N` and `#/tables/N`: `_items_in_reading_order` walked `body.children` only, so on a real 45-page SRS 13 of 15 captions were dropped — including `Figure 1. - IRIS System Flow Diagram` in a document carrying 33 figures, whose captions are the only retrievable text a figure has. Each caption keeps its own `prov`, so a citation highlights the caption rather than the whole image, and the existing `seen` set keeps a caption listed in both places from being emitted twice. docling-core's own `HybridChunker` retains 11 of the 15 on the same document; this retains all 15. **IR-241** folds a heading-only chunk forward into the section it labels: `_sectionize` opens a section at every heading, so a heading whose first child is another heading formed a section of one element that section-scoped merging could not reach, and a real 47-page submission produced six such 2–3 token chunks. METHODS > E. The GenAIMMD algorithm > Self-consistency loop`; full backend suite green (1069 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed, 2026-09-19); **IR-280** verified with `manage.py check` and `migrate` on a fresh database (every test run builds one) plus `apps/ai/tests/test_vector_dimensions.py`, which reads the live column type out of `pg_attribute` rather than trusting the Django field — the field being right while the column was wrong is the defect — and asserts it equals both the active space's dimensions and `VECTOR_COLUMN_DIMENSIONS`, for every vector table. `apps/ai/tests/test_vector_redimension_migration.py` runs 0007 against a database that **already holds a 1536-vector**, the case an empty database hides: the first version of the migration failed there with "expected 1024 dimensions, not 1536", found by the pre-existing 0003/0004 migration tests. `apps/ai/providers/tests/test_voyage_adapter.py::OutputDimensionTests` asserts the adapter puts `VECTOR_COLUMN_DIMENSIONS` on the wire as `output_dimension`. That database holds none of the 4,910 placeholder uploads, so the stub path is covered by tests only; **the manual inspection IR-116 requires — fifty chunks from a real submission read by a person, and one real scan — has not been done, so the token ceiling and the front-matter decision are still the untested defaults**; **IR-243** recorded that `AI_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` counted whitespace *words* rather than tokenizer tokens (511 words at the ceiling against docling-core `HybridChunker(max_tokens=512)`'s 355 on the same PDF) and deliberately did not recalibrate it. **IR-287 closes that**: `count_tokens` is the real `voyage-context-4` tokenizer and the ceiling is 700 real tokens — the measured equivalent of the 512 words it replaced, so the unit became honest and chunk boundaries on prose did not move. What the right ceiling is for theses remains open for IR-133's recall@10 harness, but the number now means what it says; backfilling chunk sets for records whose manuscript predates IR-195 is explicitly out of scope, not done; IR-239 renamed the chunking task, so any message queued under the old names at deploy time is lost — drain the `default` queue before deploying it. **IR-327** adds the reasoning channel on top of IR-325/IR-326's streaming endpoint: `StreamDelta.reasoning` (the vendor's own dedicated channel) and a defensive `<think>...</think>` split of the text channel (the reported gpt-oss-120b/Groq leak) both become `ReasoningDelta`, never concatenated into the stored answer and never scanned by `parse_citations`; `Turn.had_reasoning` (migration 0012) records only that reasoning happened, never the reasoning text. `apps/ai` green (1010 passed) and the full backend suite green, no `F`/`FAILED`/`ERROR` in either run (2026-09-22). **IR-328** adds `Turn.state == "partial"` for a stream that ends without reaching its own `Done` -- cause-agnostic, so a client disconnect and an unexpected vendor error (anything `LLMUnavailable` does not already turn into an honest, complete `done`) both record identically. `answer_stream` gains `on_interrupted`, called exactly once from its own `finally` with whatever text and sources had been accumulated, citation-parsed the one time a clean completion also does it (never `_warn_if_citations_went_missing`, since ending mid-marker is expected here, not a drifted format); `ChatStreamView` wires it to the same `record_turn` the clean path uses. `Turn.state` gains `choices` for the first time, migration `0013`, documenting the four values `record_turn` was already free to write. `degraded` is always true on a partial answer -- the same unconditional call `_unavailable_answer` already makes, not `retrieved.degraded`, which answers a different question. **Recorded, not fixed here**: `presentation.answer_mode` still has no `partial` case and falls through to `generative`, so a reopened transcript currently replays a truncated Turn as if it were complete -- out of this ticket's stated scope (a two-axis review flagged wiring one in as scope creep against IR-328's four acceptance criteria, none of which mention replay), left for a follow-up. Evidence: `apps/ai/answers/tests/test_service.py::InterruptedStreamTests` (an unexpected mid-stream exception calling `on_interrupted` once with a partial, degraded, citation-parsed answer while the exception itself still propagates; never called on a clean completion or on an `unavailable` done; a caller closing the generator early via `GeneratorExit` counted identically), `apps/ai/tests/test_ask_stream_http.py::InterruptedStreamTests` (the same HTTP seam IR-326 established, driven with a scripted mid-sequence cutoff -- a persisted `partial` Turn with its truncated answer and resolved citation; a one-off ask with no Conversation persisting nothing, matching the clean path). `apps/ai` green (1052 passed) and the full backend suite green (1596 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed, 71s, 2026-09-23). **IR-377** makes a stream that produces no answer text the `unavailable` state rather than a blank Turn -- the empty-answer bug reported from live use, where a reasoning model spent its whole response thinking and IRIS saved the empty result as a reply. `_produced_nothing` in `apps/ai/answers/service.py` is one predicate both paths consult, so `generate()` raising on an empty message and `answer_stream` saving one can no longer disagree; reasoning never counts as text, including `<think>`-leaked reasoning, which `ThinkTagFilter` has already removed from the buffer it reads. Sources still travel with the unavailable answer per ADR-008, and a cutoff before any text now persists nothing rather than a partial Turn with a blank answer. Evidence: `apps/ai/tests/test_ask_stream_http.py::EmptyAnswerTests` (reasoning-only, empty on both channels, and entirely-leaked-reasoning streams each yielding `unavailable` with its sources; `/ask/` and `/ask/stream/` agreeing, neither storing a blank Turn; a cutoff before any text storing nothing rather than a blank `partial` Turn; text after reasoning still storing a complete answer). `apps/ai` green; the full backend suite green apart from two pre-existing `test_llm_contracts.py` failures that read a developer `.env` (`LLM_TEMPERATURE`, `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT`) and are unrelated to this change (2026-09-28). **IR-381** reverses IR-327's "never stored": the reasoning text is persisted on `Turn.reasoning` (migration 0016) with the same lifetime as the Turn, sent on `done.reasoning` and on the transcript, and shown in a panel that is **collapsed by default for every reader**, live and replayed alike. The separation IR-327 guaranteed is untouched: reasoning is a different column from `answer`, is never joined into it, and is never scanned for citation markers -- including a `<think>` leak, which is routed to the panel. `had_reasoning` is kept as-is, redundant but still the only thing that can be said about a Turn written before the text was stored. Spec amendment recorded in [ADR-019](../adr/019-persisted-unified-conversation-history.md) §Amendment -- 2026-09-29 (IR-381). `apps/ai` stream/answer suites green (127 passed) and the ai frontend suite green (69 passed); full frontend suite green (439 passed) and the full backend suite 1826 passed / 3 failed, the three being the developer-`.env` artifacts already recorded above plus `test_startup.py::test_manage_py_check_passes_with_no_vendor_keys_at_all`, which inherits `LLM_RESOLVE_MODEL` from `.env` into its subprocess -- all 51 tests in those two files pass with `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT` and `LLM_RESOLVE_MODEL` cleared (2026-09-29) |

| **IR-334** | **Citation regions reach the wire, the manuscript is servable again, generation actually streams, and the AI Overview is generated once** — extends FR-M3-01/FR-M3-02 (stable labels only, per the frozen-SRS rule) | [ADR-031](../adr/031-pdf-citation-overlay.md) | `apps/ai/regions.py` (new) converts a chunk's stored `bboxes` (PDF points) into fractions of the page against `ChunkSet.page_sizes`, dropping a degenerate rectangle, one on a page of unrecorded size, or a malformed one — each would otherwise draw a box in the wrong place, which is worse than none; the page still travels separately as `page`. `RetrievedChunk` and `Citation` both gain `regions`; `two_stage.py` and `degraded.py` populate them, `reranking.py` now rebuilds a reordered chunk with `dataclasses.replace` instead of naming every field, so a field added to `RetrievedChunk` cannot silently stop surviving a reorder the way `regions` would have. `apps/ai/presentation.py` puts `regions` on every passage and citation; `apps/ai/conversations.py::_resolve_citation` re-resolves them from the live chunk on every replay, the same re-resolution ADR-019 already does for the quote. **The manuscript link has been dead since IR-152** — `RecordSerializer` echoed `abstract_file` as a `/media/...` path, and nothing has served that path in any environment since IR-152 removed both the nginx block and Django's `DEBUG` `static()` route. `RecordViewSet.manuscript` (`GET /records/<id>/manuscript/`) serves it inline through `visible_to(user)`, reusing `download_service.resolve_record_download_file`; `abstract_file` on the detail serializer now reports that endpoint (or `null`, via the new `has_record_download_file` cheap-existence check) instead of the stored path, and `files[].url` points at the existing authorized download view instead of `f.file.url` for the same reason. **Streaming now actually streams**: `RetryingLLMProvider`, `CircuitBreakingLLMProvider` and `FallbackLLMProvider` gain `stream()` — IR-321 wrapped every configured provider in decorators implementing only `generate()`, so against the real model `LLMProvider.stream()`'s buffering default ran and `/ai/ask/stream/` delivered the whole answer as one `text_delta`, exactly the limitation `ChatStreamView`'s docstring recorded. The rule the three new implementations share: retry and failover happen only **before** the first delta (`_open_stream`/`_yield_from_opened` in `resilience/llm.py`) — once a token has reached the reader, restarting the call would repeat or duplicate text already on their screen, so a failure after first output propagates instead. `GroundedAnswerService` also now degrades a refused (`CircuitOpen`) call the same way it degrades a failed one, on both the synchronous and streaming paths. **The AI Overview is generated once and cached**: `PaperAiOverview` used to fire `/ai/ask/` from a `useEffect` on every mount, an unscoped corpus-wide vendor call for an answer that cannot change. New `RecordOverview` model (migration `0014`, replacing the field-less `DocumentSummary` placeholder from migration 0001 that no code path ever wrote to — the same move IR-89 F made for `DocumentChunk`) keyed one-to-one on the record, storing the text, citations, degradation flag, model id and the active `ChunkSet.content_hash` it was generated against. `apps/ai/overview.py::overview_for` returns the cached row when `content_hash` and `prompt_version` still match, regenerates through `composition_root().answer_service(record=record)` — scoped retrieval, not corpus-wide — on a miss or a stale hash, and never caches an `unavailable` state, so the next view retries once the vendor is back. `GET /api/v1/ai/records/<id>/overview/` (`RecordOverviewView`) is the endpoint, resolved through `visible_to(user)` like every other record read | `apps/ai/tests/test_regions.py` (13, pure — normalization, clamping-not-dropping for coordinates a few points past the trim edge, and the five reasons a rectangle is withheld: degenerate, zero-area unflagged, inverted, unknown page size, zero page width), `apps/ai/tests/test_citation_regions_http.py` (4, DB — a citation and a search result each carrying the normalized region of its chunk, an empty list rather than an error when nothing was recovered, and a replayed Conversation Turn re-resolving regions exactly as it re-resolves quotes), `apps/records/test_manuscript.py` (8, DB, API seam — a permitted reader gets the PDF inline with `Accept-Ranges`, a reader without access gets 404 not 403, anonymous gets 401, a record with no file gets 404, an audit event is recorded, and `abstract_file` on the detail payload is the endpoint URL or `null`, never a `/media/` path), `apps/ai/resilience/tests/test_llm_streaming.py` (17, pure — every one of `RetryingLLMProvider`/`CircuitBreakingLLMProvider`/`FallbackLLMProvider`/the composed stack passing N scripted deltas through as N deltas rather than one; a transient failure *before* any delta retried, the identical failure *after* one not retried and not failed-over; a circuit that opened before any delta staying open, one that opened after a delta not counting against the breaker; a non-switchable kind — `auth` — never tried against the next configured provider), `apps/ai/tests/test_overview_http.py` (8, DB — two reads of the same record producing exactly one vendor call, asserted on `ScriptedLLM.calls`; a re-chunk invalidating the cached row and forcing exactly one more call; an unavailable model never persisted and retried on the next view; a record with no chunk set reporting `not_indexed`; a reader without access getting 404) | `apps/ai apps/records` green, full backend suite green, no new failures against the pre-existing baseline (2026-09-23) | **IMPLEMENTED** — the PDF.js reader that draws these regions, and the frontend overview cache consumer, landed as IR-335 (below). No production run against a real Voyage/LLM account: every test here runs on the deterministic fakes, the same gap every other row in this table carries until IR-250 lands |
| **IR-378** | **Inference tasks and Profiles — a caller names a task instead of asking for "the LLM"** — no FR- id; part C of IR-375's spec, and the expand half of expand–contract | [ADR-036 §Amendment](../adr/036-openai-compatible-inference-provider.md) (one adapter per protocol, **vendor chosen per Inference task**, not per environment) · [ADR-008 §Amendment](../adr/008-ai-degradation-to-fts.md) (a fallback list of models inside **one** vendor account; cross-vendor failover still refused) | `apps/ai/inference/tasks.py` — the closed four-name set (`answer`, `resolve`, `summary`, `describe_figure`); an unrecognised name raises `UnknownInferenceTask` rather than resolving to a default · `apps/ai/inference/profiles.py` — one `Profile` per task carrying vendor, model, ordered same-vendor fallback models, reasoning visibility and the uniform `NO_TRAINING` data policy, resolved from per-task `LLM_<TASK>_*` settings on demand, never at import · `apps/ai/inference/providers.py` — builds the provider a Profile describes through the existing resilience stack, and keeps appending the legacy cross-vendor `LLM_FALLBACK_*` entry for `answer` only, so an opted-in deployment is unchanged until the IR-375 contract ticket removes it · `apps/ai/composition.py` — `llm_for(task)` beside the existing `llm()`, Profiles resolved lazily and one provider cached per task; the other three tasks are off until given a model. `resolve` and `summary` are **not wired to a caller yet** and `describe_figure` is declared and unused. **Recorded contradiction, not reconciled:** `LLM_RESOLUTION_MODEL` (IR-296) is what `CompositionRoot.resolver()` actually reads, while the new `LLM_RESOLVE_*` keys configure a `resolve` Profile no caller reaches — two live namespaces for one Inference task, noted in both settings blocks and `.env.example` until the resolve-wiring ticket collapses them | `apps/ai/inference/tests/test_profiles.py` (17 — the closed set, the typo refusal, per-task vendors, the flat-settings inheritance, a named vendor stopping the flat **key** being inherited so a request cannot reach one vendor authenticated for another, an inherited base URL deciding the vendor it points at (IR-382 selects a dialect on that field), a self-hosted URL not refused where a *named* vendor outside the pair is, per-task reasoning visibility, a same-vendor fallback list, an unconfigured task raising rather than borrowing a model) · `apps/ai/inference/tests/test_reserved_task.py` (1, file scan — nothing under `apps/` reaches for `describe_figure`) · `apps/ai/tests/test_inference_tasks_http.py` (5, HTTP boundary — the `answer` task reaches the model *and key and base URL* its Profile names, with `openai.OpenAI` replaced by a recorder; Refuses 404 / 403 (no open seat) / 400 (legacy pipeline, blank reason, unopened seat, the party already asked). `withdraw_revision_request` lets any seat holder of the asking party withdraw (no reason). `decision_blocked_reason` refuses accept & route while any request is open (IR-270/271 reuse it); `office_blocked_reason` refuses Clear and Record finding to the asking office only. Owners are notified of both acts. Endpoints `POST /records/<id>/request-revision/` and `POST /records/<id>/revision-requests/<request_id>/withdraw/`; record detail's `revision` flag (with every open request, reason gated by `may_read_review`); tracker rows gain a derived `changes_requested`, resubmissions gain `version`. Frontend: *Request Revision…* (`request_revision`, EvaluationPage copy verbatim) and *Withdraw revision request* (`withdraw_revision`) in F6's action bar, *Accept & route* disabled with the server's reason, the owner's *Revision requested* banner on Overview, and *Changes requested* on the strip. Settled by the project lead on 2026-10-30 in [ADR-032 §10's IR-419 Amendment](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md), which also amends §7's table, §12 and Security Impact to match. **Writes** are `is_record_participant`'s (§7): a pool member who reads is refused 403. A draft is refused (400). `GET`/`POST /records/<id>/review-comments/`, `PATCH /records/<id>/review-comments/<cid>/` (author only). The tracker carries `review_comments`, `null` to a non-reader as `reviews` is; record detail carries none. Capability `comment_review`: a participant, on a submitted record. **Implementation choices for review:** threads are one level deep (a reply to a reply joins its thread); an edit that changes nothing does not stamp `edited_at`; each comment carries a server-computed `can_edit`; a body is at most 10,000 characters; there is no delete and no notification. | **IR-273** | **The owner answers the open revision requests with a new version; only the requesting parties' clearances and seats reset** — ADR-003's clearance-aware resubmission on the adviser-first model, under both ADR-004 policies; touches FR-M5-03 (stable label only) | [ADR-032 §5](../adr/032-adviser-first-review-and-office-reviewer-pools.md), with its 2026-10-08 IR-416 hand-off and IR-273 Amendment (open points pending the project lead's review) · [ADR-003](../adr/003-clearance-aware-resubmission.md) · ADR-004 · Jira IR-273 | `apps/reviews/new_version.py`: `submit_new_version` lets an owner of a new-model record with an open request, changed since the newest request (a manuscript other than the latest version's, an owner's `RecordUpload`, or `Record.details_edited_at`, `records/0016`, stamped only by an owner's real change), write v(n+1) through `write_version`, resolve every open request as `resubmitted`, reset the requesting parties' clearances (`CLEARANCE_AWARE`) or every clearance (`RESTART_ALL`), return the requesting parties' `done` seats to `in_review`, and record `resubmission_count`/`last_resubmitted_at` so `preserved` derives as before. Refuses 404 / 403 (not an owner) / 400 (legacy pipeline, no open request, nothing changed — naming what would count). `POST /records/<id>/new-version/`; record detail's `revision.new_version` (number, who re-reviews, which clearances are kept, why not yet) and `manuscript_unsubmitted`. The manuscript lock opens while a revision is asked for, to owners only; `/manuscript/` serves a non-owner the latest version's file until the new version is submitted, and withdrawing the last open request puts the submitted manuscript back. An owner's revised manuscript is extracted only when its version is submitted, so the chunks Ask IRIS answers from are always the submitted manuscript's (settled 2026-10-08). Requesting parties' reviewers are notified. Frontend: *Submit new version* in the owner's *Revision requested* banner (`create_version`, from the server's flag) with a confirmation naming who reviews it ("IERC will review v3. | **IR-393** | **Chunks carry a stored keyword index the database maintains** — no FR- id; part C of IR-390's spec, and the asset the hybrid retrieval in IR-395 is built on | [ADR-033 §2](../adr/033-hybrid-retrieval-and-passage-selection.md) (the chunk keyword index is a stored column the database maintains, **not** application code on save) | `DocumentChunk.search_vector` (`SearchVectorField`, `editable=False`) with a `GinIndex`, added by `ai/0015_chunk_keyword_index` · `apps/ai/keyword_index.py` holds the trigger SQL (`BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF content`, `to_tsvector('english', …)`) and one `backfill_chunk_keyword_index()` used by both the migration's `RunPython` and `manage.py backfill_chunk_keyword_index`; the backfill is idempotent and resumable by construction — what it fills is "chunks with no keyword index", recomputed per batch, never a checkpoint file — exactly as `backfill_embeddings` is · `apps/ai/retrieval/degraded.py::FullTextRetriever` reads the column instead of building a `SearchVector("content")` per chunk per query. **A trigger rather than a generated column**, both of which ADR-033 §2 permits: a stored generated column is added by an `ALTER TABLE` that rewrites the whole table under an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock, while a nullable column plus a trigger does not, which is what leaves the fill as a separate resumable step. The configuration is `english`, named explicitly rather than inherited from the server's `default_text_search_config`, so the trigger, the `SearchQuery` and `Record`'s index agree by construction. **A real finding, recorded rather than smoothed over:** the obvious rewrite — `@@` against the column, so the GIN index answers the fallback's own query — is **not** behaviour-preserving. `ts_rank` does not consult `@@`, so `rank > 0` matches a passage holding *some* of a multi-word question's terms while `@@` on Django's default `plainto_tsquery` demands all of them; on the development corpus "pond sampling" returned 20 passages under `rank > 0` and **none** under `@@`. The fallback therefore keeps `rank > 0`, and the index is left for the hybrid retrieval that follows, where the stricter predicate is the intended one | `apps/ai/tests/test_chunk_keyword_index.py` (11, DB — an inserted chunk, an edited chunk and a `bulk_create`d chunk set each indexed with no application code doing it, `bulk_create` being the path `repositories.save` actually uses and the one a signal would be skipped on; the `english` configuration asserted through stemming rather than by reading the setting back; the backfill filling a row that predates the trigger, writing nothing on a second run, and resuming after being stopped one row in; the command's dry run and its empty-backlog report) · `apps/ai/retrieval/tests/test_degraded.py::FullTextFallbackTests::test_a_passage_holding_some_of_the_question_still_matches` (new — pins the `rank > 0` predicate against the `@@` rewrite) · the eleven pre-existing `test_degraded.py` cases and `test_degradation_crosses_the_seam.py` unchanged | `apps/ai/tests/test_chunk_keyword_index.py apps/ai/retrieval/` → 62 passed. Full backend suite **1776 passed, 2 failed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed** (2026-09-28); both failures are the pre-existing environmental ones recorded on IR-378 (`test_llm_contracts.py` reading the developer's own `backend/.env`). **Migration exercised against a copy of the development database, not only an empty one** (`CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE iris_db`, 4,926 chunks): `0015` applied cleanly, all 4,926 rows backfilled with 0 left null, the GIN index and the trigger both present, `EXPLAIN` confirming a Bitmap Index Scan on `ai_chunk_search_gin_idx` for an `@@` query, and an `UPDATE` to a row's `content` re-indexing it with no code involved. The fallback's results were compared old-query against new-query on that corpus for five questions ("quantum filtering", "contamination bias", "pond sampling", "neural network training", "semiparametric estimator") — **top-20 chunk ids identical on all five**. The backfill was then run over 500 real rows, stopped at 200, resumed to completion, and re-run to a no-op. The copy was dropped afterwards | **IMPLEMENTED** — the column, its index, its trigger and the backfill exist and the fallback reads them, with reader-visible behaviour unchanged by measurement rather than by assertion. Fusing this keyword search with the vector path is IR-395; nothing consumes the GIN index on the normal path yet |
| **IR-395** | **An exact term finds its papers — keyword and vector search fused behind a switch** — no FR- id; part E of IR-390's spec, and the first of ADR-033 §5's six switches to be built | [ADR-033 §1](../adr/033-hybrid-retrieval-and-passage-selection.md) (two independent searches over the whole visible catalogue, merged **by rank position** before reranking, keyword search **not** restricted to the vector candidates) · §5 (lands behind a setting defaulting to today's behaviour) · §Security Impact (a second candidate source, **no** second visibility predicate) | `apps/ai/retrieval/fusion.py` — `fuse_by_rank(lists, key)` is the merge as its own pure unit: rank 1 of every list, then rank 2, keeping each item's best position, deduped by the caller's key. **No weight, threshold or `k`**, which is the whole reason for this shape over adding normalised scores — ADR-033 rejected normalisation because its constant is fitted to the queries it was chosen on, and a constant is a parameter that has to be tuned on questions. IR-399's sub-question merge and Listing questions reuse the same unit. `KeywordFusionRetriever` is a decorator returning `inner_result.with_passages(...)`, so `degraded`, `mode` and `embedding_space_id` carry through (the IR-279 contract) · `CompositionRoot._candidates` puts it **inside** `RerankingRetriever`, so the merged set passes the disclosure gate and the reranker exactly as the vector set does — reranking and everything after it are unchanged · the keyword half is the **same** `FullTextRetriever` the ADR-008 outage path uses, reading IR-393's stored `search_vector` column, so visibility is one `visible_to(user)` filter rather than a second copy · `AI_KEYWORD_RETRIEVAL_ENABLED` and `AI_RETRIEVAL_FUSION_ENABLED` in `config/settings/base.py` and `.env.example`, **both default False**. **Two settings, one technique**, as `apps/ai/evaluation/techniques.py` already said: neither half does anything alone — searching the keyword index and discarding the result is a query paid for nothing — so the decorator joins the stack only when both are on, and the harness warns (it does not refuse) that two switches moved. **Design decisions recorded rather than left to be read out of the code:** the keyword result's `degraded=True` is **not** adopted, because on the healthy path it would tell a reader the vendor was out while it was answering normally; an already-degraded inner result is passed straight through unfused, since it *is* full-text search and fusing it with full-text search buys a query and no candidates; and a fused passage carries its **merged position** as its score, because a `ts_rank` (~0.06) and a cosine similarity (~0.8) in one list would make the field mean two things at once and `presentation.record_sources` reads it for a record card's headline score | `apps/ai/retrieval/tests/test_fusion.py` (24 — `RankMergeTests` (6) on fixed lists with **no database**: the interleave, the first list breaking a tie, an item in both lists emitted once at its best rank, a longer list continuing, empties, and identity coming from the caller's key · `ExactTermTests` (3, DB) — the acceptance criterion, asserted in both directions: the paper holding only the exact term is **absent** without fusion and **present** with it, and what the vector path already found is kept. How the miss is reproduced is stated in the class docstring rather than hidden: `DeterministicEmbeddingProvider` hashes words, so it cannot blur a token the way Voyage does, and the miss is reproduced through the other bound on the vector path — stage 1's candidate record set · `KeywordPathVisibilityTests` (4, DB) — a stranger's draft never returned by the keyword path, a readable record not carrying its forbidden neighbour, an anonymous user getting nothing, and owner/adviser/staff **not narrowed** either, since a source returning less than `visible_to` allows is a second predicate just as much as one returning more · `DecoratorContractTests` (6) — mode and space surviving, the keyword path's degraded flag not adopted, a degraded inner result passed through **with no second query**, a vendor failure still propagating so degradation stays `DegradableRetriever`'s decision, the caller's limit bounding the fused list, and the fused score monotone with the merged order · `SwitchTests` (5, DB) — asserted on the **composed stack**, not on the settings module, since a setting read and then ignored would pass a settings assertion: absent by default, present with both on, absent with either half alone, and both switches reporting `implemented` to the harness registry) · `test_degradation_crosses_the_seam.py` — `KeywordFusionRetriever` added to `DECORATORS`, so the enumeration guard covers it · **four tests retargeted, not deleted:** `test_eval_harness.py::test_the_command_refuses_a_technique_that_is_not_built_yet` and three in `test_techniques.py` asserted "fusion is not built yet", which this ticket is what falsifies. Two moved onto whichever techniques are still unbuilt (so they survive IR-396 and IR-397 rather than breaking again), one onto the value rather than the setting's absence, one onto `relevance_cut_off` | `pytest apps/ai/retrieval apps/ai/evaluation apps/ai/tests/test_eval_harness.py apps/ai/tests/test_one_retrieval_stack.py apps/ai/tests/test_ask_http.py` → **all green**. Full backend suite **2022 passed, 4 failed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed** (2026-10-01); the four failures are the pre-existing `test_llm_contracts.py`/`test_profiles.py` environmental ones IR-378 and IR-393 already recorded — they read the developer's own `backend/.env` (`LLM_TEMPERATURE`, `LLM_REASONING_EFFORT`) — and all four were reproduced on the base commit before any change, so none is this ticket's. `flake8 apps config` clean. **Two paid harness runs, all 52 questions of `proxy_starter.json` over all 40 papers, student login, Voyage, disclosure bypass on, same commit `b5e8e1c`, same question-set SHA-256 `3b54a40c…`, same embedding space 2, `degraded_questions` 0 in all four configurations.** Baseline (both switches off) reproduced main's recorded numbers exactly — recall@10 **0.596** without reranking / **0.683** with, final-set 0.558 / 0.663 — which is the check that the baseline was not an old number. Candidate (both on): recall@10 **0.587** / **0.875**, final-set 0.548 / **0.837**. **With reranking, the configuration a deployment runs: +0.192 recall@10 and +0.174 final-set, about ten of 52 questions.** Eleven questions gained, one lost, and the gains land where ADR-033 predicted — all four exact-term questions that were missing (q19, q21, q37, q39), the near-duplicate q23, and cross-paper q34 and q52. The single loss is q29, **final-set only**: its passage is still retrieved inside the top ten, and the widened set reordered it out of the eight the model receives, which is the source cap rather than retrieval. **Without reranking it is a wash** (−0.009, under the 0.019 one question is worth; identical micro figures, 4 gained and 4 lost), and that is the mechanism working as ADR-033 describes it: interleaving hands half the top ten to keyword candidates that nothing has sorted for relevance, so the widening only pays once a reranker sorts it. Run files `docs/evaluation/runs/20260930-160932-proxy-starter.json` (baseline) and `20260930-161210-proxy-starter.json` (candidate). **Proxy tier — an engineering gate on the 40-paper arXiv proxy corpus, never a finding about CIT-U research (ADR-023).** Nothing was tuned on the question set, because the merge has no parameter to tune. **Two findings from review, raised as tickets rather than settled here:** the merge trims back to `limit`, so a keyword hit *displaces* a vector candidate rather than joining it — substitution, where ADR-033 §Decision Rationale says hybrid retrieval "widens what reaches the reranker". That is **IR-438**: widening is a second change and would invalidate the run that earned this one, and the measured result is consistent with vector's ranks 51–100 having contributed very little. The `eval_retrieval` write-ordering and stdout-encoding fix carried here is **IR-439**, filed because it is its own defect — a paid run measured both configurations, printed them, then died encoding the comparison table's delta on a `cp1252` console and lost its results file. It rides this branch because the branch could not be measured without it | **IMPLEMENTED AND MEASURED, SHIPPED OFF.** Both defaults stay `False` and this ticket does not move them however good its numbers are — ADR-033 §5 gives that to **IR-402**, which is where this run is handed on. Reader-visible behaviour is unchanged on an upgrade. Still open: the disclosure gate refuses every record until IR-250, so final-set recall needs IR-317's bypass; q29's final-set loss belongs to the source cap and is IR-397's to look at; widening rather than substituting is IR-438; and the remaining four switches are IR-396 and IR-397 |
| **IR-406** | **The Publish dialog can offer the manuscript's own title and abstract** — no FR- id; B1 of the IR-374 frontend redesign spec (§4.5, Appendix D), the backend half of F3b's prefill and assistance toward NFR-U2 | [IR-374 spec](../frontend_redesign_spec.md) §4.5 · ADR-016 (the structure it reads) | `apps/records/metadata_suggestions.py` (new) reads the record's `PdfExtraction(kind=MANUSCRIPT)` and restates its status as a local `SuggestionState`: `pending` (queued/running) · `ready` (done) · `failed` (failed, or any status added later) · `unsupported` (no manuscript extraction to read). From a `ready` structure it suggests the Docling title, **unless it is only the file name** (Docling's fallback, compared against the stored name and its stem with case and `_`/`-`/space folded, since storage rewrites spaces), and the text under an *Abstract* heading (case and trailing `:`/`.` ignored) up to the next heading, **leaving out running page headers and footers**; `null` when absent. `GET /records/<id>/metadata-suggestions/` on `RecordViewSet` is **owner-only through `get_queryset()`**, so office staff, the assigned adviser and a reader of a published record get a 404 identical in status and body to a missing record (IR-153). No LLM, no vendor call, no model change, no migration; it imports `apps.ai`'s extraction serializer and document kinds read-only and adds nothing there. `apps/tests/test_enum_vocabulary.py` gains one `ALLOWED` entry, with its reason: `SuggestionState`'s `"pending"` is ingestion vocabulary that shares a spelling with `RequestStatus.PENDING`. **Not done, by choice:** the optional B1b (`manuscript_extraction` on Record detail). **Known gaps, for the spec's owner:** a retrying extraction reads `failed` between attempts, so the dialog stops polling before a later success; and an image-only scan is stored `failed` (`EmptyExtraction`), not the `ready`-with-nulls §4.5 describes | `apps/records/test_metadata_suggestions.py` (16 tests, REST seam): each state; title and abstract read from a structure built with the extraction package's own serializer; file-name titles suppressed including a storage collision suffix; page furniture excluded; a read structure with neither gives nulls; owner 200, stranger 404, office staff / adviser / public reader 404, **a refusal's body equal to a missing record's**, anonymous 401; the extractor is never called | `pytest apps/records/test_metadata_suggestions.py` → **16 passed**. | **IR-409** | **The Publish dialog offers the manuscript's own title and abstract as chips** — no FR- id; F3b of the IR-374 frontend redesign spec (§4.5 client behaviour), the client half of IR-406's prefill and assistance toward NFR-U2 | [IR-374 spec](../frontend_redesign_spec.md) §4.5 · ADR-008 (no invented confidence) | `features/publish/prefill.ts` (new) `usePrefill` polls `GET /records/<id>/metadata-suggestions/` while Details is shown: at once, every 2 s while `pending`, every 5 s after 20 s, never past 90 s (`nextPollDelay`); it stops on `ready`/`failed`/`unsupported`, on leaving step 2, on closing the dialog, and on a new upload. **It starts again each time Details is entered**, which closes IR-406's first known gap from the client side: a retrying extraction that reads `failed` and later succeeds still offers chips when the author comes back to Details. A request that fails reads as `failed` (the neutral line), never as an error. Nothing is ever filled without that click. The provisional file-name title counts as the dialog's, not the author's; a resumed draft's saved title counts as the author's, since the file name is no longer known. `pending` shows "Reading your PDF…" under both fields (a still icon, not a spinner, for reduced motion) and disables neither, announced once through one `role=status` line; `failed`/`unsupported` show one neutral line; `expired` (90 s) shows nothing.
