The 3 skips are the pyexcel-guarded legacy-import case (x2, once per policy arm) and the full-checkout settings sweep, each declining with its stated reason rather than passing vacuously. The matrix module alone is **77 tests / 172 subtests**, split between the `CLEARANCE_AWARE` and `RESTART_ALL` arms | **VERIFIED** (2026-09-10, IR-140; re-evidenced by IR-274 on the adviser-first suites, since the IR-140 matrix it first rested on was the retired pipeline's oracle). **IR-233 is fixed** (IR-260, `6cf1120`, PR #218, 2026-10-10): its strict xfail is removed and all three cases in `apps/reviews/test_resubmission_regression.py` pass as ordinary tests, so a metadata-only revision now preserves peer clearances (see [Open defects](#open-defects)); updated under IR-97 on 2026-10-28, documentation only, nothing re-run — the workflow itself is now demonstrated by test rather than asserted: every review edge the table declares is walked under **both** resubmission policies, invalid transitions are refused rather than silently ignored, and the preserved-clearance contribution is asserted on both arms of the comparison. **One carve-out, named rather than smoothed over: `route[]` is still absent** from the API payload — an affordance for the reviewer screen, not a workflow behaviour, so it does not gate this row, but note it sits against **IR-139, which is marked Done and whose summary names `route[]` as a deliverable**. That contradiction is recorded on IR-139 for a person to rule on (descoped, or missed and owed to IR-143); it was not reconciled here. The routing model behind it exists: **`IR-136` landed 2026-09-09** in three stages. `records/lifecycle.py` holds `STAGES` (a node registry declaring each stage's kind, its participating offices, and what a `Review` records as) and `TRANSITIONS` (the edge set), with `apply()` the single entry point, atomic from its first commit. **Every `pipeline_status` write in the codebase is now a declared edge** — the eleven in `reviews/services.py` (stage 1) and the seven in `records/views.py` / `records/services.py` (stage 2), with stage 3 removing the last hand-written residue. Crucially for **ADR-003**, `resubmit_record`'s preserve-vs-restart decision no longer turns on a `CLEARANCE_OFFICES` set literal inside the function — **that literal was where the primary research contribution lived**; it reads the table now, and `clearance_offices()` derives from `STAGES` so an added office cannot leave a stale copy behind. Evidence: `apps/reviews/test_workflow_characterisation.py` (IR-197, written *before* the refactor and unchanged through it) passes at 30 tests across all three stages, which is what makes "behaves identically" a checked claim; plus `apps/records/test_lifecycle.py` — now 25 tests on the table's own structure, including that `settings.WORKFLOW_TABLE` really overrides (the ADR-005 seam). Two divergences from ADR-002's literal key are recorded in the module docstring rather than hidden: the key is `(from_status, event)` with the gate's role a field, and four edges name a resolver because their destination depends on record type, requested offices, or whether every office has cleared. Its own prerequisite **`IR-135` landed 2026-09-09**: the workflow vocabulary is now one `TextChoices` per concept in `core/enums.py`, 289 bare literals across 16 modules are gone, and `apps/tests/test_enum_vocabulary.py` fails the build if one comes back. `makemigrations` reports **no changes**, which is the evidence that nothing stored moved, and the reviewer UI that consumes this is `IR-143`. **`IR-137` landed 2026-09-09**, which is what makes ADR-003's claim *testable* rather than asserted: `RESUBMISSION_POLICY` is a third `WORKFLOW_TABLE` key selecting `clearance_aware` (production default) or `restart_all` (the evaluation arm, which resets every clearance to `pending` and re-enters at the stage the record's offices started from). The switch sits on the clearance-decline branch only — `apps/reviews/test_resubmission_policy.py` asserts a *sequential* decline behaves identically under both policies, which is what keeps the two arms differing in one thing. An unrecognised value raises rather than defaulting, so an evaluation instance cannot silently run the production arm; the policy has no endpoint and no serializer field, and `test_lifecycle.py::PolicyIsNotReachableThroughTheApiTests` fails the build if one appears (ADR-004's hard operational rule). Running the *whole* transition suite under both policies **is `IR-140`, and it landed 2026-09-10** — `apps/reviews/test_workflow_matrix.py`, which is what moved this row to VERIFIED. Evidence recorded 2026-09-09: `pytest apps/records/test_lifecycle.py apps/reviews/` in the backend container — **93 passed, 1 skipped**; the full backend suite **675 tests, 669 passed, 4 skipped, 2 failed**, both failures pre-existing on `main` and unrelated (`test_celery_routing`, the IR-199 broker difference; `test_chunker_core[fixed-window]`, a Hypothesis `FailedHealthCheck`). **Two recorded contradictions, per CLAUDE.md's "do not silently reconcile":** (1) IR-137's AC 1 says the policy is a *"per-type setting"*, while ADR-004 §Decision and §Risk say *"institution-level"* / *"set per instance"* and IR-137's own body says *"instance-level configuration"* — the ADR outranks Jira, so it is implemented per instance; per record type would let one type run each arm inside a single evaluation, which is a confound rather than a capability. (2) IR-137's *original* (superseded) DoD asks for the active policy *"recorded in the audit trail"*; `AuditEvent` still has no workflow event types, and workflow auditing is `IR-144` (blocked on `IR-138`), so this ships with a per-resubmission log line instead and the audit event is deferred there rather than guessed at here — note the log line only *is* a record because `IR-137` also added the first `LOGGING` setting this project has had, without which `apps.*` sat below the root logger's default `WARNING` and every `logger.info` was composed and discarded. (3) ADR-004 §Decision says `RESTART_ALL` routes *"to the first clearance stage for the record type"*; the implementation routes by the record's **office set** instead — `_clearance_entry_for()` returns `itso_review` only when ITSO actually holds a row. Under ADR-018 the offices are data on the record rather than a function of its type, so routing by type would be reading a fact that no longer exists there. The office-set rule is strictly narrower and cannot strand a pending office (the `rdco_review` arm is unreachable from a clearance decline, which by definition has a row), but it *is* a divergence from the ADR's wording and **may warrant an ADR-004 amendment — a decision for a person, not recorded here as settled** |
| FR-M5-03 | Explicit document requests, distinct from resubmission | ADR-022 | **IR-262, 2026-09-24.** `DocumentRequest` / `DocumentRequestItem` (`documents/0008`, additive; `UploadSlot.record` is new and set only on a per-record ad-hoc slot for an "Other" item), `DocumentRequestState` / `DocumentRequestItemState` in `core/enums.py`. `apps/documents/requests.py` creates a request (holder only: an active assignment the user can staff, `tracker.requestable_parties`, deliberately **not** narrowed by the legacy `can_act` gate) and fulfils it; `GET`/`POST /records/<id>/document-requests/` and `GET /records/<id>/document-requests/slots/` (the picklist) on `RecordViewSet`, through `get_object()` so no access is a 404; uploads reuse `/documents/submit/` with `request_item`, resolved and marked in one transaction with the item locked. `workflow_state` becomes `awaiting_document` from open rows (the IR-258 stub is replaced), tracker rows carry `awaiting_document` (◐) and `document_requests[]`, Record detail gains `can_request_document`. Notifications: owner on create, requesting party on fulfil (`notifications/0005` seeds two types). Frontend: `RequestDocumentDialog` (reviewer page and paper view) and the owner's `ActionRequiredPanel` on the paper view. **Recorded at IR-262's merge (historical — each point is settled below):** (1) the picklist endpoint and `can_request_document` were not in ADR-022 §5's API list; (2) *as shipped in IR-262*, listing followed the original ADR-022 §5 (`visible_to`), so any reader of a *published* record could list its requests and messages. That was shipped behaviour, **not a rule**, and it is superseded by the settled internal-workflow-data rule in (2) below; (3) staff pass `authorize_record_documents` and so may upload against an item too; (4) accept/reject/withdraw are IR-263, a decision closing requests is IR-270. **Settled 2026-09-24 after the post-merge review (ADR-022 §Amendment):** (1) The picklist route is now in ADR-022 §5. (2) **Corrected: document-request data is internal workflow data** (ADR-022 §Amendment 5, which amends §5 and §Security Impact). Reading a Record, published or visible to an office through `visible_to()`, does not grant its requests, messages, comments or request history. Only owners and authorised submitters, and the workflow parties relevant to the request, may read them. IR-262 as shipped did not meet this: the list endpoint and the tracker's `document_requests[]` both used `visible_to()`. **[IR-349](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-349), 2026-09-24, implements the fix:** one predicate, `apps/documents/requests.py` `may_read_requests(record, user)`. It admits the owner (`RecordOwner`) or the request's `requested_by`, and otherwise intersects `tracker.staffable_parties` with `tracker.participating_parties`: every party that held an assignment, active or closed, recorded a `Review` or signed a `RecordClearance`. It also counts every requesting `party`. The list `GET` answers 403 to a viewer who can see the Record but fails the predicate, and 404 when the viewer cannot see it, unchanged. For the same viewer the tracker serves `document_requests: null` and every party row's `awaiting_document: null`, while `workflow_state` and its label are unchanged. Record detail is untouched. The frontend types are nullable (`DocumentRequest[] | null`, `boolean | null`), and for `null` `ReviewRoutingTracker` renders no document-request section and no ◐. There is no separate "finding" model, so "acted on" means a `Review` or a signed clearance. **An RDCO user is a participant on every Thesis and Project**, because RDCO staffs Intake and Intake always holds the entry assignment. RDCO's "no access" case therefore arises only on a Proposal before it is routed to RDCO. A non-participant can still infer the requester when `awaiting_document` is shown and only one party holds the Record; the generic state is visible by design (§Amendment 5). Its two open points were settled on 2026-09-24 (ADR-022 §Amendment 5). **Access is by participation, not role:** owners and submitters, plus anyone who can staff a party that holds, held or acted on an assignment on the Record, or that created or took part in a request. An unassigned named Adviser, and RDCO before it has held or acted on the Record, have no access. **The generic `workflow_state = awaiting_document` stays visible** to anyone who may view the Record. The requesting party, messages, history and fulfilment, accept, reject and withdraw details do not, and that includes the tracker's per-party `awaiting_document` flag, which is `null` for non-participants.  (3) The requesting party may **not** fulfil its own request. **Built in IR-263** for `request_item` uploads: `requests.may_fulfil` admits only a Record owner, and anyone else gets a 403 with the item left `missing` and nobody notified. A normal owner upload to a requested canonical slot fulfils the request, built by IR-346; "Other" items still need `request_item`. **Refusals (ADR-022 §Amendment 4):** 404 means the caller cannot see the Record or object; 403 means the caller can see it but may not perform the action. As verified on 2026-09-24: an upload naming another Record's `request_item` gets **400** ("That requested document does not exist."), not 404. `authorize_record_documents` answers **403 even to a caller who cannot see the Record**. That is an intentional existing exception from IR-153, confined to the documents app, recorded and not converted; changing it would need its own decision and ticket. **[IR-263](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-263), 2026-09-24: accept, reject, withdraw (ADR-022 §3.4, §4).** `PATCH /document-request-items/<id>/` (`{"action": "accept"}` or `{"action": "reject", "reason"}`) and `PATCH /document-requests/<id>/` (`{"action": "withdraw"}`), in `documents/urls_requests.py`. A rejected item is stored as `missing` with `rejection_reason` and `decided_at` (`documents/0009`, additive), the request reopens, and the owner is notified (`notifications/0006` seeds "Document Rejected"). Accepting marks the item `accepted`. Only an open request can be withdrawn, and it records no reason; it stays in the tracker and leaves the owner's panel. **Settled by Lee Jasmin Adolfo, 2026-09-24:** (1) refusals are 404 only for a missing id or a Record the caller cannot see; 403 when the Record is visible but its request data is not (IR-349's `may_read_requests`), and 403 for a reader who cannot staff the requesting party; (2) any authorised staff member of the requesting party may act, never only `requested_by`; (3) rejection returns the item to `missing` and reopens the request, with no `rejected` state persisted; (4) no withdrawal-reason field; IR-270 defines any reason it needs. The new fields, `rejection_reason`, `decided_at` and `can_manage`, are served only behind `may_read_requests`. Request input and output moved onto DRF serializers (`documents/request_serializers.py`), shared by the list, the decision routes and the tracker, with the wire shape unchanged apart from the added fields. Frontend: `ReviewerDocumentRequests` replaces the Request documents block duplicated on the reviewer page and the paper view, and adds Accept, Reject (reason required) and Withdraw. `ActionRequiredPanel` shows a rejection's reason, drops withdrawn requests and shows an alert when the list cannot load. The tracker shows each item's state and any reject or withdrawal reason. Follow-ups: IR-347 (enum governance and the upload message; built, below), IR-348 (`seed_demo` slot lookup), IR-144 (document-request audit events, including IR-263's three), IR-346 (slot uploads fulfil, owner-only), IR-270 (a decision closes open requests). **[IR-346](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-346), 2026-10-03: an owner's ordinary slot upload fulfils (ADR-022 §Amendment 1).** `/documents/submit/` with `slot` and `/documents/uploads/create/` both store through `views._store_slot_upload`, which for a Record owner (`requests.may_fulfil`) calls `requests.fulfil_by_slot_upload` in the same transaction: every `missing` item asking for that picklist slot on an `open` request on that Record is marked uploaded through the existing `fulfil_item`, so one upload answers several requests and each fulfilled request notifies its party once, on commit. Ad-hoc slots are never matched, so an "Other" item still needs `request_item`; a staff or reviewer slot upload is stored and answers nothing; items then requests are locked in `resolve_item_for_upload`'s order and re-checked under the lock. No migration. Frontend: the Documents page offered owners an upload only on a `draft`/`declined` Record, which made the path unreachable during review; it now also offers one on a slot an open request is waiting on, naming who asked (ADR-022 §Amendment 1, as built). `FileUploadZone` gains an optional accessible name, and its file input is hidden with the `hidden` attribute instead of Tailwind's class, which axe flagged under jsdom as an unlabelled control nested in a button. **[IR-347](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-347), 2026-10-10: the document-request vocabulary is governed.** `DocumentRequestState` and `DocumentRequestItemState` join `GOVERNED_ENUMS` in `apps/tests/test_enum_vocabulary.py`, so a bare `"open"`, `"fulfilled"`, `"missing"` or `"uploaded"` in app source fails the build. Six files where the word means something else are added to `ALLOWED` one by one, each with its reason (a circuit breaker's and an ingestion job's state, four report or response keys); nothing under `apps/ai` is edited. `/documents/submit/` with neither `slot` nor `request_item` now answers "record, file, and one of slot or request_item are required." `/documents/uploads/create/` still takes only `slot`, so its message is unchanged. **[IR-270](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-270), 2026-10-09: a decision closes open requests (ADR-022 §3).** `apps/reviews/decisions.py` `decide` moves every open `DocumentRequest` on the Record to `withdrawn` inside the decision's transaction, the decider's own party's included, and records the deciding `Review` in `closed_by_decision` (`documents/0011`), which is the reason §3 asks for; a party's own withdrawal leaves it null and still records no reason (§4). Each closure writes `audit.document_request_withdrawn(..., closed_by=BY_DECISION)`. The request stays in the tracker's `document_requests[]` as `withdrawn`. `create_request` takes the record lock and refuses a party whose turn the decision closed meanwhile. IR-271 reaches the same path for a Proposal decided by its Adviser. **[IR-517](https://citiris.atlassian.net/browse/IR-517), 2026-10-11:** an owner's withdrawal or delete also withdraws open requests (`reviews.withdrawal.withdraw_review`). **Not built, and not required by §3:** the tracker serves no reason for a request, so one closed by a decision reads the same as one its party withdrew, while | FR-M6-01 | Authentication | **ADR owed** — the basis here is the requirement id alone; SDD M6 is frozen and is not authority (see CLAUDE.md) | `apps/accounts`, SimpleJWT. **Frontend return-to-destination (IR-236):** `components/auth/ProtectedRoute.tsx` passes the attempted location to `/login` in router state; `features/auth/LoginPage.tsx` captures it on mount and navigates there on success, falling back to `lib/roleDashboard.ts`; `lib/redirectTarget.ts` is the single validator every destination passes through; `lib/authSession.ts`'s idle-timeout hard redirect carries the page in `?next=`. `router/PrivateRoute.tsx` **deleted** — a second, unimported guard that ignored the hydration flag | `frontend/src/lib/redirectTarget.test.ts` (15), `frontend/src/components/auth/ProtectedRoute.test.tsx` (9), `frontend/src/features/auth/LoginPage.test.tsx` (11, of which 7 are new); shared fixtures in `frontend/src/test/authFixtures.ts` | **IR-236, 2026-09-11:** `npm test` → **9 files, 76 tests, 0 failed**; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` 0 errors (9 pre-existing warnings, none in the touched files); `npm run build` green. Asserted: hydration incomplete renders the waiting state and does **not** redirect; no token redirects carrying the destination, query and fragment intact; an expired token redirects with its own `session_expired` reason; a valid token with a permitted role renders the route; a signed-in user lacking the role gets the 403 screen rather than login. Hostile destinations rejected in favour of the role default — off-origin, same-origin absolute, protocol-relative (`//`), its backslash spelling, control-character smuggling (`/<tab>/evil`), `javascript:`, `data:`, dot-segment assembly (`/..//evil` — see the third defect below), and the auth screens themselves. The two hostile-destination login tests also assert the rejected host is **absent** from the landing page, because the role default is where a visitor with no destination goes too — without that second assertion they would pass with the feature deleted. axe-core (serious/critical) on the rendered states; all queries by role and accessible name | **PARTIAL** — rotation and blacklisting correct; **30-min inactivity expiry still absent server-side (NFR-S2)**. What IR-236 adds is that a deep link now survives sign-in, and it closed **three defects found by the tests and review rather than assumed**: (1) the expired-session `reason` never reached the login screen at all — `logout()` ran *during render*, re-rendering the guard before the redirect committed, so the second render took the "no session" branch and destroyed it; a ref now makes the decision sticky. (2) The guard's waiting state was an `aria-hidden` spinner in an unlabelled `div`, i.e. **silent to a screen reader** — it now carries `role="status"` with a name. (3) `safeRedirectPath` itself had a **live bypass**, caught by the spec review and reproduced before being fixed: `new URL` resolves `/..` by popping a segment, and popping the *empty leading* segment rewrites `/..//evil.example` into the pathname `//evil.example` — so the input never started with `//`, the origin was still ours, and the function returned the protocol-relative string it exists to refuse. The check now also runs on the **output**, which is the only placement a caller cannot walk around by making the parser do the assembling; `/review/12/../13` → `/review/13` still resolves, so the fix is not a blunt ban on `..`. **Carve-out, named rather than smoothed over:** the `?next=` query parameter is **not in IR-236's text**. It exists because the NFR-S2 idle path is a full page load that no in-memory state survives, and it is validated on arrival by the same predicate as router state — but it is a forgeable, shareable destination channel, and a hostile value is left in the address bar rather than stripped (inert, since every read re-validates). **A person should confirm that trade-off** |
| FR-M6-02 | DPA consent | **ADR-019 owed** — the basis here is the requirement id alone; SDD M6 is frozen and is not authority (see CLAUDE.md) | **Backend (IR-226):** `Record.dpa_accepted_at` / `dpa_accepted_by`, stamped by `RecordViewSet.submit` and by nothing else; `dpa_accepted` is a derived property, so no stored boolean can disagree with the timestamp. Neither field appears in `RecordWriteSerializer`'s allowlist. **Frontend:** `DpaConsentGate`, `DpaConsentModal`, `DpaConsentInline`; `recordsApi.submit(id, dpaAccepted)` takes consent as a **required** argument so a caller cannot omit it **IR-127** makes the stamp gate something: `apps/ai/policy` is ADR-015 §Security Impact's disclosure gate, a pure decision over IP status, embargo and this consent stamp, refusing every failing input rather than the first. It reads `Record.dpa_accepted` rather than `User.consent_given`, the per-disclosure fact rather than the once-at-signup one. **It currently refuses every record**, because no embargo field exists and an undetermined embargo is treated as an embargo rather than assumed absent — the correct direction for a gate, and tracked as IR-250, which blocks IR-130 and IR-131. | `apps/records/tests.py::DpaConsentAtSubmitTests` (9) | `pytest apps/records/tests.py::DpaConsentAtSubmitTests` → **9 passed in 9.43s**, run in the backend image against this branch. Asserted: a submit with no consent is refused **and the record stays in `draft`** (the ordering assertion — a check after `lifecycle.apply` would refuse a record that had already entered a review queue); an explicit `false` is refused rather than treated as merely absent; a successful submit stamps who and when; resubmission preserves the *original* timestamp; and consent cannot be set by PATCH | **IMPLEMENTED** (2026-09-10, IR-226) — **the previous IMPLEMENTED on this row was wrong and is corrected here.** It rested on two React components: the wizard's step-3 gate was browser-side only, `submit()` neither checked nor stored anything, and any direct API call bypassed it silently. The only persisted consent was `User.consent_given` — one boolean, set once at signup (FR-M6-06) — which records that a person accepted the terms, not that *this disclosure* was submitted under them. **Carve-outs, named rather than smoothed over:** (1) the migration is additive and deliberately **does not backfill** — pre-existing submitted records keep `dpa_accepted_at = NULL`, because stamping them would fabricate consent that was never given; those records are identifiable by that null. (2) **No `AuditEvent` is written**, since `AuditEvent` still has no workflow event types — that is IR-144, blocked on IR-138, and pre-empting its shape here would be guessing. The timestamp on the record is the evidence for now. (3) What the notice *says* is a separate, open problem: **IR-214** (signup consents to a ToS and Privacy Policy that do not exist) and **IR-123** (the RA 10173 text describes processing IRIS does not perform, flagged `needs-human-review`). This row covers whether acceptance is recorded, not whether the words are right — **a DPO still has to rule on those two before any real-participant validation** |

| **ADR-019** | **Persisted conversation history** — no FR- id exists for this; [ADR-019](../adr/019-persisted-unified-conversation-history.md) is the requirement, and ADR-019 §Related Requirements says so explicitly | **ADR-019** (one Conversation model, both surfaces, persisted history), amended by IR-294 §A stored citation is a pointer | **IR-295 (part A):** `apps/ai/models/conversation.py` — `Conversation` (one `User`, optional `Record`, CASCADE), `Turn` (question + answer + state, stored together) and `TurnCitation` (record, chunk, page — **no text**), replacing the two field-less `pass` classes migration 0001 created and nothing ever wrote to; migration `0008_conversations_and_turns`. `POST /ai/ask/` takes an optional `conversation_id` and appends a Turn; `GET/POST /ai/conversations/` and `GET/PATCH/DELETE /ai/conversations/<pk>/` list, read, rename and delete. Every queryset is scoped to `request.user` by `views/conversations.py::_owned` — **scoped, not permission-classed**, so a refusal is a 404 identical to a missing row (the IR-153 shape). `conversations.py::turns_for_reader` re-checks every stored citation against `Record.objects.visible_to(user)` on each read, so a pointer into a Record the reader lost access to is dropped rather than rendered. The wire's answer-state mapping moved to `presentation.py` so a replayed Turn and a live answer cannot drift. `Turn.state` stores the **domain** state and is mapped to the wire on read, so renaming an API string cannot reinterpret old rows. `record` is set when a Conversation starts and is not writable afterwards. **IR-296 (part B):** `apps/ai/resolution.py` — `QuestionResolver.resolve(question, turns)` rewrites a follow-up into a standalone question before retrieval runs, guarded by `has_back_reference` (a cheap word check, no model call) and returning `None` outright with no history at all; a failed model call is caught (`LLMUnavailable`) and also falls back to `None` rather than erroring — both fall back to the raw question, exactly as before this ticket. `Turn.resolved_question` (migration `0009_turn_resolved_question`) stores what actually ran, blank in every fallback case. `views/chatbot.py::ChatQueryView` resolves *above* the retriever — `GroundedAnswerService.answer()` and the `Retriever` port are unchanged, per IR-294's Testing Decisions ("No new seams. Resolution is a module, not a port...") — and both retrieval and synthesis run on the resolved question when one exists; the raw question is still what is stored and shown as `question`. `composition.py::CompositionRoot.resolver()` is the independent switch (`AI_QUESTION_RESOLUTION_ENABLED`, `None` when off) and the separate, smaller model (`LLM_RESOLUTION_MODEL`, sharing `LLM_BASE_URL`/`LLM_API_KEY` with the answering model per ADR-036's one-adapter shape). The cache is Django's default cache, keyed like the query-embedding cache in `resilience/query_cache.py` (question + the resolved history's Turn ids, normalized and hashed) — wired directly here rather than deferred, because resolution needs no `EmbeddingSpace` id at construction, unlike that cache. **IR-297 (part C):** `apps/ai/memory.py` — `ConversationMemory.recall(conversation, query_vector, space_id, exclude_ids)` finds Turns from earlier in a Conversation relevant to the question just asked, by `CosineDistance` over `TurnEmbedding` rows scoped to that Conversation and that `space_id` — never a second implementation's job, a module like `resolution.py`, for the identical reason (IR-294 Testing Decisions: one implementation, no vendor at all, fully exercisable through HTTP). `TurnEmbedding` (migration `0010_turn_embedding`) mirrors `ChunkEmbedding`'s shape exactly, keyed on `(turn, space)`, so a vector from a retired space is never compared against a current one. **The memory vector is the search vector**: `RetrievalResult` and `GroundedAnswer` both grew a `query_vector`/`embedding_space_id` pair carrying the embedding `TwoStageRetriever` already computed to search the corpus, and `conversations.py::record_turn` writes it onto the new `TurnEmbedding` — no second `embed_query` call, pinned by a call-count assertion. `answers/citations.py::build_prompt` gained `history` (the Conversation's recent Turns, verbatim, bounded by the same `resolution.MAX_HISTORY_TURNS` window resolution already uses) and `recalled` (memory's finds, labelled separately, in full — **nothing is summarised**); `GroundedAnswerService.answer()` takes the Conversation and its recent Turns and only ever calls memory when both a Conversation and a configured `memory()` collaborator exist. `composition.py::CompositionRoot.memory()` is the independent switch (`AI_CONVERSATION_MEMORY_ENABLED`, `None` when off) — unlike the resolver, this collaborator never calls a vendor, so there is no key that could be missing. A missing query vector (a degraded, full-text retrieval, or no active space) degrades recall to `[]` with a logged warning rather than raising, so a Conversation whose vectors are unavailable falls back to the recent-Turns window it already had. **IR-298 (part D):** Paper Chat is a Conversation that happens to have a Record attached, same models and same API as Ask IRIS — the title-prefix scoping hack (`aiApi.ask(\`${record.title}. ${question}\`)`) is gone from `PaperChatDock.tsx`. Retrieval scoping stays out of the `Retriever` port on purpose (IR-294 story 31): `TwoStageRetriever` and `FullTextRetriever` (`retrieval/degraded.py`) each take an optional `record` at **construction**, narrowing `visible_to(user)` to that one Record before anything is scored rather than instead of it — a Record can stop being readable after a Conversation was scoped to it, and both retrievers re-check on every call. `composition.py::CompositionRoot.retriever()`/`answer_service()` gained a call-time `record` parameter that builds a differently-scoped stack per request; the `Retriever.retrieve(question, user, limit)` signature itself is untouched, so this is not the fourth-implementation-widening ADR-026 warns against. `views/chatbot.py::ChatQueryView` reads an optional `widen` flag: scoped by default when the Conversation carries a Record, unscoped only for the one question that asked to widen — never remembered onto the next question, asserted by `test_widening_is_a_per_question_choice_not_a_standing_one`. `Turn.widened` (migration `0011_turn_widened`) stores which scope actually produced each answer, and the `ask` response's `widened` field and `ConversationListCreateView`'s `?record=` filter (for "does a conversation already exist for this paper") are new. **Frontend:** `lib/chatMessages.ts` (`newChatMessage`, `turnToMessages`) is the one mapping from a live answer or a replayed Turn to the `ChatMessage` shape both `RAGChatPage.tsx` and `PaperChatDock.tsx` render through `ChatMessageBubble` — Ask IRIS and Paper Chat now read and write the same Conversations through the same API, with no second chat system. `lib/chatStorage.ts` and `buildRagQuestion` (the question-concatenation helper) are deleted, along with `types/chat.ts`'s local `Conversation` type; `RAGChatPage.tsx` lists, creates, selects and deletes Conversations through `aiApi.conversations`, and a question is sent as itself — no transcript gluing. A replayed citation carries only its pointer (record, chunk, page) until IR-299 re-resolves the quote, so `ChatMessageBubble`'s `PassageCitation` and `SourceContextPanel` render a bare link rather than a blank quote when `text`/`record_title` are absent. `PassageQuote.tsx::ScopeNotice` is the one place a widened answer says so, live or replayed. A dismissible notice (`RAGChatPage.tsx`) explains the sidebar emptying when a browser still holds the pre-IR-298 `iris_rag_conversations` key, and clears it on dismissal — old local chats are not migrated (IR-294 §Further Notes: demo and testing conversations against a stub corpus). **Post-review fixes (2026-09-22, same commit range):** the `"text" in citation` quote-presence check was duplicated between `ChatMessageBubble.tsx` and `SourceContextPanel.tsx`; both now call one `hasCitationQuote()` in `lib/citedPage.ts`. `aiApi.conversations.rename` was speculative — added for CRUD completeness, called from nowhere — and is removed; nothing in this ticket needs renaming a Conversation. Paper Chat's find-the-conversation-or-start-one sequence (`list` → `get` or `create`) moved out of `PaperChatDock.tsx`'s effect into `aiApi.conversations.findOrCreateForRecord()`, which owns the decision instead of the component reaching into three separate calls. `RAGChatPage.tsx`'s `?record=` deep link created a scoped Conversation and displayed the prefilled question as an inert user bubble that was never actually asked — a real bug, not merely a smell — since Conversations no longer live in `localStorage` where the old code at least persisted the same half-finished state; `sendQuestion()` (shared by this path and `handleSend`) now actually calls `aiApi.ask` for it, so the deep link lands on a real, stored answer. **IR-299 (part E):** `conversations.py::turns_for_reader` re-resolves each stored citation against two batched lookups for the whole transcript, not one pair per citation — which cited Records `Record.objects.visible_to(user)` still allows, and which cited chunks are still live (`DocumentChunk.objects.filter(deleted_at__isnull=True)`; re-chunking tombstones a chunk rather than deleting the row, IR-115). The new `_resolve_citation` helper returns `None` for a Record the reader may no longer see (dropped, unchanged from IR-295 — existence must not leak), the full `Citation` shape (`text`, `record_title`, `page`, `context_path`, all read live off the chunk, never the stored `page` column) when the Record is visible and the chunk is live, and a bare pointer (`marker`, `record_id`, `chunk_id`, `page` from the stored column) when the Record is visible but its chunk was tombstoned — a record-level link rather than a stale or fabricated quote, exactly the degradation IR-294 §Acceptance asks for. No frontend change: `hasCitationQuote()` (`lib/citedPage.ts`) already branched on `"text" in citation`, so a replayed Turn whose citations now carry `text` renders the quote through the exact code path a live answer already used | `apps/ai/tests/test_conversations_http.py` — 40 tests, plus 3 new in `CitationReResolutionTests` (IR-299): a replayed citation's `text`/`record_title`/`page`/`context_path` match what the live answer returned for the same chunk; tombstoning a citation's chunk (`deleted_at`) degrades the replayed citation to `record_id`/`chunk_id`/`page` with no `text`/`record_title`; re-reading an 8-turn conversation costs the same query count as re-reading a 1-turn one, asserted by comparing `CaptureQueriesContext` counts rather than pinning an absolute number. 43 tests total (40 pre-existing + 3 new), at the HTTP boundary with the deterministic provider fakes (27 IR-295, 13 IR-298: 9 in a `ScopedRetrievalTests` class asserting a scoped Conversation never answers from another paper unasked, widening reaches every readable paper, widening is per-question not standing, widen is a no-op with no Record scope or no Conversation, the widened flag is stored and replayed, and a Record that stopped being readable mid-conversation yields no results; 2 for the `?record=` listing filter, scoped to the caller): a Turn is stored and reopened, a stored citation holds no passage text, a citation into a now-hidden Record disappears while the Turn stays, another user and **staff** (KTTO, IERC, Adviser, and a Django superuser) get 404s indistinguishable from a missing conversation, deleting a Record deletes the Conversations scoped to it, a two-year-old Conversation is still listed, and **twelve turns succeed** with a transcript well over 4,000 characters — the condition that used to fail, **now fixed for users too**: `buildRagQuestion` is deleted along with the browser storage that made a conversation die at roughly six turns. `apps/ai/retrieval/tests/test_two_stage.py` and `test_degraded.py` each gained a `record=`-scoping unit test (a scoped retriever returns only that Record's chunks even when a better match exists elsewhere; scoping to a Record the caller cannot read returns nothing; the degraded fallback stays scoped too). Frontend: `PaperChatDock.test.tsx` (new, 8 tests) — opens or continues the Conversation for the Record being viewed rather than starting a new one every time, never prefixes the question with the title, sends `conversation_id` and `widen`, defaults to "this paper," widens only once the reader turns the control on and never on its own, and shows the scope notice on a widened reply. `RAGChatPage.test.tsx` (new, 7 tests) — continues the most recent Conversation on load, sends a follow-up as itself rather than a glued transcript, actually asks the `?record=` deep link's prefilled question rather than leaving it as an unanswered bubble (the post-review fix above), deletes a Conversation and falls back to what remains, and the legacy-storage notice appears, stays silent, and clears the key on dismissal. `ChatMessageBubble.test.tsx` gained 3 tests for a replayed pointer-only citation and the scope notice. **IR-296:** `apps/ai/tests/test_resolution.py` — 21 pure tests (no `db_required`, a duck-typed `Turn` stand-in) covering the back-reference word check **(superseded by IR-445, 2026-10-02: the check is deleted and these cases rewritten — see the ADR-026 §7-§8 row)** (including a genuine miss — "here" is a referring word the deliberately short, deliberately loose list does not cover, asserted as the soft failure the spec calls for: no call, no error, `None`), prompt bounding to the most recent Turns, the cache key, and `QuestionResolver.resolve` itself — a repeated (question, history) pair costs one model call, a failed call is not cached (so a transient outage is retried rather than frozen onto the fallback), and a healthy call afterwards still succeeds. `apps/ai/tests/test_resolution_http.py` — 7 tests at the HTTP boundary: a follow-up whose literal word ("limitations") appears only in the *other* paper's text still retrieves the right record once resolved — the failure mode this ticket exists to fix — the resolved question is stored on the Turn and returned on replay, a resolver backed by a broken LLM falls back to the raw question without erroring, three tests pin the **zero-call** cost guarantee by asserting an injected `ScriptedLLM`'s call list stays empty (first Turn, self-contained follow-up, no `conversation_id` at all), and one asserts `AI_QUESTION_RESOLUTION_ENABLED=False` skips resolution without ever attempting to build a vendor adapter **IR-297:** `apps/ai/tests/test_memory.py` — 8 tests one level below HTTP, driving `ConversationMemory.recall` directly against real `TurnEmbedding` rows: the closest Turn wins, an excluded id is never returned even when it would otherwise be closest, recall is bounded by its configured limit, a Turn from someone else's Conversation is never reachable, a vector under a retired space is never compared against the active one, and a missing query vector or a missing active space both degrade to `[]` with a logged warning (the logger under test needs `caplog.handler` attached directly, like `test_service.py`, because `apps` sets `propagate: False`). `apps/ai/tests/test_memory_http.py` — 11 tests at the HTTP boundary: **the headline test** — a fact from a Turn pushed out of the verbatim window by `MAX_HISTORY_TURNS` fillers is still found and shown to the answering model many Turns later — recent Turns still read verbatim alongside a recollection, a recalled Turn's answer reads in full (nothing summarised), a Turn still inside the recent window is never doubled into the recollection too, the prompt stays bounded (`MAX_HISTORY_TURNS + MAX_RECALLED_TURNS` `Q:` lines, never the full 22-Turn history asked in that test), a stored Turn vector belongs to the active `EmbeddingSpace`, a vector moved to a retired space stops being recalled, two tests pin the **zero-extra-call** cost guarantee with a counting embedder (storing the vector costs nothing beyond the one search call; recall itself calls no embedder), a vendor outage degrades to recent-Turns-only without erroring, and `AI_CONVERSATION_MEMORY_ENABLED=False` switches recall off independently. `apps/ai/answers/tests/test_citations.py` gained 5 pure tests for `build_prompt`'s `history`/`recalled` parameters (a duck-typed `Turn` stand-in, no database) — verbatim order, a Turn with no answer yet omits its `A:` line, the recalled section is labelled and reads after the verbatim one, and a one-off question with neither carries none of this scaffolding | 1402 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed (full backend suite, 2026-09-21; IR-295). Migration applied, reversed and re-applied against the dev database holding 58 Records and 3,518 chunks. **Re-run 2026-09-21 after IR-296:** full backend suite exits 0 with no failures and migration `0009_turn_resolved_question` applies cleanly; `apps/ai` alone verified test-by-test under `pytest -rA` (every case PASSED, including the 27 new to this ticket) because this environment's pytest does not render the usual numeric summary footer — a pre-existing local quirk, not a suite failure, and worth confirming again in CI's own output. **Re-run 2026-09-21 after IR-297:** full backend suite exits 0 with zero `fail`/`error` occurrences in the captured output (same local quirk suppressing the numeric footer); `apps/ai` alone re-run file-by-file, all passing, including the 24 tests new to this ticket (8 + 11 + 5); migration `0010_turn_embedding` applied cleanly against the same dev database, adding only a new table **Re-run 2026-09-22 after IR-298:** full backend suite (`python -m pytest -q`, same local quirk, no `F` in the dot output and no failures section) and `manage.py check` both clean; migration `0011_turn_widened` applies with `makemigrations --check` reporting no drift. `apps/ai/tests/test_conversations_http.py` now 40, `retrieval/tests/test_two_stage.py` and `test_degraded.py` now 11 each. Frontend: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run lint` 0 errors (7 pre-existing warnings, none in touched files), `npm run build` succeeds, and `npx vitest run` is 177 passed / 0 failed across 21 files (up from before this ticket touched the suite). **Re-run 2026-09-22 after a Standards/Spec review pass** (duplicated quote-check, unclaimed `rename`, feature envy in the find-or-create effect, and the unanswered-prefill bug, all above): same four checks clean again, `npx vitest run` is 178 passed / 0 failed across 21 files. **Re-run 2026-09-22 after IR-299:** full backend suite (`python -m pytest -q`) exits 0 with zero `F`/`FAILED`/`ERROR` in the output; `apps/ai/tests/test_conversations_http.py` alone is 43 passed; `manage.py check` and `manage.py makemigrations --check --dry-run` both clean (no model changed, no migration expected). **Post-review fixes (2026-09-22, same commit range):** a code review flagged two findings. Medium: `DocumentChunk.objects.filter(...).only("content", "source_page", "context_path")` in `turns_for_reader` is a latent N+1 trap — an unannotated `.only()` silently fires a per-instance query if `_resolve_citation` is ever changed to read a field outside that list; a comment now names it explicitly. Low: `types/ai.ts`'s `ConversationTurn.citations` was typed `ReplayedCitation[]`, which was accurate before this ticket but became a lie once a replayed citation could carry the full `Citation` shape — retyped to `ChatCitation[]` (the union the type already existed for), and the stale `ReplayedCitation`/`Citation` doc comments predicting IR-299 as future work were updated to describe it as done. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings, none in touched files) both re-run clean; `apps/ai/tests/test_conversations_http.py` re-run at 43/43 | **IMPLEMENTED** — tested at the seam the caller uses (HTTP boundary on the backend), but no human review recorded yet and the feature is five parts of five (IR-294 A, B, C, D, E) |
| **ADR-026 §12-§15 · ADR-027 §9** | **Retry, multi-part questions, Listing questions and reader-feedback privacy** — no FR- id exists for these; the ADRs are the requirement, and the reader-facing vocabulary is [`CONTEXT.md`](../../CONTEXT.md) (Retry, Reader feedback, Listing question) | [ADR-026](../adr/026-conversational-retrieval-and-memory.md) §12 (a Retry is a new Turn that searches again on the previous Turn's Resolved question — never a reuse of its stored citations, which would skip the retrieval-time `visible_to(user)` check §10 enforces, and never an overwrite), §13 (failed and refused Turns stay visible to the reader and are excluded from the history handed to the model and the rewriter), §14 (the multi-part flag comes from the routing classifier — **closing the open question §2 left** — with per-sub-question retrieval, merge by rank position, at least one passage per sub-question, and the sub-questions shown), §15 (feedback stores the verdict and cited Record ids only unless the reader separately chooses to share the exchange; deleting a Conversation deletes its feedback; feedback is never a ranking input) · [ADR-027](../adr/027-corpus-level-questions.md) §9 (**listing** as a fourth routing outcome, answered from a catalogue search with `visible_to(user)` applied **before** ranking, and the model may describe the list but never extend it — §4's computes-then-reports rule applied to Records instead of counts; a separate ADR was rejected because that guard already exists here) | — **IR-392 records the decisions only; no code** | — | — | **NOT STARTED** — specified by IR-392; built by IR-398 (Retry), IR-399 (multi-part), IR-400 (listing) and IR-401 (feedback), under IR-390 |
| **ADR-026 §7-§8 (amended) · §13 · ADR-015 rule 3 note** | **Conversational memory correctness** — no FR- id exists for it; the ADRs are the requirement. Resolution runs on every follow-up and a Turn's answer is embedded as well as its question | [ADR-026](../adr/026-conversational-retrieval-and-memory.md) §Amendment — 2026-10-02 (IR-444): §8's back-reference word check is **struck** — the failure is not soft, evidenced by the IR-443 transcript ("give me a longer explanation" resolved to nothing, retrieved arbitrary passages, drew a refusal); the first-Turn skip, the cache and the raw-question fallback remain, and cost control moves to the cache plus the small model. §7 now embeds the question **and** the answer, the answer vector is a real per-Turn cost, both are stored under `input_type="query"` so `recall` ranks one list — **provisional**, with a two-list `fuse_by_rank`-style fallback named. §6 is unchanged and no summary tier is authorised; the ADR names no Turn count for the verbatim window, so nothing there changed. [ADR-015](../adr/015-voyage-embedding-and-reranking.md) rule 3 gains a note that storing answer text under the query type is deliberate; the rule itself is unchanged | — **IR-444 records the decisions only; no code** | — | **Supersedes a tested behaviour.** The **ADR-019 row's IR-296 line** asserts the back-reference word check as a soft failure ("no call, no error"); that is now the wrong specification and is **re-evidenced by IR-445**, which deletes the check. IR-296's tests are not edited here | **PARTIAL** — **IR-445, 2026-10-02:** `has_back_reference` and its word list are deleted and `QuestionResolver.resolve` returns `None` only for no history, an empty reply or `LLMUnavailable`; evidence is `apps/ai/tests/test_resolution.py` (the six phrasings the old list missed, plus a test that the check no longer exists) and `test_resolution_http.py::FollowUpWithNoPronounTests` ("give me a longer explanation" after a Turn: the resolved question, not the typed one, is what retrieval embeds). **IR-446, 2026-10-02:** recall applies a cosine-distance cut-off instead of always returning its four closest Turns — `AI_MEMORY_RECALL_MAX_DISTANCE`, read per request, provisional default 0.80 taken from observed `voyage-context-4` query distances (related Turns 0.26-0.74, unrelated 0.83-1.01; the numbers are in `config/settings/base.py`), non-positive disables it. Evidence is `apps/ai/tests/test_memory.py::RelevanceCutOffTests` (dropped beyond, unaffected within, read per request, disabled at 0) and `test_memory_http.py::RelevanceCutOffTests` (no Turn clearing it leaves the `Relevant earlier in this conversation:` heading out; a loose cut-off brings it back, pinning the cause; no distance reaches the answer or the transcript). **Three memory HTTP tests were changed deliberately**: they relied on the default scripted resolver, whose stand-in resolved question is about nothing in the Conversation, so under a floor their recall is correctly empty — they now inject a resolver that resolves to the flood question, which is the relevance they were always meant to assert. **IR-447, 2026-10-02:** a Turn carries **two** vectors per Embedding Space, not one — `TurnEmbedding.kind` (`question` | `answer`, migration 0017, unique on `(turn, space, kind)`), the answer embedded through `embed_query` by `conversations._store_answer_vector` **after** the Turn's transaction commits, and `ConversationMemory.recall` de-duplicating to one row per Turn at its best distance while scanning `VECTORS_PER_TURN x limit` rows so a Turn matching on both vectors cannot crowd out three others. `AI_MEMORY_ANSWER_VECTOR_ENABLED` (default **True**, unlike ADR-033 §5's techniques — the amended ADR decides the answer *is* embedded; the switch exists so ADR-023's with-and-without run is possible) restores question-only recall exactly when off. Evidence is `apps/ai/tests/test_memory.py::RecallingByTheAnswerTests` (the ADR's own illustration as a **pair** — the same Turn, answer and query, recalled with the second vector and missed without it; returned whole; de-duplicated; not crowding out other Turns; still excluded by `exclude_ids`; and an answer vector in a retired space never compared against a current one) and `test_memory_http.py::RecallingByTheAnswerTests` (the same pair through HTTP — a fact stated only in an answer reaches a later prompt, and does not with the switch off), `::AnswerVectorStorageTests` (stored vector equals `embed_query(answer)` and **not** `embed_documents`, pinning ADR-015 rule 3's documented exception where it is made; no row at all with the switch off; a `no_sources` Turn is never embedded, its text being an explanation rather than an answer) and `::AnswerVectorFailureTests` (a vendor failure on the answer vector still answers the request 200 and still stores the Turn with its free question vector — ADR-008, and the reason the vendor call is outside the transaction). **Migration tested against seeded pre-IR-447 data, not only an empty database** (2026-10-02): four existing single-vector rows across an active and a retired space all backfilled to `question` with every vector byte-identical, the widened constraint then admitting an `answer` row beside a `question` row on the same turn and space and still rejecting a duplicate of the same kind; 0017 also reverses cleanly. **Four memory HTTP tests were changed deliberately** — three (`NoExtraVendorCallTests`, now `PerTurnEmbeddingCostTests`) asserted §7's superseded "answer recall is free" claim and now assert what survived it: the question vector is still retrieval's own and never re-embedded, and the answer vector costs **exactly one** extra call, measured as the difference the switch makes; the fourth (`EmbeddingSpaceOwnershipTests::...retired_space...`) moved "the" row of a Turn and had to move **all** of them, its assertion untouched. **Checked against the IR-447 card's thirteen acceptance criteria once Jira became reachable**, which caught three gaps the ADR alone did not state: `partial` Turns were being embedded and are now excluded with `no_sources` and `unavailable` (`test_memory.py::AnswerVectorStateTests`, parametrised over all three, plus an empty answer and the degraded no-space path); the single-ranked-list property now has its own test (`::OneRankedListTests` — every stored vector reproduces `embed_query` and **not** `embed_documents`, and recall is shown not to filter by kind, which would otherwise make the answer vector a stored no-op every other test still passed); and the backfill command the card requires now exists — `apps/ai/turn_backfill.py` plus `manage.py backfill_turn_vectors --dry-run`, evidence `apps/ai/tests/test_turn_backfill.py` (15 tests: the plan printed before anything is sent, a dry run spending nothing, `embed_query` and never `embed_documents`, idempotent and resumable by recomputed query rather than a checkpoint, per-space skip so a retired space's vector is not mistaken for done, refusal past the ceiling from both the flag and the setting, 0 disabling it, and one Turn's failure not abandoning the rest). `promote_embedding_space` is **unchanged** and Turn vectors are deliberately not part of its readiness gate. **IR-448, 2026-10-02:** a failed or refused Turn is kept out of both prompts (ADR-026 §13, decided under IR-392 and implemented nowhere until now) — `MODEL_HISTORY_STATES` (`apps/ai/models/conversation.py`) is the one predicate, reached as `model_history_q(prefix)` and surfaced as `in_model_history()` on a `TurnQuerySet` and a `TurnEmbeddingQuerySet`, so `views.chatbot._recent_turns` (the rewriter's history **and** the answering prompt's verbatim window, one function) and `ConversationMemory.recall` cannot drift; a `Q` rather than the stronger-reading `turn__in=Turn.objects.in_model_history()` subquery, which would semi-join the whole `Turn` table to answer a question about one Conversation. **No migration** — a manager is not model state, confirmed by `makemigrations --check`. `conversations._EMBEDDABLE_STATES` is now that same object rather than a second frozenset agreeing with it, since embedding a Turn exists only to let a model be shown it later. `turns_for_reader` is untouched and the asymmetry is the decision, so every exclusion test is paired with a read of the reopened transcript. **`partial` is excluded with the other two** — a truncated answer teaches truncation, nothing in the prompt says the stop was a transport failure rather than a choice, and the fragment is the part of an answer least likely to carry what a follow-up needs; Retry (§12, IR-398) is the path back to a whole answer. Evidence is `apps/ai/tests/test_failed_turns_http.py` (8 tests: the IR-443 transcript itself — good answer, refusal, third question, asserting the stored refusal text is in neither the answering prompt nor the rewriter's, while the good Turn stays in both; the refusal still replaying in the transcript as `no_results` with its question and `message`; `unavailable` and `partial` each from its real producer rather than written straight through the ORM, since a state nothing writes would pass; a Conversation whose only prior Turn was a refusal behaving as a first Turn, asserted as **zero** rewriter calls and no `Conversation so far:` block, because a resolver that ran and returned the question unchanged would look identical on the wire; and the structural criterion — both querysets' SQL carrying the same states and `model_history_q` being what they are built from) and `test_memory.py::FailedTurnsLeaveRecallTests` (8 tests: each excluded state unreachable by vector even as the closest match, **paired** with the same Turn `generated` being recalled so a filter excluding everything cannot pass, exclusion before the `MAX_RECALLED_TURNS` cut so refusals cannot crowd out real Turns, and the exclusion being by `state` and never by answer text). **Verified to fail without the change**: reverting the two call sites and nothing else fails 9 of the 16, which is what says these tests reach the bug. Full backend suite 2143 passed, 2 skipped (pre-existing, `pyexcel`), 1 xfailed; `manage.py check` and `makemigrations --check` clean. Still to build: IR-449 (token budget), under IR-443 **IR-449, 2026-10-02:** the verbatim window is a **token budget, not a Turn count** — `MAX_HISTORY_TURNS = 6` is deleted and `apps/ai/history.py::history_window` fills newest-first until `AI_HISTORY_TOKEN_BUDGET` (default 3000, read per request) is reached, then stops rather than skipping to a smaller older Turn; the newest Turn is always present, truncated via a shallow copy so the reader's transcript is never mutated. Counting is local and reuses the already-pinned `voyage-context-4` tokenizer through a new `tokens.truncate_to_tokens` (offset-based, so a multi-byte character is never split), with `AI_HISTORY_TOKEN_MARGIN` (1.15) covering the gap to a Llama-/Qwen-class answering model — **no vendor token-counting call exists to make**, and IR-330 is named as what would calibrate the margin. `views/chatbot._recent_turns` is the single place the window is decided and hands the same list to the resolver prompt and the answering prompt, so neither can disagree about how much history there was; `resolution_cache_key` now folds in each Turn's id **and** its text lengths, because a variable window changes which ids are used and one Turn can arrive truncated two different ways. Evidence is `apps/ai/tests/test_history_window.py` (many short Turns admit more than the old six and very long Turns admit fewer, both asserted and pinned as one comparison; fills newest-first and stops; stays inside the budget; the newest Turn kept and truncated, its text a literal prefix of the original and the stored Turn unmutated; margin monotonic and clamped below 1.0; both prompts hold identical history lines; the counter *is* the pinned tokenizer and counting a prompt makes no `httpx` call; two truncations of one Turn do not share a cache key) and `test_resolution.py` (the count no longer exists; the prompt renders the window it is given whole). **`test_memory_http.py`'s scaffolding was changed deliberately**: `_fill_past_the_recent_window` asked a fixed six Turns, which under a budget no longer pushes anything out of the window, so it now asks until `_recent_turns` — the view's own predicate — reports the first Turn gone, under a deliberately tight 120-token window, and `BoundedPromptSizeTests` asserts the budget and the recall limit instead of a Turn count. **The combined ceiling is written down in `AI_HISTORY_TOKEN_BUDGET`'s comment** (12000 estimated tokens: 3000 history, ~2000 recall, ~7000 sources) because IR-449 shipped first and IR-397 owns the sources half. **Honest limit:** nothing bounds the recalled Turns' tokens, and for Turns short enough the real ceiling is `HISTORY_CANDIDATE_LIMIT` (the 50-row bound on the query), which the suite asserts rather than hides |
| **ADR-033** | **Hybrid retrieval and passage selection** — no FR- id; [ADR-033](../adr/033-hybrid-retrieval-and-passage-selection.md) (Accepted 2026-09-28, IR-391) is the requirement. Keyword search over the whole visible passage catalogue merged with vector search **by rank position** inside reranking; a database-maintained chunk keyword index; a relevance cut-off applied to **reranker scores only**, absent in degraded mode and never shown to a reader; passage selection (per-paper cap, ignored in Paper Chat · neighbour joining that keeps every region and cites the first page · a token budget in real `voyage-context-4` tokens). Amends [ADR-008](../adr/008-ai-degradation-to-fts.md) (FTS gains a second job, on the normal path) and [ADR-023](../adr/023-retrieval-quality-evaluation.md) (labels are `(record, page, quote)`; every run reports retrieval recall **and** recall over the final set sent to the model; one change at a time against a fixed baseline; a manual command, never CI) | **Two of six techniques built, both shipped off.** Every technique lands behind a setting **defaulting to today's behaviour**, and a default moves only on a harness run: IR-393 (keyword index, done), IR-394 (harness, done), **IR-395 (§1's fusion and keyword retrieval, done — `apps/ai/retrieval/fusion.py`, measured at +0.192 recall@10 with reranking on the proxy corpus and still defaulted off)**, IR-396 (nothing-relevant), IR-397 (selection) | See the IR-395 row: `test_fusion.py` (24) plus the enumeration guard | Visibility is unchanged and asserted, not assumed: both searches filter through the single `visible_to(user)` predicate inside retrieval, before scoring or merging, and `test_fusion.py::KeywordPathVisibilityTests` holds the keyword half to it (see NFR-S4) | **PARTLY BUILT** — §1-2 land in IR-395, off by default; §3 and §4 are IR-396 and IR-397; no default moves before IR-402. **Divergence recorded 2026-10-02 (IR-453):** §5's "a default moves only when a run shows the change helps" is **unsatisfiable for §3's cut-off** — both harness measures are recall measures, a floor can only remove passages, and neither committed question set holds a single unanswerable question, so a run can report what a floor costs and never what it buys. **§5 was then amended the same day (IR-453) to exempt §3's cut-off, which now ships ON** — the first and only exemption, granted because §3 is not a recall optimisation like the other five but the precondition for a reader-facing behaviour (ADR-034), so leaving it off deletes that behaviour rather than deferring it, and because a rule that can never be satisfied cannot be what holds a default shut. The reversal's cost is recorded, not softened, against three mandatory safeguards: a deliberately loose provisional default taken from observed reranker scores recorded in the PR (the IR-446 precedent), **IR-454**'s correct-refusal and false-refusal guards, and ADR-034's label shipping in the same change as the behaviour. §5 still governs the other five techniques and IR-402 still owns their defaults. §3 also gains a dependant: [ADR-034](../adr/034-ungrounded-answers-as-a-distinct-state.md) |
| **ADR-034** | **An ungrounded answer is a distinct state, never a grounded one** — no FR- id; [ADR-034](../adr/034-ungrounded-answers-as-a-distinct-state.md) (Accepted 2026-10-02, IR-453) is the requirement. A new `Turn.state` `ungrounded`, reachable **only** when [ADR-033](../adr/033-hybrid-retrieval-and-passage-selection.md) §3's relevance cut-off leaves zero passages; `GROUNDING_RULES` unchanged and a separate prompt with no `Sources:` block; no citations and no Record cards, ever; a visible not-from-the-repository label; **no research question may reach it** and a zero-source research question stays a refusal; excluded from the model's history; **ON by default** — amending ADR-033 §5, which exempts the cut-off from its off-by-default rule — and absent in degraded mode. **The not-from-the-repository label ships in the same change as the behaviour**, because with both defaults on a floor without a label is a window in which an ungrounded answer is indistinguishable from a cited finding | Amends [ADR-008](../adr/008-ai-degradation-to-fts.md) (*never a fabricated answer* is kept and given an explicit boundary — fabrication is a grounded answer that is not grounded) · [ADR-026](../adr/026-conversational-retrieval-and-memory.md) §13 (`ungrounded` joins the excluded states; `MODEL_HISTORY_STATES` stays `{generated}`) · [ADR-027](../adr/027-corpus-level-questions.md) §4 (an absolute bound: the Lens computes and the model only reports, so no landscape or corpus-level question goes ungrounded) · depends on ADR-033 §3 | **NOT BUILT.** No code yet. The gap this records is verified in code 2026-10-02: `apps/ai/retrieval/two_stage.py` ends in `.order_by(distance)[:limit]` with no relevance floor, so an off-corpus question retrieves its top *k* anyway, meets `GROUNDING_RULES` in `apps/ai/answers/citations.py`, and the model's own refusal is stored as `generated` — so `MODEL_HISTORY_STATES = frozenset({GENERATED})` (`apps/ai/models/conversation.py`) does **not** exclude it and IR-448 does not reach it. IR-396 builds the floor; this state is IR-453 | **None yet.** When built: a research question must refuse rather than answer from model knowledge (IR-453's AC, and the test ADR-027 §4 as amended demands); a zero-source question whose passages were withheld by the disclosure gate must be `no_sources` and never `ungrounded` (see NFR-S4 — the inverse would signal that relevant-but-unreadable material exists); the state must be absent in degraded mode; `MODEL_HISTORY_STATES` must still be `{generated}` | **Verified in code 2026-10-02 (IR-453), not asserted:** driving `POST /api/v1/ai/ask/` against a two-paper corpus retrieved two irrelevant papers, both reached the prompt under `Sources:`, and the resulting model-written refusal was stored `generated`. Both committed question sets were checked for calibration material and **neither holds a single unanswerable question** — `apps/ai/evaluation/fixtures/synthetic_set.json` (2) and `docs/evaluation/proxy_starter.json` (52) are all labelled, which is why ADR-033 §5's gating rule cannot be satisfied for §3's cut-off and why that is recorded as a divergence in ADR-033 rather than reconciled | **ACCEPTED, NOT BUILT.** **Reachable as soon as IR-396 lands** (revised 2026-10-02): ADR-033 §5 was amended to exempt §3's cut-off from the off-by-default rule, because leaving the cut-off off would not defer this behaviour to IR-402 — nothing can branch on a zero-source condition that never occurs — it would delete it. **IR-402 no longer gates this**; it keeps §5's other five defaults. The trade is recorded rather than softened: the floor is switched on with no run able to show it helps, against three mandatory safeguards (a deliberately loose provisional default, IR-454's correct-refusal and false-refusal guards, and the label shipping with the behaviour). **Known limitation, recorded not fixed:** in degraded mode there is no reranker, so no cut-off, so the `generated`-refusal history gap persists there; closing it needs a signal degraded mode does not have and one is deliberately not invented (ADR-008 §Amendment — IR-391) |

Worker: atomic claim (row count checked), token fencing on every write, one stale reclaim, release-and-retry or `retries_exhausted` on the final attempt, skips for no model / answer breaker / spent shadow budget, parity recorded not skipped, union route stored, fenced-out completions counted, `coverage()` for IR-467 · `apps/ai/models/shadow.py` + migration `0018` (`ShadowEvidenceDecision`, one per Turn, cascades from it; `docker compose config` valid for both files. Existing HTTP suites unmodified; `test_techniques.py`'s two registry-set assertions were changed deliberately because ADR-035 §1 adds a seventh switch. **No live vendor call and no real broker**: the task ran eagerly and by direct call | **BUILT and executed offline, not Done, and not enabled anywhere.** Default `off`, rate 0. **ADR-035 §11's vendor request/response retention check is not recorded, so shadow must not run on real reader questions** — a process control nothing in the code enforces. Not built: production `on`, web search, any agent loop, a stale-row sweep (reclaim happens only on broker redelivery; a running row past its one reclaim stays running and reports missing), and IR-467's report. **Breakers are per process**: the `answer`-breaker skip is read in the web process that served the question, so it reflects that process only, and the worker's own re-check cannot see reader failures — the bound between enqueue and run is the shadow budget and concurrency 1. IR-468 owns reader-path rate limiting. Contention at the vendor stays unprioritised, bounded only by concurrency 1 and the shadow budget |

| **ADR-023** | **Retrieval quality is measured** — no FR- id; [ADR-023](../adr/023-retrieval-quality-evaluation.md) (Accepted 2026-09-17, **amended 2026-09-28 by IR-391**) is the requirement: recall@10 over a labelled question set, two tiers never conflated, labels that survive re-chunking, two measures per run, one change at a time, manual command never CI | ADR-023 §Amendment (label format, the two measures, comparison discipline) · ADR-033 (the techniques whose switches a run gates) | **IR-133 + IR-394 (one body of work, not two):** `apps/ai/evaluation/` — `labels.py` (a label is Record + page + quote; loader refuses a quote too short to identify a passage and one long enough to straddle a chunk boundary; `--drop-incomplete` measures a partly-labelled set and names what it skipped), `harness.py` (one run = root + question set + config → report; **one retrieval call per question**, both measures off the same `RetrievalResult`), `report.py` (recall@k and final-set recall, macro and micro, plus provenance), `validation.py` (the no-cost pre-flight a labeller runs), `techniques.py` (**IR-394's configuration surface**: ADR-033 §5's six switches by name and setting, resolved against `django.conf.settings` at run time, so a technique counts as built only when its setting really exists. Every run records all six at the values it used; `--technique NAME=on|off|<n>` moves one and `--list-techniques` prints them. Asking for a switch whose setting does not exist yet is **refused**, because a run measuring the baseline under a results file claiming to have measured fusion is the one failure the registry exists to prevent. Overrides are applied around the whole run, before the retriever is built, and restored afterwards. `ResolvedTechniques` is the value the run, the report and the command all ask, so none of them reaches into a mapping's keys, and a moved switch is spelled `fusion=on` in the results file exactly as on the command line. Provenance also pins the question set by **SHA-256** as well as by path, since a set is re-labelled while it is human work in progress. ADR-023's "exactly one changed setting" is **diverged from and recorded as such** in that ADR's IR-394 divergence note: the harness warns rather than refuses, because fusion and keyword retrieval are one technique in two settings, and the note awaits a human decision. `apps/ai/answers/selection.py` is the new seam: the disclosure gate and source cap split out of `GroundedAnswerService` so the final set can be measured **without calling a model**, and so IR-396/IR-397 land where the harness already measures. `CompositionRoot.without_reranking()` swaps in a `NoOpReranker` rather than dropping the decorator the gate lives inside. Command: `manage.py eval_retrieval`. Question sets: `apps/ai/evaluation/fixtures/synthetic_set.json` (committed, run by the suite) and `docs/evaluation/proxy_starter.json` (52 labelled questions over 40 papers: paraphrased-mechanism, exact-term, near-duplicate and cross-paper) | `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_labels.py` (18 — normalization, loader refusals, and `test_a_label_survives_rechunking_at_any_ceiling`, which is the format's whole justification, plus a guard that the three ceilings really do re-chunk the fixture) · `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_techniques.py` (19 — all six ADR-033 §5 names present with unique settings, an unbuilt switch recorded as unbuilt, an override recorded as an override, an unbuilt override refused and its ticket named, a value a switch cannot take refused, and — the load-bearing one — an override reaching the stack before retrieval is built) · `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_provenance.py` (5 — `IRIS_GIT_COMMIT` wins, a blank variable falls back to git, `--dirty` is asked for, and no git is `unknown` rather than a guess) · `apps/ai/tests/test_eval_harness.py` (18 — the committed synthetic set scores full recall, a quote in no record scores zero and is named, the two measures diverge, the disclosure gate shows up in the second one, k bounds the first, both configurations run and are labelled, and the command's dry run passes and fails for the right reasons, plus the technique configuration reaching the results file and an unbuilt switch refused through the command) | `pytest apps/ai/evaluation apps/ai/tests/test_eval_harness.py` — **61 passing** 2026-09-30. `manage.py eval_retrieval --dry-run` verified against the dev database the same day: a real label resolved, a wrong one was named and the command exited non-zero | **HARNESS IMPLEMENTED, PROXY BASELINE RECORDED (2026-09-30).** All 52 questions over all 40 embedded papers, student login, Voyage, disclosure bypass on: recall@10 **0.596** without reranking and **0.683** with (+0.087, about 4.5 questions; one question is 0.019); final-set recall 0.558 / 0.663; zero degraded questions. Run file: `docs/evaluation/runs/20260930-060412-proxy-starter.json`. An earlier run over 28 questions and the first 20 papers (0.661 / 0.786) is kept beside it: the drop is the corpus doubling, not a regression, since the added papers are distractors for the old questions. **Proxy tier: an engineering gate, never a finding about CIT-U research (ADR-023).** IR-423 check: single-label questions on the 7 papers that needed windowing were found 9/9 with reranking (7/9 without), against 25/38 for the other papers, so no boundary penalty shows; n is small and this is not conclusive. `--list-techniques` and the unbuilt-switch refusal were verified against the dev database 2026-09-30, and `--dry-run` resolved all 57 labels of the 52-question set the same day; no paid run was made for this change, because with all six switches unbuilt a candidate run is the recorded baseline by construction. `git_commit` now records `IRIS_GIT_COMMIT` from the caller, else `git describe --always --dirty`, else `unknown` — superseding the "reads `unknown`" note this row carried. Still open: the disclosure gate refuses every record until IR-250, so final-set recall needs IR-317's bypass; the six switches themselves are IR-395, IR-396 and IR-397, and no default moves before IR-402. **IR-463 (annotation schema, 2026-10-06):** `labels.py` now reads and validates `kind`, `evidence_required` (`none`/`corpus`/`corpus_multi`), `expected_outcome` (`answer`/`clarify`/`decline-no-evidence`/`decline-restricted`) and `institutional`, as two fields so an empty expected list never doubles as "refuse". An empty `expected` loads only when those fields are declared (`Question.deliberately_empty`), so `--drop-incomplete` skips the unlabelled and never the deliberate; contradictions are refused; `QuestionSet.scored` keeps no-passage questions out of both recall averages. The proxy set gains ten questions in five new kinds. Tests: `evaluation/tests/test_evidence_requirement.py` (incl. the no-model source assertion), `test_proxy_set.py`, `apps/ai/tests/test_eval_no_passage_questions.py`. The refusal measure and false-refusal guard stay IR-454's; the model-facing command is IR-464's |
| **IR-423** | **A paper longer than voyage-context-4's 32k-token window can be indexed** — extends FR-M3-02 (stable label only) | [ADR-015](../adr/015-voyage-embedding-and-reranking.md) (contextualized chunk embedding) | `apps/ai/providers/batching.py` (`EMBED_WINDOW_TOKENS` = 30,000 and `window_for_embedding`, the existing `batch_by_token_budget` rule named for this use) · `apps/ai/indexing.py` (`embed_active_chunk_set` presents a long paper as consecutive windows, each its own document, and reports `windows` on the outcome) · `apps/ai/tasks.py` (carries `windows`). The Voyage adapter and the `EmbeddingProvider` port are unchanged: the adapter still refuses to split a document, and what counts as one is decided where the chunks are known. **Trade-off recorded:** a chunk is contextualized only against its own window, so chunks either side of a boundary no longer see each other; a paper that fits one window is unchanged. Before this, `embed_active_chunk_set` sent every chunk of a paper as one example and Voyage answered 400 ("Contextualized chunk embeddings do not support truncation"), so 7 of 20 newly loaded papers had no vectors | `apps/ai/providers/tests/test_batching.py::EmbeddingWindowTests` (8 — under the cap, one window when short, consecutive and ordered, none over budget, just-under and just-over, an oversized chunk left alone, budget read at call time) · `apps/ai/tests/test_chunk_embedding.py::LongDocumentWindowTests` (6 — a ~42k-token paper is several documents, none over the window, one vector per chunk, vectors stay on the right chunk across windows, `windows` reported, a short paper is still one document) | `pytest apps/ai/providers/tests/test_batching.py apps/ai/tests/test_chunk_embedding.py` green 2026-09-30. **Against the real vendor:** the 7 papers that had failed (49k, 44k, 53k tokens and four just over 30k) embedded 839 chunks with zero failures; `backfill_embeddings --dry-run` then reported all 40 papers embedded | **IMPLEMENTED, tested against the deterministic fake and the real vendor.** Not yet measured: whether window boundaries cost retrieval quality (the eval harness can now answer that on the seven long papers) |

Setting `AI_HNSW_EF_SEARCH`, default 200, clamped to pgvector's 1..1000. pgvector's own default of 40 meant the reranker's `recall_limit` of 100 was never reached | `apps/ai/retrieval/tests/test_two_stage.py::ScanDepthTests` — with sequential scan and sort disabled so the planner uses the HNSW index, a limit of 100 returns more than 40 rows (41 before the fix) and a low depth setting lowers the count | Run `docs/evaluation/runs/20260930-182254-proxy-starter.json` (52 questions, 40-paper proxy tier, `degraded_questions` 0): recall@10 no-rerank 0.596 → 0.712, rerank 0.683 → 0.885; final-set rerank 0.663 → 0.846. Fusion re-measured on the new depth (`…-182520-…json`): rerank 0.885 → 0.894 (under one question), final-set 0.846 → 0.875, and without rerank it is worse (0.712 → 0.692), so most of IR-395's +0.192 was the cap, not keyword matching.
