Two planner contexts make that comparison mean anything: `exact_scan()` forbids **both** index and bitmap scans, and `index_scan()` forbids the sequential alternative so a small table is not compared with itself — the same two flags IR-440's own test needs. `used_index` carries whether the plan really read the HNSW index (checked by `EXPLAIN` inside the measuring context), because a figure from a sequential scan says nothing about the index. `probe_titles` asks an injected retriever about each paper by name and names what came back instead. `apps/ai/management/commands/check_vector_index.py` is the thin shell, exiting non-zero below `--min-recall` and warning when no index was read. **Why both measures:** an index can be perfectly faithful to an exact scan whose top ten is still the wrong paper, and a stored-vector probe is an easier question than a real query vector (`input_type=query` lands elsewhere in the space), so the free check cannot replace the paid one | `apps/ai/tests/test_index_health.py` (24 — recall arithmetic including the empty-exact and short-result cases, the pinned probe phrasing, the title probe against a fake retriever, and against real pgvector: perfect recall on a small table **asserting `used_index`** so a vacuous pass fails, sampling capped by rows, an empty index reported rather than crashing, the depth recorded, determinism per seed, both planner contexts setting their flags and restoring them including on an exception, and the command refusing below the floor, refusing `--titles` with no user, and refusing with no active space). The pgvector tests give each chunk a separated random vector rather than the deterministic fake, whose vectors for similar strings sit almost on top of each other — a near-tied top-k makes exact and approximate disagree legitimately and would measure the fixture | `pytest apps/ai/tests/test_index_health.py` 24 passed 2026-10-01. **Two vacuous-pass bugs found and fixed while testing**, both of which made the checker report perfect health unconditionally: `SET LOCAL` survives a nested `atomic()`, so in any outer transaction (every `django_db` test) `enable_indexscan = off` outlived `exact_scan()` and the approximate side ran exactly too; and the approximate side never forced the planner onto the index, so on a small table both sides were sequential. **On the dev database:** `chunk_embedding_hnsw_idx` recall@10 0.995, `embedding_hnsw_idx` 1.000, both at depth 200, and 40 of 40 papers found by their own title. The 0.995 is the honest figure — it read 1.000 before the index was forced. **Bulk-load experiment** (scratch table, index created empty then filled the way `backfill_embeddings` fills it, 2,445 vectors, plan confirmed HNSW): recall **0.995 at depth 40 and 0.995 at depth 200**, against 0.978/0.995 after `REINDEX` — bulk loading does not degrade the index and `REINDEX` does not improve it, so **no reindex step was added**. **Eval identical** — `docs/evaluation/runs/20261001-141341-proxy-starter.json` reproduces IR-440's fusion-on run `…-182520-…` to four decimal places on every measure (rerank recall@10 0.8942, final-set 0.8750, no-rerank 0.6923/0.6250, `degraded_questions` 0), so the `REINDEX` moved retrieval quality by nothing this question set can see. **Compared against the fusion-on run on purpose:** this dev deployment's `.env` sets `AI_RETRIEVAL_FUSION_ENABLED` and `AI_KEYWORD_RETRIEVAL_ENABLED` to `True`, which the harness records per run, so the fusion-off baseline `…-182254-…` is not the comparable one. That same setting is why `apps/ai/retrieval/tests/test_fusion.py::SwitchTests` fails on this machine — the tests pin IR-402's documented default of off | **IMPLEMENTED, tested, measured.** What is **not** explained: a real index on the dev database did fail a title-shaped question before a `REINDEX`, and a freshly bulk-loaded index does not reproduce it, so the cause is a history that can no longer be replayed. Recorded as unexplained rather than guessed at — the checker is what makes the next occurrence visible |

### Non-functional

| Req | Requirement | Implementation | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NFR-S1 | Transport security | Not yet deployed | — | **NOT STARTED** |
| NFR-S2 | 30-minute inactivity expiry | **IR-166.** Sign-in opens a `UserSession` (`accounts/0011`) whose id rides in every token as `sid`. `apps/accounts/authentication.py` refuses a request or a refresh whose session has been idle past `SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MINUTES` (30) with `401 code: session_idle`, and ends it for good. Activity is the *user's*: the interface sends `X-IRIS-Idle-Seconds` (`frontend/src/lib/activity.ts`), so a poll from an untouched tab extends nothing. A request with no header counts as activity. An access token with no `sid` is accepted for its own 30 minutes; a refresh token with none is refused, so pre-deploy sessions end within 30 minutes. Idleness is judged from the *stored* activity, before the request in hand counts, so a click after a 31-minute absence does not excuse it; a 5-minute heartbeat (`GET /users/me/`, only when there is unreported input) keeps a user reading a request-free page signed in. The interface also signs itself out at 30 idle minutes (`AuthBootstrap`). The Ask IRIS streams use `fetch`, not `apiClient`, so they send the header and, on a 401, refresh once through the same `refreshSession()` and retry. A failed refresh (an idle session's included) logs out and redirects (IR-634). The refresh token's 7 days remain the absolute limit. Activity writes are throttled to one a minute, so a session can end up to a minute early, never late | `backend/apps/accounts/test_sessions.py` (18): 31 idle minutes refused and 29 not; **coming back after 31 minutes refused even though the request reports a fresh click**; refresh refused once idle; an ended session stays ended; **eight active hours not expired**; polling with a growing idle header expires at 31 minutes; background refresh does not extend; header absent, malformed or negative; logout ends the session; pre-IR-166 tokens. `frontend/src/lib/activity.test.ts` (17, including the heartbeat), `frontend/src/components/auth/AuthBootstrap.test.tsx` (3), `frontend/src/api/client.test.ts` (4) and `frontend/src/api/ai.test.ts`'s "a stream's session" block (4) for IR-634 | **VERIFIED (IR-166, 2026-10-29).** CI run 40794738484 on `91a79b0`, merged as PR #307: backend **4298 passed, 0 failed**, all 18 session tests included; frontend job green, including both files above. The first run (40792870931) failed 4 of these tests, which exposed a real defect: a request was credited with its own reported activity before the idle check, so a user back from 31 minutes away was never expired. Fixed in `91a79b0` with the tests unchanged |
| NFR-S3 | Secure configuration | **IR-154.** `CORS_ALLOW_ALL_ORIGINS` is gone from every settings module — `development.py` now lists its dev origins explicitly, and `production.py` refuses to start if the setting reappears. `SECRET_KEY`, `DB_NAME`, `DB_USER` and `DB_PASSWORD` are read through `base.py`'s `required()` and have no defaults, so a missing one raises `ImproperlyConfigured` instead of falling back to the committed credential. `production.py` re-reads `ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` without base's `localhost` default and rejects empty, `*` and non-https values. Both Compose files and the gateway's `DATABASE_URL` take the credential from the repo-root `.env` via `${VAR:?}`. **IR-379** extends the same posture to per-task Inference configuration: `apps/ai/inference/startup.py` refuses startup on a task name outside the closed set (a typo like `LLM_ANWSER_MODEL` configures nothing, so without this it reads as a feature never switched on) and on a task whose model is set while no key resolves, naming the task, the variable that turned it on and every variable that could supply a key. **Configured means an operator set a model in the environment**, not that a model resolved: `answer` inherits the flat `LLM_MODEL`, which ships *with* a default, so refusing on the shipped default would make a vendor account a precondition for running IRIS at all. Setting `LLM_MODEL` does count, inherited or not, so the long-standing flat configuration — a model set and the key forgotten, which is the reported failure — is checked like any other; `.env.example` ships `LLM_MODEL` empty to match. An **unconfigured** task is off rather than an error — which is what keeps IRIS, and the test suite, startable on a machine with no vendor account. The rules are pure functions and `AiConfig.ready()` raises, so `runserver`, gunicorn, a Celery worker and `manage.py check` all refuse alike | `apps/tests/test_settings_validation.py` — **18 executed, 0 skipped**: 16 against the pure rules in `config/settings/validation.py` (blank-vs-absent, every production rule, accumulation) and 2 repository sweeps (no `CORS_ALLOW_ALL_ORIGINS` assignment; no retired credential literal). Plus the real modules booted end to end, six environments: good prod config boots; empty `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `*`, an http origin, a blank `SECRET_KEY` and a blank `DB_PASSWORD` each raise `ImproperlyConfigured`; development boots unchanged. `docker compose config` validates both files with the vars set and fails by name without them. A CI step repeats the good/empty pair. `apps/ai/inference/tests/test_startup.py` — **20 executed, 0 skipped**: 16 against the pure rules (both mistakes, one typo across several variables reported once, every real task name and every legacy flat `LLM_*` variable accepted, blank-vs-absent key, accumulation) and 4 that run `manage.py check` in a subprocess — a per-task keyless model, the inherited flat `LLM_MODEL` with no key, and an unknown task name each exit non-zero naming the offender, while a run with nothing configured still exits 0. Three more in `test_profiles.py` pin what a refusal may name: a named vendor drops the inherited key from the list but not the inherited model. Verified red by disarming the `ready()` call | **PARTIAL** — every code-side criterion is met and tested. Open: **rotating the previously committed credentials is an operations step**, and the interim VPS environment is populated by `IR-157`. Note also that the AI gateway still receives a `DATABASE_URL` at all, which contradicts [ADR-014](../adr/014-ai-gateway-as-a-service.md) precondition 4 (no direct DB access) — recorded here rather than reconciled, and owned by `IR-58` |
| NFR-S4 | Server-side authorization | Originally 12 endpoints unprotected. **The authorization matrix (IR-615) is the standing evidence:** every records and documents route is declared per role and fired, and an undeclared route fails CI (`apps/tests/test_access_matrix.py`; see the IR-615 section above).<br><br> 2 `RecordViewSet` actions (`submit`, `tags`) fixed and tested (`IR-120`). Separately, **the public `/media/` route that bypassed all of them is gone (IR-152)** — nginx alias, the prod web-container mount, and Django's `DEBUG` `static()` route all removed | `apps/records/tests.py::SubmitOwnershipTests`, `::TagsPermissionTests`; `apps/documents/tests.py` — 4 executed (owner 200, non-owner 403 — 404 since IR-627, anonymous 401, URLconf), **2 skipped** inside the backend container, which cannot see `nginx.conf` or the compose file. Plus a **manual, unautomated** probe: `/media/abstracts/thesis-manuscript.pdf` 200 → 404 | **PARTIAL** — the `/media/` bypass is closed and the download path is proven, and 2 of the 12 endpoint-level gaps are closed and tested. The nginx guard runs only on a full checkout, and **the deployment smoke test S-01 asks for does not exist** (belongs with IR-157). The remaining endpoint-level gaps are tracked as `IR-147` subtask B (`IR-153`).<br><br>**IR-165 (2026-09-06) closes the role-level half.** Every `is_django_staff(user) or <role check>` is removed, so `ADMIN_ROLES` constrains for the first time; migration `accounts/0009` reverses `0005`'s `is_staff` seeding for role-holders; audit narrows to RDCO (SRS FR-M6-06); `_can_review` and `_can_submit_clearance` no longer let one office act for another; and `RecordViewSet.create`, previously ungated, is now `IsAuthor` (Student or Adviser, per SRS M2-2.1/M2-2.2). Evidence: `apps/tests/test_authorization_matrix.py` — 8 tests, every role × action asserted in both directions, negatives asserting **403** rather than "not 200". Still PARTIAL: the object-level half (`visible_to`) remains open as IR-153.<br><br>**IR-153 (2026-09-09) closes the object-level half.** `Record.objects.visible_to(user)` is the one read predicate — office staff, owner, assigned adviser, or the public catalogue — and `RecordViewSet.get_queryset()` applies it on **every** action, not only `list`, so `GET /records/<id>/` no longer serves any record to any authenticated account. Filtering in the queryset (rather than refusing in a permission class) is what makes the refusal a **404 identical to a genuinely missing record**, so the API does not confirm someone else's draft exists. `list` narrows further to `PUBLICLY_VISIBLE_STATUSES`, so Discover stays a catalogue. On the documents side, the six endpoints that resolved a record from a request parameter with no ownership check — `submit/`, `records/<id>/slots/`, `uploads/?record=`, `uploads/create/`, `files/?record=`, and `files/download-all/?record=` (a ZIP of every file on any record) — now go through `authorize_record_documents()`, and the five hand-written owner-or-staff checks in `documents/views.py` and `reviews/views.py` are replaced by the single `core.permissions.owns_or_staffs_record()`. Evidence: `apps/records/tests.py::RecordVisibilityTests` (10 tests: owner, stranger, assigned vs unassigned adviser, staff, published, anonymous, and two Discover regression guards including the 404-indistinguishability assertion) and `apps/documents/tests/test_record_document_authorization.py` (6 tests, each sub-testing all six endpoints in both directions). **Status of that evidence: see the PR — these were authored but not executed locally**, because the developer Postgres role lacks `CREATEDB` and the test database cannot be created on this host; CI's own Postgres service runs them. **Proposals leave the public catalogue (IR-264, 2026-09-19, ADR-021 §13):** `PUBLICLY_VISIBLE_STATUSES` narrows to `(PUBLISHED,)`, so the one predicate -- `visible_to()` and `publicly_visible()` -- stops exposing approved and completed Proposals everywhere it is read: Discover, record detail, `similar`, the dashboard charts and totals, and Ask IRIS `search`, `ask` and `status` (no `apps/ai` file edited). Owners, the assigned adviser and office staff keep access. Deletion is decoupled onto a new `DELETE_REVIEW_STATUSES = (PUBLISHED, APPROVED, COMPLETED)`, so an approved or completed Proposal still needs RDCO review to delete; the owner's `approved_mine` count reads the same set. The vocabulary check on the public-status count was changed deliberately, marked "Rule change, IR-264". Evidence: `apps/records/test_proposal_visibility.py` (11 tests through the records, Ask IRIS and dashboard APIs: stranger and unassigned-adviser 404, owner/adviser/ITSO/RDCO 200, Ask IRIS search/ask/status and `similar` exclude Proposals, delete raises a `DeleteRequest`, published thesis unaffected; the `similar` test was confirmed to fail against the old public set), one added case in `test_complete_authority.py`, and `features/discover/DiscoverPage.test.tsx` (3 Vitest, axe clean; it also found a pre-existing unnamed filter listbox, fixed). Runs 2026-09-19: targeted backend 150 passed locally; Discover Vitest 3/3, `tsc` and touched-folder ESLint clean; **CI (PR #92, run 35437613909) backend 982 passed, 2 skipped (IR-315), 1 xfailed**. **The full frontend Vitest suite has no run on record**: the local run was stopped repeatedly by host memory exhaustion (not a test failure), and CI's Frontend job runs lint and build only, not `npm test`.<br><br>**IR-460 (2026-10-06) — a refusal cannot say why.** `empty`, `withheld_all` and `none_relevant` are one case at the response boundary: equal field for field on `/ai/ask/` and `/ai/ask/stream/`, a withheld-all question recorded `no_sources`, no withheld count in any response body or ordinary log (`log_summary` drops it), and no module outside the classifying three reading the outcome. Evidence: `apps/ai/tests/test_outcome_indistinguishable_http.py`. `none_relevant` is forced by the test, since no relevance floor exists until IR-396. **Residual, accepted:** timing differs with gate work and is not mitigated; repeated probing at a non-zero temperature is not addressed. Corrects ADR-034 §Security Impact<br><br>**IR-474 (2026-10-06) — an office attaches only where it takes part.** `POST /documents/files/upload/` (`attach_file`, ADR-032 §10 as amended) checked `IsStaff` and then wrote a `RecordFile` onto any record id. It now resolves the record through `authorize_record_documents()` (404 for an id that names nothing, instead of a 500) and requires an active assignment the user can staff, `tracker.requestable_parties` — the same test Paper View's adapter uses to offer the action. `owns_or_staffs_record` alone would have changed nothing, since it admits every office. An office with no part gets **403**, not the card's 404: office staff can see every record, and ADR-022 §Amendment 4 keeps 404 for a record the caller cannot see. Students stay 403 by role. Evidence: `apps/documents/tests/test_record_document_authorization.py::RecordFileUploadAuthorizationTests` (6 tests: taking part 201, no part 403 and nothing written, completed assignment 403, missing and malformed id 404, Student owner and stranger 403, anonymous refused)<br><br>**IR-476 (2026-10-06) — an office removes only its own file, while it takes part.** `DELETE /documents/files/<id>/` authorized with `owns_or_staffs_record`, so any office could delete any office's supplementary file on any record, and the stored file with it. A file now belongs to the office that filed it: `RecordFile.party` (migration `documents/0010`, backfilled from the uploader's current role; RDCO always `rdco`, never `intake`; a gone or non-office uploader stays null). `apps/documents/attachments.py` is the one rule: the file's party is one the user can staff **and** `requestable_parties` is non-empty. The delete view and a per-file `can_remove` on both `GET /documents/files/` and the record detail `files` ask it, and `FilesSection` shows Remove only where it is true. Owners lose the delete they had; the superuser's escape hatch is `RecordFile` in Django admin, which deletes the stored file on single and bulk delete and writes an IRIS audit event. The rule is recorded in ADR-032 §10's IR-474/IR-476 amendment. Refusals follow ADR-022 §Amendment 4: 404 for a missing file **or one on a record the caller cannot see**, 403 otherwise. Evidence: `test_record_document_authorization.py::RecordFileRemovalAuthorizationTests` (14 tests: own file 204 with the stored file gone, another office's 403, no part 403, completed assignment 403, owner 403, null party 403 for everyone, RDCO at intake and final review 204, missing 404, unseen record 404, anonymous refused, attach stores the party and RDCO stores `rdco`, `can_remove` per viewer on both payloads), `::RecordFileAdminTests` (3), `test_record_file_party_migration.py` (office, non-office and null uploaders), and `FilesSection.test.tsx` (Remove shown for `can_remove: true`, absent for `false`)<br><br>**IR-316 (2026-10-10) — a request cannot confirm that a private record exists.** `POST /records/download-requests/` and `POST /records/delete-requests/` resolved `record` against every Record, so an id the caller cannot read (an approved Proposal, private since IR-264) was answered differently from an id no record has. Both serializers now resolve it through `VisibleRecordField` (`visible_to(user)`), so an unreadable id fails with the **same 400, code and message** as a missing one, and nothing is created or notified. **The card's premise that delete requests were already guarded was wrong:** IR-165 guarded only approve/decline, so any account could file a delete request for any record. Filing one now takes what `DELETE /records/<id>/` takes, owner or office staff, and is a **403** on a record the caller can read but does not own (IR-153's 404-vs-403 rule). `PATCH /records/delete-requests/<id>/` fell through to `IsAuthenticated` and could repoint a pending request at another record before RDCO approved it; it is RDCO-only now. `status` is read-only on both serializers, so a request is always created pending. Evidence: `apps/records/test_request_visibility.py` (10 tests through the API: stranger refused a private Proposal exactly as a missing id on both endpoints, nothing created and no notification, published record and own Proposal still requestable, posted `status` ignored on both, stranger 403 deleting a readable record, owner 201, stranger PATCH 403 with the request unchanged); run recorded on the PR<br><br>**IR-496 (2026-10-10) — the delete-request queue has no write routes.** A delete request is raised only by `DELETE /records/<id>/`, which moves accepted work to `pending_delete` and records the status to restore. `POST /records/delete-requests/` and `PATCH /records/delete-requests/<id>/` are retired (405 for every role); `DeleteRequestViewSet` is a `ReadOnlyModelViewSet`, so neither can be wired back by a URL alone, and the unused frontend `requestDelete` is gone. Before removal, CI pinned what the POST did (run 38031335537): it put nothing on hold, approve then deleted an unheld record, and decline answered 400 **after** saving the request as declined. IR-316's delete-request create and PATCH tests were **deliberately** replaced by the 405 tests. Evidence: `apps/records/test_request_visibility.py::DeleteRequestWriteRouteTests` (2 tests, every role sub-tested) and `apps/records/test_delete_request_queue.py` (3 tests: delete puts the record on hold with its previous status, approve soft-deletes, decline restores)<br><br>**IR-300 (2026-10-11) — mark-read resolves the id within the caller's own notifications.** `PATCH /notifications/<id>/read/` looked the id up unscoped: an unknown id was a 500, and any signed-in user could mark someone else's notification read, creating a `NotificationRead` for it. All three notification endpoints (list, mark-read, mark-all-read) now go through one scope, `Notification.objects.visible_to(user)`; anything outside it is a 404 identical to a missing id. The shared scope also closes a leak the old inline filter had: for a user with no role (`admin@cit.edu`), `Q(broadcast_to_role=user.role)` matched `IS NULL`, i.e. every direct notification. Evidence: `apps/notifications/test_failures_and_scope.py::MarkReadScopeTests` (6 tests: unknown id 404, own direct 200, another user's direct 404 with no read row, another role's broadcast 404, own role's broadcast 200, roleless user 404 and empty list) and `::OneScopeTests` (3 tests: list equals the scope, mark-all-read writes only the scope, a roleless user's mark-all-read writes nothing)<br><br>**IR-627 (2026-10-29) — upload and file routes stop confirming ids on records the caller cannot see.** `GET /documents/uploads/<id>/download/`, `DELETE /documents/uploads/<id>/` and `GET /documents/files/<id>/download/` take an upload or file id, not a record id, and answered 403 for an object on a hidden record beside 404 for a missing id, so any signed-in account could probe which ids exist on drafts. Per ADR-022 §Amendment 4 all three now resolve through `documents.views._on_visible_record` (shared with `RecordFileDeleteView`, IR-476): a hidden record is a 404 byte-identical to a missing id, and a caller who can see the record but may not reach its documents (the assigned Adviser) still gets 403. `DocumentDownloadPermissionTests::test_non_owner_is_refused` asserted the 403 and was changed deliberately. Evidence: `apps/documents/tests/test_document_access_control.py::ObjectIdRouteExistenceOracleTests` (3 tests: hidden equals missing on all three routes, Adviser 403 on all three, a hidden upload is not deleted); and the IR-615 matrix, whose three `KnownDefect("IR-627")` markers on `record-upload-download`, `record-upload-delete` and `record-file-download` are removed here, so those cells now assert the 404 |
| NFR-S5 | Audit immutability | **IR-166.** A PostgreSQL trigger (`audit/0005`) refuses every DELETE on `audit_auditevent` and every UPDATE that changes the row (compared whole, so a future column is covered too). The `user`/`record` foreign keys become `DO_NOTHING` with no database constraint in the same migration, so deleting a subject leaves its rows untouched, still carrying its id; the API shows `user_name: null` for it. Binds every client of the table, not just the ORM. INSERT is unaffected. The audit API stays list-only (`GET /audit/`, RDCO). Not covered: TRUNCATE, and the table owner dropping the trigger, which now takes a deliberate, reviewed migration | `backend/apps/audit/tests/test_immutability.py` (17): ORM save, queryset update, raw SQL UPDATE, row/queryset/raw DELETE all refused and the row unchanged after; insert still works; deleting the user leaves the row exactly as it was; clearing or re-pointing the user refused; an unchanged save allowed; a deleted user's row still lists; POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE on `/audit/` → 405 for RDCO; no detail route | **VERIFIED (IR-166, 2026-10-29).** CI run 40794738484 on `91a79b0`, merged as PR #307: all 17 tests above pass against real PostgreSQL, within 4298 passed, 0 failed. **Not covered**, by design: TRUNCATE, and the table owner dropping the trigger in a deliberate migration |

## Open defects

### IR-300: a failed notification left nothing in the logs — FIXED

Every notify function in `apps/notifications/services.py` wrapped its whole body in `except Exception: pass` (18 blocks), so a renamed `NotificationType`, a bad query or a failing email dropped the notice silently. Each function still never raises into its caller; the `except` now calls `_log_failure`, which logs at ERROR with the traceback through the module logger, naming the function and the record (or the request and its record, or the user for a role request).

**Test.** `apps/notifications/test_failures_and_scope.py::NotifyFailureIsLoggedTests` rejects a record through `POST /records/<id>/decide/` with `Notification.objects.create` patched to raise: the decision returns 200, exactly one ERROR record names `notify_decided` and the record, and it carries the traceback. `::NoSilentExceptTests` parses the module and fails on any handler whose body is only `pass`.

Evidence, 2026-10-11, local against a pgvector Postgres: `pytest apps/notifications/test_failures_and_scope.py` — **11 passed**; full backend suite — **2907 passed, 2 failed**. Both failures also fail on `main` without this change: the `ktto_review` and policy source scans walk into a `backend/venv/` checked out inside the tree and cannot decode a cp1252 file there.

**Merged with `main` 2026-10-11.** IR-274 deleted three of the converted functions (`notify_record_reviewed`, `notify_clearance_result`, `notify_resubmit`) and IR-517 added three with bare `except Exception: pass` (`notify_record_withdrawn`, `notify_record_deleted`, `notify_delete_requested`); those three now call `_log_failure` too, so the count is still 18. `pytest apps/notifications` — **20 passed**, IR-517's delete-notification tests included; full backend suite — **3266 passed, 5 failed**, the same five that fail on `main` (the two `venv/` source scans, the two `test_complete_authority` old-URL subtests, and the IR-202 chunker flake).

---

### IR-422: a long paper title crashed record creation (FR-M2-01) — FIXED

`Record.abstract_file` was declared with no `max_length` override, so Django's `FileField` default of 100 applied — a ceiling on the whole stored path (`abstracts/<name>.pdf`), not the filename alone. `load_corpus` (IR-313) and the ordinary submission path both derived the stored filename directly from the source filename or title with no truncation, so any title with a stem over roughly 90 characters crashed with `SuspiciousFileOperation` instead of saving. Found loading a 20-paper astrophysics corpus for the IR-133/IR-394 retrieval eval: 6 of 20 real arXiv filenames (145-149 characters) hit this and aborted the whole `load_corpus` run at the first offender.

**Fix.** `abstract_file_path` (`apps/records/models.py`) replaces the bare `upload_to="abstracts/"` string: the stored name is now a random UUID plus the original extension, so the title's length and any filesystem-hostile characters (`--`, runs of `.`) never reach storage. `max_length` is raised to 255 (migration `records.0013`) for headroom regardless. `load_corpus` also now catches `SuspiciousFileOperation` per-record and reports it like a non-PDF file rather than aborting the batch, as defense in depth for the same class of storage-layer failure. `download_service.resolve_record_download_file` was updated to rebuild the download's suggested filename from `record.title` rather than reading it back off the now-random stored path, avoiding a UX regression (a meaningless UUID filename on download).

**Test.** `apps/records/tests.py::LongManuscriptFilenameTests` posts a manuscript with a 145-char filename stem (matching the reproduction) through the real API and asserts a 201 and a correctly-saved file, rather than only inspecting the field definition. `apps/records/test_load_corpus.py::test_a_title_past_the_old_100_char_ceiling_loads_without_crashing` proves the same at the point the bug was found — a bulk load. `test_a_storage_failure_is_skipped_per_record_not_fatal_to_the_batch` proves the per-record catch independently of the model fix.

Evidence, 2026-09-30, local: `python manage.py test apps.records apps.documents apps.ai` — **252 passed**.

---

### IR-233: a resubmission refused by the upload guard looks like the Record went back to declined (FR-M5-01) — FIXED

**Superseded 2026-10-10 by IR-260:** the new-version endpoint accepts a
metadata-only revision, the strict xfail marker is removed, and all three
regression cases pass. The reproduction and xfail evidence below describe the
pre-cutover system.

**Reproduced 2026-09-15** against the report's own Record (seed_demo's `[DEMO] Declined by IERC, ITSO and KTTO preserved`, id 38 in the dev database). The backend log shows the owner's first `POST /api/v1/reviews/resubmit/` returning **400** with a 77-byte body. That is exactly `{"detail":"Please upload at least one updated document before resubmitting."}`. The Record stayed `declined`, and the paper view kept showing the IERC decline twice: once in the clearance track and once in the review history. After an upload, the second resubmission succeeded, leaving `parallel_review`, IERC `pending`, and ITSO and KTTO `cleared` with `preserved: true`. **The clearance-aware transition itself works.**

**Root cause.** There are two halves. (1) `resubmit_record` accepts only a new `RecordUpload` as evidence of a revision, so a resubmission with no revision is refused, and so is one that only edits metadata. (2) The paper view shows the refusal as a small red line under a button that offers no upload, so a refusal reads as a second decline. The other candidates were ruled out. **Candidate 2:** the IERC decline stored `Review.stage = "ierc"`, so the preserving branch ran: IERC `pending`, ITSO and KTTO preserved. **Candidate 3:** the page did refetch after the successful resubmit, and the refused attempt changed no state to go stale. **Candidate 4:** the UI never called `records/<id>/submit/`. That action still accepts `declined` with no `(declined, submit)` edge in the table, a separate broken entry point that IR-260 removes. **Removed by:** **IR-260** (backend cutover) for the guard, which will accept an upload or a metadata edit under ADR-021 §11. **IR-259** for the display, which moves refusals into the Action required panel. IR-262/IR-263 let an office ask for a document without declining at all.

**Test.** `apps/reviews/test_resubmission_regression.py` sits outside every suite IR-260 retires, and it asserts behaviour through the API rather than stage names.
- `test_resubmission_with_no_revision_is_refused_…` passes. It pins the reported click path, which stays correct under ADR-021 §11, and stops IR-260 flipping the xfail by simply deleting the guard.
- `test_resubmission_after_an_upload_…` passes. It guards the clearance-aware path that already works.
- `test_resubmission_after_a_metadata_revision_…` is `xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError, reason="IR-233…")`, so an unexpected pass fails the build and a broken setup step errors instead of passing as the known defect. **IR-260 removes the marker.**

**Note that the xfail pins ADR-021 §11's metadata-edit rule, not the reported click path.** A strict xfail of the literal report is impossible: with an upload it passes today, and with no revision it must keep failing after the fix. **The display half has no regression test**; that is owed to IR-259's Vitest suite.

Evidence, 2026-09-15, backend container: `pytest apps/reviews/test_resubmission_regression.py` gave **2 passed, 1 xfailed**. Without the marker, the xfail test fails on `400 != 200 … Please upload at least one updated document`. With a strict xfail added to the upload test instead, the run reports `XPASS(strict)` → FAILED. The full backend suite gave **841 passed, 1 xfailed, 8 skipped, 4 failed**, run before the no-revision test was added. None of the four failures touches this change: `test_celery_routing` (IR-199, container-only broker difference); a Hypothesis `FailedHealthCheck` in `test_chunker_core[fixed-window]`; and two source-file sweeps (`test_lifecycle` policy mentions, `test_enum_vocabulary` `ktto_review`) that crash on a cp1252 file in a gitignored local `backend/venv/`, visible only through the container's bind mount.

---

## The audit gap — closed by IR-144

Until IR-144, `AuditEvent` had no workflow event, so the research metrics in
[`../mvp-validation/03-final-evaluation-plan.md`](../mvp-validation/03-final-evaluation-plan.md)
(turnaround time and preserved-clearance counts) had no data source. IR-144 adds the workflow
types (see its section above and [`../evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md`](../evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md)).
**The log is not backfilled:** acts before the deployment that carries IR-144 have no rows, and
events not written during the Weeks 11-12 pilot cannot be reconstructed afterwards.

---

## Maintenance

- Update this table **in the same PR** as the change that affects a requirement
- Never move a row to VERIFIED without a link to actual evidence
- If a requirement is descoped, set DEFERRED and cite the ADR or SRS amendment — do not delete the row
- Review the whole table before the technical defence; an unverified requirement reported as unverified is defensible, a false VERIFIED is not

## IR-514 — reader-question router (before reader integration)

ADR-038 §3 and §9 require fixed phrase and Paper Chat scope checks, one Jev Decisions request with six route questions and an injection question, a `route` Inference backup on Jev failure or uncertainty, and a passage fallback. `apps/ai/routing/` holds that decision without any Passage or previous answer input. A flagged or unscreenable question has `planner_allowed=False` and records `QUESTION_INJECTION` without the question text, with the reader user attached when supplied. Landscape remains on the passage path until the Lens exists. `AI_JEV_ROUTING_ENABLED` defaults off, and Groq cannot serve the backup without a separately recorded approval switch. IR-515 will consume the decision on the reader path; this change does not alter an answer yet.

Evidence: `apps/ai/tests/test_question_routing.py`, `test_route_question_command.py`, `providers/tests/test_openrouter_decisions.py` and `inference/tests/test_profiles.py` cover request contents, fixed routes, scope, vendor failures, uncertainty, screening, audit dispatch and profile inheritance without vendor accounts. The focused tests and the full backend `pytest` suite passed on 2026-10-11 using a dedicated temporary test database; `manage.py check` and `makemigrations --check --dry-run` also passed. A real public-question call on 2026-10-11 returned all seven named Noul answers in one HTTP 200 response from `typesafe/jev-1.13-20260917`; `count=0.97`, `injection=0.03`. `manage.py route_question 'How many papers about aquaponics are in the repository?'` returned `lane=count`, all seven probabilities and `stage=fixed` with Jev enabled for that command. The one-question fallback remains in the adapter for a provider rejecting a multi-question shape.

**Status: IMPLEMENTED, NOT INTEGRATED.** ADR-038 in `main` still says Proposed despite IR-499 being marked Done. The owner accepted the ticket decisions in Jira, but the ADR document's named approval and merge gate have not been reconciled; this branch must not merge until they are.

## IR-489 — selected answer models and request data controls

The owner selected `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, followed by `openai/gpt-6-luna`, `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, and `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`. The answer task can configure this ordered chain through OpenRouter. A pinned chain sends one request per model, each with its own `provider.only` list beside `data_collection: deny`; startup refuses missing or incomplete pins and pins on Groq. The deployment operator must verify the endpoint locations before setting `LLM_ANSWER_PROVIDER_PINS`. No paid quality comparison is part of IR-489, and no model quality claim follows from this change.

## IR-512 — bounded single-loop planner, answer checks and private run audit

**Implemented, offline only; ADR-038 remains Proposed.** The inner loop in
`apps/ai/research/planner.py` offers the existing corpus tools plus an
argument-free `finish`. Identity, scope, disclosure and limits are supplied
by the application. Duplicate calls reuse the registry cache and spend a
call; malformed calls get one correction and the second stops planning.
The `plan` inference profile inherits the answer model/account by default
and has its own breaker. The initial slice fed tool results back; the IR-513
section below records its extension with history, notes, aggregate context
and the outer loop.

`synthesis.py` supplies the ledger to `GroundedAnswerService`, preserving
numbered-source prompts and `parse_citations`. Validation checks raw markers
before parsing, literal numbers, marked/explicitly introduced titles and
completeness phrases. Leaked reasoning is removed before validation, and
validation uses the exact sources supplied to synthesis.
Failures withhold the answer, retaining sources and reason codes. The owner
confirmed this D4 policy in the IR-512 implementation chat on 2026-10-11.
ADR-038's broader architectural approval remains outstanding.
See [RESEARCH_PLANNER.md](../engineering/RESEARCH_PLANNER.md) for
the literal validator's limits and wall-clock exhaustion behavior.

Evidence: `research/tests/test_planner.py` covers successful citation,
duplicates, every planning budget, corrections, provider failure, empty
sources, late responses, argument rejection and validation withholding.
`test_validation.py` covers invented markers, numbers, titles and
completeness; `test_audit.py` exercises owner-only HTTP access and the
`ask_agent` command; `test_plan_profile.py` covers profile/account inheritance.
`test_tool_deadline.py` demonstrates that Voyage retrieval calls receive
the remaining run deadline. The pre-change answer-request snapshots in
`tests/test_shadow_off_snapshot.py` pass unchanged on both Ask IRIS endpoints,
and `test_one_retrieval_stack.py` passes without widening its one-predicate
rule. Tests use scripted models and deterministic embeddings/reranking.

The final full backend run on a fresh PostgreSQL test database on 2026-10-11
reported **3,118 passed, 1 xpassed, zero failures** in 207.37 seconds. Backend
static checks (`flake8 apps/ config/ core/ testing/`) also passed. The run
isolated local vendor/profile configuration; no live credentials or models
were used. Standards and spec reviews found no remaining material blockers
after the fallback budget, source mapping, reasoning and title fixes.

Migration `0019_research_run_audit` was rolled back and reapplied on a
development database copy on 2026-10-11, retaining **60 records and 2,145
chunks**. Audit tables store only identifiers, digests, statuses and
measurements; deletion cascades with the owner/Conversation. Django checks
and migration drift checks pass (the existing Axes deprecation warning
remains). No paid planner measurement or reader rollout is claimed.

## IR-513 — full research context and bounded outer planning

**Implemented, offline only; ADR-038 remains Proposed.** The human user
approved the explained design and public test seams on 2026-10-11 with
"do what you recommend", including D6's policy to omit an entire prior
exchange if any cited paper is now restricted or gated. The design is
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-ir-513-research-context-design.md`.

The planner makes an explicit short plan before any corpus call. After each
bounded sub-task, it checks sufficiency against the aggregate evidence and
finishes, continues or replans. Outer plans, local calls, run-wide calls,
prompt tokens and time remain bounded by settings; only local calls reset.
Prompt assembly sends retained passages and abstracts verbatim with stable
handles, generated recent owned history, separate short non-citable notes
and code-produced tool facts. No memory recall runs. Visibility, scope and
disclosure are rechecked, including clearing planner-derived context when
access is lost. Single-request and cumulative prompt budgets drop whole
weakest passages first, with a deterministic coverage note.

Evidence: `research/tests/test_context_loop.py` captures requests for recent
answers, omitted restricted/gated/widened exchanges, no recalled text,
verbatim passages and abstracts, and permission changes during a run. Stale
metadata counts leave planner and synthesis requests, and aggregate caches
recompute when visible membership changes; private provenance is not rendered. It
demonstrates first-task sufficiency, outer/inner/run limits, unchanged
duplicate caching across replans, reported token usage accumulated across
replans, invalid plans, passage-count and prompt-token dropping, and the
single final answer call. Note/unknown reference tests preserve sources,
withhold invalid answers and demonstrate no regeneration. Scripted providers
and deterministic embeddings/reranking are used, never a paid vendor call.
Existing planner tests were deliberately adapted to the new explicit control
protocol, including audit step counts; their underlying assertions remain.

Static checks, Django checks and migration consistency passed on 2026-10-11;
the existing Axes deprecation warning remains. No migration or live routing
change is needed. The full-suite and independent-review results are recorded
on an isolated checkout and PostgreSQL test database: **3,176 passed,
1 expected failure, zero failures and zero skips** in 235.65 seconds. The
expected failure is the existing non-strict IR-469 HNSW scan-depth test.
Provider/profile configuration was isolated, with no paid calls. Existing
test warnings remain. Independent standards and spec reviews using GPT-6 Luna
found no remaining material findings after the stale-count finding was fixed
and re-reviewed. This section claims no reader rollout or research-quality
measurement.

## IR-501 — paper screening and topic counts

See also IR-513 above for the planner's context assembly around these tools.

**Implemented, offline tools/workflow only.** `screen_records` checks an
application-supplied candidate set using the independent `screen` inference
profile. Model failures, missing/invalid decisions and unsupported quotes
leave papers unassessed. The disclosure gate is checked before each batch;
gated candidates contribute only to the unassessed total, without individual
identity or reason. The human user confirmed this D3 policy and the public
test boundaries in the IR-501 implementation chat on 2026-10-11.

`topic_count` screens every visible scoped record at or below the ceiling;
above it, it screens ranked candidates and reports `matches_found`. Its SQL
count uses distinct included record IDs over current visibility. Owners,
versions and chunks never multiply the count. The code supplies conservative
wording and possible-duplicate flags; separate records are never merged.
Metadata counts retain their existing contract. See
[PAPER_SCREENING.md](../engineering/PAPER_SCREENING.md).

Evidence: `research/tests/test_screening.py`, `test_topic_count.py` and
`test_screen_profile.py` exercise the registered tool, count workflow and
public profile interface with scripted providers and PostgreSQL. They cover
disclosure and scope, batch/vendor failures, malformed responses, quote
provenance, budgets, candidate-set caching, screening beyond retrieval's top
ten, duplicate flags, include/exclude counts and author/version multiplicity.
The focused research/inference suite and unchanged-answer/retrieval guards
passed **266 tests** on 2026-10-11 before the final in-flight-edit regression
was added. The final full backend suite on a fresh PostgreSQL test database
passed **3,152 tests, 1 xpassed, zero failures and zero skips** in 217.29
seconds. The xpass is the existing non-strict IR-469 HNSW scan-depth test.
Provider/profile configuration was isolated; tests used no live model calls.
Backend static checks, Django configuration and migration drift checks passed.
Existing test warnings remain, including Axes deprecation, pagination and a
closed command-output wrapper. Independent standards and spec reviews found
no remaining material findings after permission/content-aware caching,
publication revalidation and in-flight-edit fixes.

No screening quality measurement, real institutional result or reader rollout
is claimed. ADR-038's overall Proposed status remains unreconciled with Jira's
accepted-scope comment; broader human approval remains required before merge.


## IR-504 — research lane rollout: serving, progress and staff-first enablement

ADR-038 (Proposed). `apps/ai/tests/test_asgi_streaming.py` drives Django's real
`ASGIHandler`: three concurrent long research streams each deliver their first
events while still running and a fourth request completes meanwhile; a reader
leaving closes the run on its own thread. Both tests fail with
`EventStreamResponse` removed (checked 2026-10-12). `test_research_rollout_http.py`
covers `staff` mode (staff routed, readers on the unchanged `off` path with the
router never constructed), `agent_step` ordering and content-free payloads, and
replay of `Turn.research` steps and coverage notes. `test_routed_chat_http.py`'s
off test still pins every endpoint's off behaviour. `research/tests/test_progress.py`
covers running-then-outcome steps, an interrupted audit and the content-free run
log line. Frontend (superseded by IR-596: `ResearchSteps` is deleted; see `StreamStatusRow.test.tsx`): `ResearchSteps.test.tsx` (live trail, progress line,
collapsed finished trail, coverage notes, replay, axe) and `useAskStream.test.tsx`
(step upsert, unknown events ignored). Five planner tests now read coverage notes
from `coverage_notes` instead of the answer text — a deliberate contract change.
See [RESEARCH_LANE_ROLLOUT.md](../engineering/RESEARCH_LANE_ROLLOUT.md).

The default-off development switch preserves the existing passage adapter request

and missing evidence, landscape refusal (including router outage), overrides, Paper
Chat/injection restrictions, widened research dispatch, persisted decisions and

## IR-436: overview visibility and disclosure are explicit

**Basis:** ADR-015 Security Impact (per-record disclosure), CLAUDE.md Security
(one visibility predicate), and IR-425 part F.

`RecordOverviewView` resolves through `Record.objects.visible_to(user)` before
calling the overview service. `overview_for` checks the existing per-record
vendor disclosure predicate before either cache access or whole-paper assembly.
A refused record returns `unavailable` with no overview and no model call,
including when an overview was stored before disclosure was refused. Visibility
still returns the same 404 body for an inaccessible record and a missing ID.
Allowed readers continue to share one stored overview row.

Dedicated evidence: `backend/apps/ai/tests/test_overview_security.py` covers draft
owner access, an inaccessible cached record versus a missing record, refusal
before assembly with no model call or stored row, refusal of a cached overview,
and two readers receiving the same stored row and timestamp with one model call.
These tests are independent of IR-431's whole-paper assembly coverage.

**Executed 2026-10-11:** all four overview test modules, 65 passed. The cached
refusal regression fails against baseline `b1378a1` and passes with this change.
Configuration check passes with the existing Axes deprecation warning; flake8
and `git diff --check` pass. Full backend suite in an isolated test database:
3,277 passed, 4 failed, 1 xpassed (`python -m pytest -q -o
addopts=--strict-markers`, `DB_NAME=iris_ir436_verification`, `IRIS_REQUIRE_DB=1`).
All four failures also reproduce on unchanged baseline `b1378a1`: the two
`ResolveProfileTests` vendor inheritance tests and the streaming reasoning-body
and grounded-answer temperature adapter tests. They are local model-configuration
failures outside this change. An earlier shared-database run was invalidated by
another test process dropping/rewinding its database; it is not verification
for this change. Standards and Spec review each found zero actionable issues.

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required.

## IR-543 — exact metadata counts in the `count` lane

A supported metadata inventory question ("how many papers are in the repository", by classification,
PSCED area, record type, completion year or IP flag) returns a database `COUNT(DISTINCT id)` over the
public catalogue the asker can read, with its predicate, snapshot, distinct-Record unit, possible
duplicate titles and AI eligibility stated; unsupported fields are declined by name.
`apps/ai/research/metadata_count.py`; contract in
[`docs/evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md`](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md) §8.
Evidence: `apps/ai/tests/test_metadata_count_http.py` (JSON and SSE boundary, reopened conversation, exact
fixture counts, repeated owner/chunk joins, duplicates, empty results, staff vs student, AI-ineligible
records, changed permissions, declined fields; every model and outbound HTTP forbidden),
`apps/ai/tests/test_metadata_count_interpreter.py` (rule set, no database), the b01 `count` baseline in
`fixed_workflow_baseline.json`, and `ChatRouting.test.tsx` for the transcript rendering.

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required. Scope is the development
research lane only.

## IR-544 — durable queued questions resumed through the existing progress stream

With `AI_DURABLE_ASK` on (default off), a question asked inside a Conversation over `/ask/stream/` becomes an owned
`AskRun`. The run is created with its first event in one transaction and dispatched after commit to the `default`
Celery queue. The same SSE stream follows the run's ordered public journal (`id:` cursor), and
`GET /ask/runs/<uuid>/stream/?after=N` replays and tails it. Fenced attempts give exactly one terminal outcome and at
most one Turn. Design: [ADR-040](../adr/040-durable-queued-ask-iris-questions.md) (**Proposed**); contract in
[`docs/evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md`](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md) §9.

Evidence:
- `apps/ai/tests/test_durable_ask_http.py`, with deterministic providers only:
  - submit, disconnect and reconnect to one final Turn equal to the reopened transcript;
  - full replay and `Last-Event-ID`;
  - redelivery after success writes nothing;
  - a restarted worker fences out the attempt it replaced;
  - a conversation deleted before, or during, the answer stays deleted;
  - no reasoning and no router probabilities in the journal;
  - replay re-checks visibility;
  - a foreign run is indistinguishable from a missing one;
  - deadline expiry by the worker and by a reader;
  - switch off and one-off questions unchanged.
- `src/lib/sse.test.ts` (the `id:` cursor and keepalive comments).
- `src/features/ai/hooks/useAskStream.test.tsx` (reconnect from the cursor, a restarted attempt, `run_failed`, giving up, unknown events ignored).

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required, as does ADR-040's acceptance.
Enablement is gated by the pilot README §6.

## IR-545 — queued answers under deployment-wide capacity and spend controls

With `AI_PROVIDER_ADMISSION=enforce` (default `off`), every paid provider attempt is admitted against shared limits
before it is sent. That covers a chat completion, a tool call, a Voyage embedding or rerank, and a Jev decision. A
retry or a fallback model is another attempt. Reservations live in Postgres (`ProviderCampaign`,
`ProviderReservation`, migration 0024) and are written on their own `admission` connection under a campaign row lock.
A queued question is admitted to a bounded queue with per-asker caps. Design:
[ADR-041](../adr/041-shared-provider-admission.md) (**Proposed**); contract in
[`docs/evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md`](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md) §10. **No limit value
is approved.** Every limit is a required setting, and an unset one refuses paid work.

Evidence, with deterministic fakes and real Postgres; no vendor called:
- `apps/ai/admission/tests/test_shared_processes.py`. Independent processes with their own interpreters contend for
  the same campaign row:
  - one lane limit is shared (3 admitted of 10 asked);
  - one campaign ceiling is shared (7 of 20, the 75% core of 10);
  - a crashed worker's reservation holds its slot until its lease ends, then is abandoned and charged in full.

  With the row lock removed, the lane test admits 4 and fails.
- `apps/ai/admission/tests/test_ledger.py`:
  - worst-case reservation, then settlement to reported usage;
  - unknown usage charged at its reservation;
  - an unsent attempt released;
  - the dearest fallback price reserved;
  - a retry and each fallback model admitted separately;
  - refusals that send nothing: unpriced, uncapped, lane not configured, not configured;
  - denials not retried, failed over or counted against a circuit;
  - lane and account concurrency, including one credential across two lanes;
  - the protected reserve, and its release;
  - a sticky run cap;
  - a store failure refusing; a settle failure staying charged;
  - the lease bounded by the run deadline;
  - a closed stream settled; lease renewal; an abandoned lease refusing renewal;
  - the campaign-row invariant;
  - `manage.py provider_campaign`.
- `apps/ai/admission/tests/test_wiring.py`:
  - the production chain is Retrying → Admitted → adapter;
  - the completion cap is sent, tool calls included;
  - SDK retries are off;
  - real-adapter usage is reported;
  - Voyage is charged to its lane or workload;
  - a refused Voyage request degrades retrieval;
  - Jev decisions are admitted, and a refused one routes to the backup;
  - every Inference task has a lane.
- `apps/ai/admission/tests/test_limits.py`:
  - every required setting fails closed;
  - a malformed value stops startup;
  - `AI_DURABLE_ASK` needs enforcement;
  - the 75/25 split.
- `apps/ai/admission/tests/test_scope.py`: lanes and runs around generator steps, including steps in fresh contexts as
  under ASGI.
- `apps/ai/tests/test_admission_http.py`, over HTTP and the event stream:
  - a queued answer is charged to `ask:<run id>`;
  - every vendor call is bounded by the original 30 s deadline;
  - an exhausted campaign journals `capacity_limited` and ends `unavailable` with its sources;
  - the queue is bounded and fair between askers (`503 busy`), and a refusal queues nothing;
  - unset queue limits refuse;
  - local fakes incur no charges;
  - ownership is still rechecked.

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required, as do ADR-041's acceptance and every
limit value (pilot README §6). Nothing has measured real-provider throughput or sizing for 100 simultaneous
submissions.

## IR-546 — early candidate preview and bounded, screened listing

A "which papers are about X" question in the `listing` lane selects at most twenty distinct candidate Records from
the approved word, meaning and passage signals, shows them at once as relevance-pending, screens them in bounded
parallel title-and-abstract checks, and returns every candidate in exactly one state (matches, does not match,
uncertain, could not be assessed) with source-linked quotes, subset wording and explicit inconclusive, zero-match and
degraded-search states. Access is read again before each send, update, replay and output.
`apps/ai/research/listing.py`; contract in
[`docs/evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md`](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md) §11.

Evidence, with deterministic fakes and real Postgres; no vendor called:
- `apps/ai/tests/test_listing_workflow_http.py`, over the JSON, SSE and conversation boundaries:
  - preview before any decision, every state reconciled, stable ids, reopened Turn equal to the live listing;
  - the twenty-candidate cap, including when every assessment fails and with many chunks per Record;
  - an invented handle, title or quote cannot become a decision;
  - a paper AI may not read is counted and never sent or named;
  - uncertain papers rechecked with passages when limits allow, and not when they do not;
  - a paper that loses access before sends, during a call or after the run, and one edited mid-call;
  - no candidates, zero matches and word-matching-only search worded as subsets;
  - batches in flight never exceed the configured bound; a stalled batch ends at the deadline with partial results;
  - a worker carries the question's workload and admission run;
  - the queued journal replays candidates in order and re-filters them for the reader.
- `apps/ai/research/tests/test_listing_outcome.py` (selection cap and wording, no database).
- The b03 `listing` baseline in `fixed_workflow_baseline.json` and `test_fixed_workflow_baseline_http.py`.
- `src/features/ai/components/CandidateList.test.tsx` and `useAskStream.test.tsx` (pending then moved by id, status
  announcement, axe, AI-restricted never named, inconclusive, older listings).

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required. The `screen` endpoint is still not
approved for pilot traffic (pilot README §5). No screening recall, latency or cost has been measured, so the
twenty-candidate limit and the 10 s goal stay unvalidated.

## IR-547 — topic-match counts and the screening cache

A "how many papers match X" question in the `count` lane now selects at most twenty distinct candidate Records through
the IR-546 workflow, screens them, and states the include decisions as a classified-subset count with its assessed and
unassessed totals, criterion, method and version. It is never a model-written number, a catalogue metadata count or a
claim about the whole repository. A per-reader screening cache (`AI_SCREEN_CACHE`, off by default) reuses verdicts for
listings and counts, re-checking access, disclosure, content and quote on every reuse. Cross-reader sharing is not
built and needs a named person's approval. `apps/ai/research/listing.py`, `apps/ai/research/screening_cache.py`,
`apps/ai/models/screening.py`; contract in
[`docs/evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md`](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/README.md) §12.

Evidence, with deterministic fakes and real Postgres; no vendor called:
- `apps/ai/tests/test_topic_count_http.py`, over the JSON, SSE, queued-replay and reopened-conversation boundaries:
  - seven matches among sixteen assessed of twenty selected, with four unassessed, the criterion, method, version and
    the not-a-catalogue-count statement; the sentence equals `done.answer` and the reopened Turn;
  - pending contributes nothing; all-unassessed is inconclusive and never "0 matches"; zero, none found, uncertain and
    twenty-five available (twenty selected) are worded as subsets; many chunks are one candidate;
  - a model reply carrying its own count is rejected whole; malformed, timed-out and unavailable screens are unassessed;
  - cold, warm, changed abstract, extraction, model, approval policy, criterion and prompt; one reader never reads
    another's rows; listing and count share verdicts; the cache holds a decision and its verbatim quote, never a title, abstract or failure;
  - revoked access before, during and after a warm run, a gate that now refuses a cached paper, and a quote that no
    longer holds; hits never exceed the twenty-record allowance;
  - concurrent writers and a lost insert race leave one row; an unreadable cache never fails a count;
  - observed hits, misses, calls, estimated tokens and stage times, with the note that a warm run shows no capacity;
  - the queued journal replays a count and re-filters it for the reader; a reopened Turn rebuilds the count.
- `apps/ai/tests/test_routing_lanes.py` (the count seam, no database) and the b02 baseline in
  `fixed_workflow_baseline.json` with `test_fixed_workflow_baseline_http.py`.

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required. The `screen` endpoint is still not
approved for pilot traffic (pilot README §5), enabling `AI_SCREEN_CACHE` needs the policy label set by someone with
authority, and no screening recall, latency or cost has been measured.

## IR-556 — on-demand, cited whole-paper briefs

**Contract:** [proposed ADR-036 amendment](../adr/proposals/ir-556-paper-brief-task.md).
The new inference task and runtime feature are implemented; architectural
acceptance remains a separate human decision, and the feature defaults off.

`apps/ai/briefs/` reads every active undeleted chunk in sequence with page
markers, using the explicitly recorded main-model context window minus bounded
completion and instruction reserves; each fallback must fit before sending. Oversized papers
are read in section briefs and combined without losing verified values or
citations. All seven fields distinguish reported, not reported and unsupported.
Quotes must match stored chunk text at their cited page before saving.

`PaperBrief` and migration `0026` retain content/prompt/Profile versions,
generating models and token accounting. The authorized HTTP endpoint and
`brief_for(..., user=...)` queue first use only, share atomic claims across
readers, suppress duplicate worker delivery and fence stale writes. Generation
uses the Overview's existing queue and IR-545's research/account admission,
with a stable spending run across explicit retries. Disclosure and current
access are checked for cached uses, every generation call and final writes.

**Tests:** `apps/ai/tests/test_paper_brief_http.py` exercises first use/reuse,
content/prompt/model/fallback invalidation, long-paper order and subdivision,
not-reported fields, invalid quotes/pages, denied or revoked access/disclosure,
concurrent first requests, duplicate delivery, stale claim fencing, shared
admission exhaustion and usage, retry totals and fallback model provenance.
The inference Profile/startup and admission wiring suites cover inheritance,
explicit context-window parsing, output cap and the named task/lane.

**Local evidence, 14 October 2026:** focused HTTP/worker, inference and admission
checks after review fixes: 124 passed, including revocation between physical

applied to an isolated copy of the local database; all 60 Records and 42

Final fresh-database backend suite: **3,671 passed, 1 xpassed**, zero failures.
The historical backfill test's cleanup now uses its existing historical-model

## IR-554: a Publish click survives Discover's search debounce

**Defect.** Discover's search box writes `q` to the URL 300ms after the last keystroke, and once on load. The timer called the `setSearchParams` of the render that armed it, and react-router-dom 6.30's functional form builds from that render's URL, so a Publish click inside the window was written back out and the dialog closed. It surfaced as a flaky page-head test (5 of 12 runs failed on `main`, 2026-10-13) and failed PR #249's Frontend job once.

**Fix.** `DiscoverPage` keeps the latest `setSearchParams` in a ref and the timer calls that, so it builds from the current URL.

**Test.** `src/features/discover/DiscoverPage.test.tsx` › "keeps the Publish dialog open when the search debounce fires after the click" and "keeps both a just-typed search and the Publish dialog in the URL" — both failed on `main` before the fix. Evidence, 2026-10-13, local: the Discover file **21 passed**, and **0 of 20** consecutive runs failed; `npm test` **876 passed, 3 failed**, the same three failing on `main` without this change (`Sidebar.test.tsx` ×2, `PaperAiOverview.test.tsx` ×1, timing-sensitive on this machine; green in CI); lint 0 errors, build green.

Question-specific section reads stay within that Record's verified model context.
The existing durable run fixes a comparison deadline and explicit token/cost caps;
campaign and concurrency limits still apply. Cache reuse, generation, publication
and replay recheck permissions, disclosure and source versions. Stale replay
withholds prose, table and sources. Comparison history is excluded from model reuse.

**Evidence, 14 October 2026.** `apps/ai/tests/test_comparison_http.py`: **28 passed**,
covering first use/cache, bounds/ambiguity, hidden/missing/gated Records, partial
results, invented claims, source changes during generation and replay, independent
sectioned long papers and question-specific rows. `admission/tests/test_shared_processes.py`
adds a two-process test demonstrating one shared comparison ceiling distinct from
the chat ceiling. Existing durable/scope regression tests: **22 passed**.
Frontend comparison/API/progress/stream tests: **45 passed**, including accessible
table ordering, exact removed-claim count, page navigation and replay.
Frontend typecheck/build pass; lint has no errors and six existing warnings.
The full frontend run before the final bounds test had **967 passed, 3 failed**;
all three Sidebar failures and AbortSignal errors reproduce on an isolated,
unchanged `origin/main` copy (**15 passed, 3 failed**).

**Limitations.** IR-542 contains no labelled comparison baseline. Deterministic
fake-provider tests establish application behavior, not semantic correctness or
real-provider quality. Human labels and separate correctness/claim/citation review
remain necessary. No paid evaluation, architectural acceptance or rollout occurred.
See [Record comparison contract](../engineering/RECORD_COMPARISON.md).

Final fresh isolated backend suite: **3,708 passed, 1 xpassed**, zero failures.
The first non-isolated attempt overlapped another test process using the same
database; it was stopped and superseded by the isolated run. The prior isolated
run found a workflow-status literal, now replaced by the existing tool status,
and the already documented Windows CRLF proxy-checksum mismatch. Restoring the

Both new migrations also preserved 12 populated legacy Turns and existing

configuration and backend static checks pass (the existing axes warning remains).

horizontal overflow; the table scrolls within its region at phone width.

Independent standards/spec reviews found and resolved citation-text duplication
in Turns, first-use brief accounting, named-route admission and incomplete detail

## IR-548 — open, cancel and retry research from owned conversation URLs

Every Ask IRIS conversation has its own address, `/ai/conversations/<id>`, under the Ask IRIS layout. `/ai` only
chooses one (the latest, a new one, a Record's or a handed-off draft's) and replaces itself with that address. The
address grants nothing: the page loads through the owner-scoped Conversation API, so someone else's conversation reads
exactly like a missing one, and a deleted one stays deleted. Opening, refreshing, Back and Forward reload the
transcript; a queued run still answering is followed again from its first event, replayed through the existing
re-authorising journal. With `AI_DURABLE_ASK` on, the asker can cancel a run
(`POST /ask/runs/<uuid>/cancel/`) and retry one that ended without its answer (`POST /ask/runs/<uuid>/retry/`). Design:
[ADR-040](../adr/040-durable-queued-ask-iris-questions.md) §Amendment — IR-548 (**Proposed**).

Evidence:
- `apps/ai/tests/test_durable_ask_http.py::OwnedRunLifecycleTests`, deterministic providers only:
  - an opened conversation names its unfinished run (`latest_run`) and stops once its Turn exists;
  - cancel mid-answer commits one terminal `cancelled` state, writes no Turn (partial or whole) and journals one terminal
    event; cancelling again changes nothing; cancelling a finished run is a 409 that keeps the answer;
  - a cancelled queued run is never started by its late delivery;
  - a cancelled run's scope admits no new provider attempt (`run_cancelled`);
  - retry is a linked run with its own deadline and a replay that starts over; a repeated retry returns the same run;
  - only a run that ended with no Turn can be retried, and not while another run in the conversation is unfinished;
  - a `partial` run is refused, so a retry never adds a second Turn for the same question;
  - a stopped run stops being offered once a later answer exists;
  - someone else's run cannot be cancelled or retried (404 identical to a missing one);
  - a deleted conversation's runs stay deleted.
- `src/features/ai/hooks/useAskStream.test.tsx` (following an existing run from event 0, Stop calling cancel and
  ending on `run_cancelled`, detaching without cancelling).
- `src/features/ai/RAGChatPage.test.tsx` §owned conversation URLs (the address, opening a named conversation, moving
  between conversations and back, an unavailable conversation, following a running run, Stop, Retry, a Record's
  conversation keeping its draft).

Not covered here: Paper Chat does not yet re-attach to a run that is still answering when its panel opens; it stays
bound to its Record's conversation, and that conversation opened at its Ask IRIS address follows the run. Real-provider
behaviour is unmeasured. `Turn.reasoning` keeps IR-381's policy and no historical reasoning is deleted; replay still
carries none.

**Status:** implemented and tested; human review and approval remain required, as does ADR-040's acceptance.
