## IR-550 — first fixed-workflow milestone evaluation

An instrument that scores the metadata count, topic-match count and presence listing against pinned labels, each
measure with its own denominator and ground truth, plus capacity accounting in which every submission stays in the
denominator, and a campaign plan that refuses paid work which does not fit before the protected reserve. The
recommendation is **do not enable**: [first-milestone report](../evaluation/fixed-workflow-pilot/first-milestone-report.md).

Evidence:
- `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_milestone.py`: metadata counts checked against their population, a contract count that
  disagrees with its population refused, missing cases never counted correct, screening precision/recall over assessed
  labelled candidates only, candidate recall owed every visible labelled-relevant Record, visibility and restriction
  breaches, topic-count consistency, the held-out lock (mismatch, overlap, label change), exploratory standing for
  public or unsplit data, review fraction/rubric/disagreements, judge scores never interpreted.
- `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_capacity.py`: rejection, failure, timeout, cancellation and unfinished work in the
  denominator; latency from the original submission; previews, unavailable and inconclusive never successes; the 30 s
  deadline; only a real, cold, simultaneous, 100-distinct burst is a capacity claim; plans that would reach the reserve.
- `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_http_driver.py`: SSE parsing, a simultaneous burst over a mock transport, transport
  errors as failures, sanitized run files.
- `apps/ai/evaluation/tests/test_pilot_commands.py`: the proposed campaign cannot fit a 100-submission burst at the
  illustrative per-run caps; a paid burst that does not fit sends nothing; a paid run refuses without enforced
  admission; rescoring a published run with a reviewer's verdicts.
- `apps/ai/tests/test_pilot_milestone_http.py`: snapshot S0 through the authenticated HTTP/SSE boundary with fakes,
  sanitized and scored end to end.

Not covered here: no paid run, no pilot-family labelled set on proxy or CIT-U data, no held-out split, no human review
and no capacity burst. The control run's figures measure arithmetic and plumbing, not quality, latency or cost.

**Status:** instrument implemented and tested; every evaluation gate is OPEN and human review remains required.

## IR-601: the AI Overview polling test no longer races its own timer

**Defect.** `PaperAiOverview.test.tsx` › "explains that generation is pending and polls until the overview is ready" advanced the fake clock by 2500ms once. The component's poll timer is set by an effect that React runs on a faked timer after "being prepared" is on screen. A single advance could therefore run that effect partway through and leave the new 2500ms timer beyond the advance, so the poll never happened in time. It is a test bug; in a browser the timer simply fires.

**Measured, 2026-10-15, local:**
- Run alone, the test failed **30 of 30**, and in every failure the poll timer did not yet exist when the clock moved. In its whole file it failed 2 runs in 5. It also failed PR #276's Frontend job once.
- Waiting for the timer before advancing: **0 of 15** failed, which confirms the cause.

**Fix.** The test advances in steps, at most three, until the poll has happened. It does not use `waitFor`, because its own timeout is a faked timer too. The assertions are unchanged: the pending state is announced, exactly one poll follows, and the overview renders. A deliberately broken poll (a 60s interval) still fails the test.

**Evidence:** alone, **0 of 30** failed; in its file, **0 of 10**. `npm test` **1071 passed, 3 failed**, the three `Sidebar.test.tsx` tests that fail identically on `main` locally and pass in CI.

## IR-589 — approved general-tools conversation contract and regression baseline

IR-589 records [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md) as **accepted by Jive Tyler Revalde on 2026-10-15**: the controller is the default reader path without an on/off switch; detailed supported answers survive nonessential tool failures; one bounded repair may preserve independently supported parts; both prompt boxes carry the mistake notice; and the outdated SRS/SDD are not current engineering authority. It changes no runtime behavior, approves no provider or spending limit, and does not accept the separate proposed ADR-038/040/041 details. The [version 1 specification](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §§1–2, 5–11 owns A01–A15. The [fixture pack](../../backend/apps/ai/evaluation/fixtures/general_tools_contract_v1.json) separates approved expectations from source-observed current failure wording and unmeasured cases; `test_general_tools_contract_fixture.py` validates scenario coverage, record and ordering consistency, snapshot digest, literal current wording, and specific regression oracles without a vendor or database. `test_general_tools_current_http.py` is a runnable fake-provider control over HTTP, SSE and reopened Conversation; its 34 database cases skipped locally without Postgres. The [evaluation protocol](../evaluation/general-tools/README.md) and [acceptance map](../evaluation/general-tools/acceptance-map.md) own development/held-out split, human rubric, pinned manifest, proposed thresholds and paid-test approval boundaries. **Evidence:** 3 fixture tests passed, 34 HTTP controls skipped, owner decision recorded; runtime behavior, paid quality results and production gates remain open for downstream tickets.

## IR-590 — typed evidence-only tool contracts and scoped handles

**Requirement.** [General-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §3.1 and §3.3 under [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md): a versioned result envelope with no answer, wording or UI hints; closed, bounded schemas with application-injected context; run-scoped handles and bound cursors; coverage that names its subject; cached results reauthorized before reuse.

**Built.** `ToolRegistry.invoke` returns `results.Envelope` (`schema_version` 1; `status` one of ok/empty/partial/ambiguous/unavailable/denied; `items`, `evidence`, `facts`, `coverage`, `continuation`, `limitations`) with a private `Trace` that `public()` never emits. `envelope_of` adapts the legacy `ToolResult`; `call()` keeps its return type and the research lane its prompts. Each tool declares a `CoverageSubject`; an unknown total is `null`; a truncated read is `partial`; `denied` is reserved for references this run may not use and carries no content or counts, while budget, floor and stale-cursor refusals are `unavailable`; forbidden keys (`answer`, `wording`, `route`, raw ids, in any case or camelCase) are refused at construction. `invoke` logs the private trace (run id, tool, reason code, timing), never content. `check_schema` now refuses unbounded strings, integers and arrays and any parameter named for injected context or a raw id. `ToolRun.start(..., scoped_handles=True)` tags handles with the run, so another run's or user's handle is rejected as unissued; `reissue` turns canonical record ids back into handles only where still readable and disclosable; `issue_cursor`/`resume_cursor` bind a continuation to tool, arguments, caller and source version. A cached result is reused only while every source is still readable, disclosable and on its recorded version; otherwise it is recomputed. **This changes the legacy lane too:** a repeated call there is now reauthorized (extra queries per duplicate, and a stale cached `screen_records` result can spend another screening call), and a handle argument now admits 24 characters instead of 12.

**Not built here.** No tool issues a cursor yet, nothing produces `ambiguous`, there is no separate AI-eligibility coverage for metadata aggregates, and the legacy lane does not opt into scoped handles; these belong to the generalized operations (IR-591) and the controller. The envelope is not on an HTTP path until the controller consumes it, so the HTTP capture below exercises the legacy wrappers, not the envelope; stale cursors and source versions are covered at the registry seam only.

**Evidence (2026-10-15).** `apps/ai/research/tests/test_envelope.py`, `test_registry_contract.py`, and the IR-590 cases in `test_ledger.py` and `test_schema.py`; `apps/ai/tests/test_research_tool_contract_http.py` captures every planner request at the fake vendor over `/ask/stream/` and shows malformed and foreign-handle calls rejected and a hidden draft never sent. `pytest apps/ai/research` passes except `test_plan_profile.py` and `test_screen_profile.py`, which fail identically on `main` because this machine's `backend/.env` holds a real API key.

## IR-591 — generalized resolution, search, reading, aggregation and evidence extraction

**Requirement.** [General-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §3.2 under [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md), acceptance scenarios A04–A07: five composable, read-only tools on IR-590's envelope, with no answer prose and no fixed task sequence.

**Built.** `general_tools()` registers `resolve_papers`, `search_research`, `read_papers`, `aggregate_records` and `extract_evidence` (`apps/ai/research/tools/{resolve,search,read,aggregate,extract}.py`) beside the legacy six, which are unchanged. A tool may return `Shaped`, an envelope-native status/items/facts/coverage/continuation that `envelope_of` passes through. **Resolve:** exact titles first (`research/titles.py`, now shared with the comparison lane's `_named_records`), then short title, then whole-phrase; several matches, duplicate titles included, are an `ambiguous` group of at most five candidates ordered by an optional topic hint; unknown, hidden and vendor-withheld references are all `match: none`. Ordinals resolve only against an application-supplied `ToolRun.presented` (`Presentation(revision, record_ids)`); with none, `no_presented_list`. **Search:** papers (record fusion) or passages (the one retriever), real metadata filters (completion year, not publication year), issued-handle scope inside Paper Chat scope, exact named-paper matches ranked first and labelled apart from related ones, each paper's best passage before any second, always `non_exhaustive`, a cursor bound to query, caller and the reader's visible set, and handles issued only for the page shown. **Read:** `outline` returns section identifiers (`S` + a digest of the whole heading path, so a repeated heading under another parent stays distinct) and page spans; `sections` accepts only those identifiers, `pages` only existing pages; each paper reads within its own share of the read cap, always advances at least one chunk, and returns its own continuation, so a late section is read directly and a long paper resumes without replaying its prefix. `brief` returns a cached IR-556 brief through `briefs.cached_brief`, which never queues or generates one. **Aggregate:** `count_records`/`corpus_facets` with the metadata predicate and snapshot, no topic argument, and a separate `ai_readable` fact. **Extract:** brief fields come from a ready cached brief via the comparison lane's `table_fields` (renamed from `_table_fields`); other fields (at most eight, five papers) are read under `InferenceTask.BRIEF` with the IR-556 reader, now parameterized by field set and able to verify a typed `metric` whose number is printed in its own cited quote. Each field stays `reported`/`not_reported`/`unsupported`; a paper read only in part turns `not_reported` into `unsupported`; a model failure is `unavailable`, never absence; a paper withheld or re-chunked during extraction contributes nothing. `purpose=screen` screens only the issued records under the existing twenty-candidate limit and reports a `subset_classification`. `run_research_tool --general` runs them as a user.

**Not built here.** No controller calls these tools yet (IR-593), so no HTTP path carries them; the authenticated boundary exercised is the user-scoped management command. Conversation presentation state does not exist (IR-592); the `Presentation` seam is what it will fill. Extraction batches are capped at six per paper per call. Retrieval weights remain application settings.

**Evidence (2026-10-15).** `apps/ai/research/tests/test_general_tools.py` (resolution, search, aggregation, contract, command) and `test_general_reading.py` (outline, late sections, continuation, stale cursor, revocation, cached brief and its invalidation, sourced fields, typed metrics, unsupported fields, screening scope), all with fake providers. `pytest apps/ai/research` plus the comparison, brief and IR-590 contract HTTP suites pass except `test_plan_profile.py` and `test_screen_profile.py`, which fail identically on `main` because this machine's `backend/.env` holds real model keys. The full backend suite on this branch has 47 failures, the same 47 as clean `main` (dc585f7) run with the same `.env`; none is in a module this change touches.

## IR-592 — conversation state and bounded follow-up context

**Requirement.** [General-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §4 under [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md); acceptance scenarios A01–A03, A08, A11, A12.

**Built.** `ConversationState` (migration 0030, additive; one row per Conversation, cascades with it) stores topic, filters, the ordered presented record ids, selections, exclusions, a pending clarification, the answer Turn, request and retrieval query separately, and the active ChunkSet hash of each shown paper. `Turn.context_revision` records which revision a Turn committed. `apps/ai/conversation_state.py` holds the value and its transitions (topic change, find-another, select by ordinal), `commit` (inside `record_turn`'s transaction, after the durable guard), `snapshot`, `reissue_handles` and `conversation_context`. Only a `generated` Turn advances the state; a listing fixes order from its shown records, never its screened-out candidates; a comparison selects its pair; an answer citing only already-shown papers keeps the order and one citing others clears it. A commit against a stale revision keeps the newer choices and clears the displayed order (`basis=conflict`). A snapshot rechecks `visible_to` on every read: a revoked paper keeps its slot and resolves to nothing, and a changed source version is reported stale. With no row, state is rebuilt from stored Turns without writing; topic is never guessed and passage-only history stays unresolved. The request path pins the revision before any model call (`ChatQuestion.context_revision`); a queued run stores it at submission, so a retry answers against the context it was asked in. Turns citing a now-unreadable Record are dropped from the verbatim window (chat views, override, evidence shadow parity), memory recall, the research planner history and therefore the resolver cache key. The planner receives the state's papers as this run's handles.

**Not built here.** The controller that would set topic, filters and clarifications (IR-593) does not exist, so today only legacy listing, comparison and passage Turns write state and topic stays blank; `conversation_context` has no caller yet. The older-turn summary is the questions and their cited ids, not a model-written summary. The migration was exercised on the test database only, not on a copy of a populated one.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `apps/ai/tests/test_conversation_state.py` (31 cases), including a two-thread real-transaction race (`ConcurrencyTests`) and two HTTP cases; `pytest apps/ai/research apps/ai/evidence` and the conversation, memory, history and durable suites pass with `AI_RESEARCH_LANE=off AI_JEV_ROUTING_ENABLED=False`, except `test_plan_profile.py` and `test_screen_profile.py`, which read this machine's `backend/.env` model settings.

## IR-603 — the tool run's Presentation comes from committed conversation state

**Requirement.** [General-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §3.2: "An ordinal is valid only against the relevant displayed set and state revision." Connects IR-591's `Presentation` seam to IR-592's `ConversationState`.

**Built.** `conversation_state.presentation(ctx)` is the one builder: the owned Conversation's committed displayed order (or its reconstruction at revision 0) as `Presentation(revision, record_ids)`, with no visibility check of its own — `resolve_papers` still reauthorizes every id through `disclosable`. `ToolRun.start` calls it, so every run constructed for a Conversation (research, listing, count, comparison, both management commands) carries it. `RunContext.context_revision` holds the revision the question was pinned to (`ChatQuestion.context_revision`, threaded through `RoutedChat`, `research_run`, `lane_events` and durable submission; the two operator commands pin `current_revision` at start). `ToolRun.current_presentation` returns the presentation only when its revision equals that pin; otherwise every ordinal is `match: none` with the limitation `stale_presentation`, and the resolver cache key carries no stale list. Another reader's Conversation presents nothing.

**Not built here.** No controller calls `resolve_papers` yet (IR-593), so no HTTP path exercises the ordinal. An unpinned run with a Conversation (`context_revision=None`) resolves no ordinal by design.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `apps/ai/research/tests/test_general_tools.py`: `test_ordinals_resolve_from_stored_conversation_state` (a listing committed through `record_turn`, ordinals resolved out of order, a hidden draft still `none`), `test_a_stale_state_revision_resolves_no_ordinal` (a second commit after pinning leaves both ordinals `none`, status `empty`, `stale_presentation`) and `test_another_readers_conversation_presents_nothing`. The IR-591 seam test now pins its revision explicitly, because an unpinned presentation no longer resolves. `test_general_tools.py` and `test_schema.py` pass. The full `apps/ai` suite fails the same tests as the IR-591 head (eb65ac7) with the same `.env`, except the IR-202 chunker property flake and `test_the_refusal_reaches_neither_prompt`, which passes 3 of 3 when its file runs alone with this change, so its full-suite failure is order-dependent.

## IR-593 — the bounded conversational controller

**Requirement.** [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md) and the [general-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §2, §7, §10: one controller where `PLAN` chooses tool calls, a clarification or ready-to-compose; only the application runs tools; `ANSWER` writes all public prose; answer capacity is reserved before research; rounds, tool calls, read concurrency and repair are bounded; duplicate no-progress loops stop without an LLM judge; versions are pinned per run.

**Built.** `apps/ai/controller/`: `loop.Controller` drives `general_tools()` through `ToolRegistry.invoke` with run-scoped handles, offers only the five general tools (no `plan_research`/`continue_plan`/`subtask_done`/`finish`), reads the original request, the resolved question as a hint, the committed conversation state and readable history, and re-gates every envelope each round (a revoked record or a changed metadata population drops the envelope and the last exchange). The finish signal is a JSON reply with no tool call (`prompts.decision_from`); planner text is never published. `compose.ReadyToCompose` → `AnswerComposer` is the ANSWER seam IR-594 replaces; it cites ledger passages through `generate_cited`, and an evidence request with nothing readable gets the existing `no_sources` wording, so empty, withheld and irrelevant read alike. `budget.ControllerBudget` reads six `AI_CONTROLLER_*` settings with no defaults (unset → unavailable, no model call); `FAKE_TEST_PROFILE` is spec §7's 4 rounds, 6 tool calls, 2 concurrent reads, 1 repair. Answer tokens and seconds for the answer plus its repair are reserved before every PLAN call and tool call. The run's wall clock is the shorter of `AI_RESEARCH_WALL_CLOCK_SECONDS` and the time left in the original deadline (a queued run's 30 s default is not extended). `ChatOrchestrator` takes the run's engine from `ChatQuestion.engine` or `CompositionRoot.engine` (`controller/versions.py`); durable submission pins it into `AskRun.request["pin"]`, a run without one is legacy, and an unknown engine ends `failed` with no Turn. The Turn's state commit names what was shown: papers cited in order, or a pending clarification.

**Recorded contradiction.** The IR-593 ticket asks for a "default-off controller" setting with legacy lanes as rollback. ADR-042 (Accepted) says there is no on/off switch and rollback is a code rollback; ADR-042 governs. No setting selects the engine: `NEW_RUN_ENGINE` in code stays `lanes-v1` until ADR-042's release gates pass (IR-594–IR-597), and tests select the controller through the composition root.

**Not built here.** Answer validation and the bounded repair (IR-594; the slot is only reserved); public activity events and serializers (IR-595 — the controller emits only `retrieval_started` and `done`, no tool names or routing); UI (IR-596). Reads run sequentially in waves no wider than the bound: a threaded executor needs `contextvars.copy_context` and per-thread database connections and belongs with IR-597's worker verification. The answer reserve is held in the controller's own token and time budget; it is not reserved in the admission ledger. The router's injection screening has no replacement yet. Record abstracts are context, not citable sources.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `apps/ai/tests/test_controller_http.py` (19 tests, `/ask/`, `/ask/stream/` and the queued worker, fakes only): no-tool direct answer composed by ANSWER with planner text discarded and only general tools offered (A08); discovery then rewording keeps the papers shown (A01/A08); ordinals resolved from the committed order, both papers read and cited (A01/A02); ceremony tool and malformed arguments rejected and stopping the run; duplicate and no-new-evidence loops stopped; round and tool-call limits; answer reserve preventing research; unset limits refusing; Paper Chat scope; a failed tool plus a PLAN outage keeping supported parts (A09); clarification composed and kept pending; revocation mid-run reaching neither planner nor answer; a stale metadata count dropped; engine pins surviving a rollback in both directions and an unknown pin ending without a Turn.

## IR-594 — grounded answers with typed validation and one bounded repair

**Requirement.** [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md) and the [general-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §5, §6.2: model-composed answers at the requested depth (two-paper tables allowed, no tool narration); a draft with source bindings, typed numeric facts and an optional analytical summary; validation of citation identity, current access and source version, numeric bindings and completeness; harmless numbering, equations and quoted phrases pass while a number bound to the wrong paper or metric fails; exactly one repair, then supported parts kept and security/version failures fenced; the validated answer delivered whole, and an honest unavailable state with safe sources on model failure.

**Built.** `apps/ai/answers/assurance.py` is the contract and its checks: `parse_draft` splits the Markdown from an optional fenced `bindings` JSON block (facts bound to a source and metric or to a computed fact's name, papers bound to a source, `summary`), which never reaches a reader; `check` works per sentence/table row and returns reason codes (`unknown_citation`, `source_withdrawn`, `unsupported_number`, `unsupported_count`, `wrong_binding`, `completeness_overclaim`, `malformed_bindings`). Math, code, quotes, figure/table labels and list/heading numbering are skipped; a number in a cited sentence must be in a cited source or a computed/typed fact, an uncited percentage, decimal or count must be a computed or validated typed fact, and a count of papers may equal the distinct papers the sentence cites. An exhaustive claim needs a metadata count and no limitation. `safe_summary` drops a summary carrying markers, snake_case identifiers or tool/routing words, or a number the answer lacks. `controller/compose.AnswerComposer` replaces IR-593's single call: a new answer policy and contract prompt (`ANSWER_PROMPT_VERSION = "answer-1"`, now pinned per run), one repair with code-level feedback quoting the draft's own failing sentences, skipped when a source was withdrawn or the run is cancelled; afterwards failing sentences are removed with a plain note, an answer left empty becomes `unavailable` with `UNSUPPORTED_TEXT`, and an outage or reasoning-only reply is `unavailable` with the still-current sources. The controller supplies `ReadyToCompose.current` (readable, disclosable, chunk in an active, undeleted `ChunkSet`) and records the final codes in `ResearchRun.validation_codes`. `GroundedAnswer` gains `reasoning_summary` and `validation`.

**Not built here.** The summary is produced and validated but not stored on the Turn or sent to the reader: the public `reasoning_summary`/`activity` protocol is IR-595, the UI IR-596. The repair uses the same evidence; the spec's optional targeted re-retrieval is not built. The legacy lanes keep their behaviour per ADR-042, including `research/validation.py` whole-answer withholding and the comparison lane's sentence filter. Untyped numbers are checked by literal membership in the cited sentence's sources, so it cannot tell a right number under the wrong metric, or which of two papers cited together a number belongs to; typed bindings and human review cover those. Years in a cited sentence are not checked. Numbers derived by arithmetic fail and are removed after the repair. An exhaustive claim passes on any metadata count with no limitation, whatever that count's predicate. A paper named in prose without a `papers` binding is not checked; "never invent a title" is held by the prompt alone. `NEW_RUN_ENGINE` is unchanged. `test_a_run_pinned_to_the_legacy_engine_never_reaches_the_controller` fails on a developer `.env` on clean `origin/main` as well (a legacy-path PLAN call); it is not caused by this change.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `apps/ai/answers/tests/test_assurance.py` (16, pure): A10 harmless formatting, years and abbreviations pass; removal leaves no orphan list markers or header-only tables; wrong-paper and unsupported numbers fail; uncited counts and percentages; invalid markers and handles; wrong metric, paper and computed-fact bindings; malformed bindings; exhaustive claims; withdrawn sources fenced; summary safety. `apps/ai/tests/test_answer_composer.py` (13, no database): one repair with feedback, no second; partial delivery with a note; nothing supported → unavailable with sources; fencing without repair; zero reserved repairs; outage, reasoning-only and blank replies unavailable; repair outage keeps supported parts; two-paper table and policy text; no model call with no evidence. `apps/ai/tests/test_grounded_answer_http.py` (6, `/ask/` and `/ask/stream/`, fakes): the final public payload and stored Turn carry the repaired answer with no internal wording (A10); an invented citation removed after a failed repair; a wrong count corrected against the computed fact (A06); a two-paper table delivered whole (A07); an answer-model outage returning sources and no findings (A09). Semantic quality stays human-evaluated.

## IR-595 — public reasoning summaries and activity, separate from private provider traces

**Requirement.** [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md) and the [general-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §6, acceptance scenarios A11, A13, A14: the reader protocol carries `activity` and an optional `reasoning_summary` and nothing diagnostic; the same rules apply live, on replay and reconnect, and to old Turns; a reasoning-only completion is unavailable.

**Built.** `apps/ai/public.py` is the one server-side allowlist (`protocol=public-1`, chosen per request by body or query field or the `X-IRIS-Protocol` header; unknown values are a 400; omitting it keeps the legacy body byte-for-byte). It serves `/ask/`, `/ask/stream/`, `/ask/runs/<id>/stream/` (replay and reconnect, activity not repeated, an activity frame shares its event's `id:`) and `GET /conversations/<id>/` (old Turns projected; `routing`, `research`, `reasoning`, `had_reasoning`, validation codes and `agent_step` are never sent, and nothing is deleted). `Turn.reasoning_summary` and `reasoning_summary_source` (migration 0032) store the summary with its provenance and Turn lifecycle; it is shown only on a generated answer whose stored citations are all still readable. `safe_summary` omits (never repairs) a summary with tool or process narration, markup, citations, a vendor name, or a number, quoted phrase or proper noun absent from the answer and the cited passages; no extra model call. Adapters declare `LLMCapabilities` through their dialect (`display_summary`, `continuation_artifacts`; OpenRouter only); a vendor summary is a private `ToolCompletion.provider_summary` candidate, and opaque `ContinuationArtifact`s are echoed back unchanged by the controller and planner and never rendered.

**Allowlists and limits.** Nested blobs (`listing`, `comparison`, `sources`, citations, candidate `source`, `latest_run`) have explicit allowlists, so `observed` figures and topic `method`/`version` fields are never sent; run `reason` values are kept only from a fixed code set; `capacity_limited` becomes a detail-free `waiting` activity. The summary fact check covers numbers, quotes and capitalised names (sentence openers included, bar a small function-word set); lowercase claims are not checked. The code-owned listing wording still names the topic-count method as prose.

**Not built here.** No transcript-export endpoint exists, so there is nothing to project; a future one must use `turns_for_reader(..., public=True)`. The reader UI requests the protocol as of IR-596. `latest_run` on conversation detail is unchanged. The OpenRouter `reasoning_details` shapes follow the vendor's published format and were not run against a live account.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `apps/ai/tests/test_public_protocol_http.py` (supported, unsupported, narrating and think-tag summaries, reasoning-only completions, activity dedupe, legacy body unchanged), `test_public_history_http.py` (legacy history, summary withheld after a revoked source, queued replay and reconnect), `test_continuation_http.py` (signature round trip), `providers/tests/test_reasoning_capabilities.py` (supported, unsupported, redacted) and `answers/tests/test_assurance.py`, all on fakes.

## IR-596 — clean answers, one status row and collapsed reasoning summaries in the reader UI

**Requirement.** [ADR-042](../adr/042-general-tools-conversation-contract.md) and the [general-tools spec](../architecture-review/2026-10-15-askiris-general-tools-spec.md) §6.1–6.2, acceptance scenarios A13 and A14 (UI half): no routing, lane-override or tool narration in Ask IRIS or Paper Chat, one status row while working, an optional collapsed Reasoning summary above the answer, and the persistent mistake notice under both prompt boxes.

**Built.** `frontend/src/api/ai.ts` sends `X-IRIS-Protocol: public-1` on `/ai/ask/`, `/ai/ask/stream/`, `/ai/ask/runs/<id>/stream/` and conversation detail (Ask IRIS reopen and Paper Chat), and no longer sends `route_override`. `useAskStream` is driven by `activity` frames (`waiting`/`thinking`/`preparing`), keeps `startedAt` for a truthful elapsed time, and ignores every record already applied (same `id:`+event, or an id below the cursor), so a replayed `activity`, `text_delta` or `done` changes nothing. `StreamStatusRow` is the one live row (label in a single polite `role="status"`, elapsed time outside it, Stop for a followed queued run, pulse off under `prefers-reduced-motion`). `ReasoningSummary` is a button with `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` whose body is not rendered until opened, shown only for a non-empty `reasoning_summary`. `AskIrisNotice` sits below the prompt box in both. The routing panel, override chips, research-step trail, raw reasoning panel and the old stage status line are deleted, with their types (`RoutingDecision`, `ResearchTrace`, `ChatLane`, `reasoning`). `RAGChatPage` skips a followed run's `done` whose Turn is already in the transcript. Copy answer copies `content` only.

**Not built here.** There is no transcript export in the reader UI, so the opt-in "Include reasoning summary" choice does not exist; a future export must default it off. Elapsed time is not shown for a run followed after a reopen (its submission time is not in `latest_run`). Streamed `text_delta` text still renders as it arrives when the server sends it; the controller's atomic final answer (spec §5) is a server behaviour. Paper Chat now offers Stop on a queued run. The legacy protocol is no longer requested by the reader client, so the UI cannot show `routing` even against an old server. Colour contrast is not checked under jsdom.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `StreamStatusRow.test.tsx`, `ReasoningSummary.test.tsx`, `AskIrisNotice.test.tsx`, `StreamingMessageBubble.test.tsx`, `ChatMessageBubble.test.tsx` (labels, live region, Enter/Space toggle, collapsed body absent, empty summary, copy, notice, prohibited strings, axe); `useAskStream.test.tsx` (activity frames, dedupe on replay, summary on done, diagnostics never read); `api/ai.test.ts` (protocol on every request); `RAGChatPage.test.tsx` and `PaperChatDock.test.tsx` (live, reopened and Paper Chat transcripts, prohibited strings absent from the DOM, notice, duplicate `done`).

## IR-560: Discover keeps its query, results and facets across Back, reload and Load more

**Requirement.** IR-552 user stories 3–5: Back and reload restore the query, filters and results; facets cover the whole catalogue; an obsolete page request never mixes into newer results.

**Implementation.**
- `frontend/src/features/discover/discoverQuery.ts` is the one parsed query (search text, filters, sort). It round-trips through the URL, which is now the only copy, and unrecognised values fall back to their defaults; the page corrects the URL to match.
- Every page fetch, including Load more, carries the generation it was requested in, and a response from an older query or retry is dropped.
- How many pages a reader loaded, and their scroll position, are kept in `sessionStorage` for that query only. Returning asks for all of them in one request, at most 8 pages (the list endpoint's `max_page_size` is 100).
- `GET /records/facets/` returns the distinct `year_accomplished` values from the same queryset `list` serves (`visible_to(user)` narrowed to `PUBLICLY_VISIBLE_STATUSES`), with no counts. The Year menu used to be built from the cards loaded so far, using `created_at` as a fallback, so it could miss years beyond the first page and offer years the `year_accomplished` filter never matched.
- A failed first load, a failed Load more and failed filter data each have their own retry.

**Tests.**
- `apps/records/test_catalogue_facets.py`: 5 tests through the API, covering a year only beyond the first page, no-year Records, unpublished statuses never contributing even for the owner or RDCO, an empty catalogue and an anonymous refusal.
- `src/features/discover/discoverQuery.test.ts`: 11 tests on parsing, round-tripping and defaults.
- `src/features/discover/DiscoverPage.test.tsx` › "continuity": 9 accessible page tests, covering URL restore, the search box following a URL change, URL writes, correction of invalid values, Back from a Record restoring both pages, a delayed old Load more discarded after a filter change, catalogue-wide years, and separate recovery for filters and for Load more.

**Evidence, 2026-10-15, local against a pgvector Postgres:** `test_catalogue_facets.py` **5 passed**; the Discover test files **45 passed**. Full runs: `npm test` **1090 passed, 4 failed**, and `pytest` **3799 passed, 5 failed**; none of the failures is in a file this change touches. Three of the frontend failures (`Sidebar.test.tsx`) fail identically on `main` without this change, and the fourth (`PaperAiOverview` polling) is a timing test that has failed on `main` before. Four backend failures are known (the IR-202 chunker flake, two `test_complete_authority` subtests and the proxy-audit hash pin). The fifth is `test_enum_vocabulary`, which `main` fails too: IR-347 made `open` and `missing` governed while IR-549's code uses them for other concepts. Lint 0 errors; build green. Scroll restoration is not exercised under jsdom, which has no layout.

## IR-566: Saved Records and reading status follow the reader's account (design accepted, not built)

**Status.** Design accepted, not implemented. [ADR-044](../adr/044-account-backed-research-library.md) was **Accepted** on 2026-10-30 by Lee Jasmin Adolfo, with its product decisions answered: Likes are retired, reading history stays browser-only and is cleared on sign-out, and Calls & Conferences saves are out of scope. No code, migration or test exists for it yet, so no requirement here is met.

## IR-570: Ask IRIS on a phone: a full-width answer, with history and sources in drawers

**Requirement.** IR-552 user story 11: a phone reader gets one usable view, and history or tools never squeeze the answer. Paper entry keeps IR-569's default (Ask IRIS opens first), and this ticket owns the phone presentation and the drawers.

**Implementation.**
- `RAGChatPage` measures its own width (`useElementWidth`). From 900 px (`ASK_PANELS_INLINE_PX`: the 640 px reading minimum plus the 260 px history), history and sources sit beside the answer as before. Below that, both are closed by default and open one at a time as `ChatDrawer` dialogs. History comes from the start edge and sources from the end.
- While a drawer is open, the page behind it is `inert`. Focus moves into the drawer, Tab stays inside, Escape or the scrim closes it, and focus returns to the toggle that opened it. Widening the page or changing conversation closes the drawer.
- New chat moves into the history drawer, and the toggles become 44 px with `aria-haspopup="dialog"`. The delete control shows without hover on touch.
- Answers are not followed by a sources drawer opening on its own.
- `ConversationHistoryList` and `SourcePassages` are extracted, so the side panels and the drawers render the same lists.
- `useDialogFocus` is extracted from `Modal`, which now uses it, and it takes the opener explicitly: in Chrome, focus has already left a control that turns inert before the dialog's effect can read it.
- Surface roles (IR-558): the answer column is the page's one `surface`, and history and sources are `canvas`. `#FBFCFD` and the white toolbar, composer and sources header are gone.
- The page is `h-dvh` rather than `h-screen` with a 480 px floor, and `index.html` declares `interactive-widget=resizes-content`, so the composer stays above a software keyboard. Keyboard hints are hidden on coarse pointers, and the placeholder shortens to "Ask a follow-up…" below 900 px while the accessible name stays whole.
- The empty state centres with `m-auto`, so on a short screen it scrolls rather than clipping its top.
- Activity (IR-504's status line and research steps; superseded by IR-596, whose `StreamStatusRow` tests replace it): `StreamStatusLine` is one polite live region placed under the answer text, with Stop beside it. It stays mounted while empty, so the next stage is announced.
- `LiveCandidates` drops its own `role=status`, because the status line already says "Screening N candidate papers (k checked)…".
- **Desktop also changes here, deliberately:** the status line and Stop now sit under the answer at every width, as the criterion asks.
- Paper (IR-569 delivered entry, Back to paper and the citation bar): the minimized bar's accessible name changes from "Show Ask IRIS" to "Ask IRIS: back to the answer", its two visible lines. A followed citation is now announced as Back to the answer.
- The spec's "header launcher" is, since IR-569, the round Ask IRIS icon or this bar. The test drives the bar.

**Tests.**
- `RAGChatPage.test.tsx` › "Ask IRIS on a phone (IR-570)": 7 page tests at a measured 360 px:
  - the answer is full width with nothing open and the composer reachable, with the short placeholder;
  - the history drawer is labelled, keeps focus, makes the page inert, and gives focus back on Escape;
  - the history drawer's close button returns focus;
  - New chat works inside the drawer;
  - sources stay closed after a cited answer, then open as a drawer and return focus on close;
  - widening closes the drawer and restores the column;
  - at 1100 px, history sits beside the answer as before.
- `StreamingMessageBubble.test.tsx`: one polite live line, after the answer text, carrying the screening count; the live region persists while empty.
- `PaperViewPage.test.tsx`: at 360 px, entry closes the navigation drawer, and an unsent draft survives Back to paper and the bar without creating a Conversation. The reader is the same element throughout, so its page and scroll position are kept rather than rebuilt; jsdom cannot scroll, so the position itself is not asserted. This characterises IR-569's behaviour and passed on first run.

**Deliberate test changes.**
- `CandidateList.test.tsx` "announces progress once, in a status region" became "leaves announcing progress to the stream's status line, so it is said once". The count's text is pinned in `CandidateList.test.tsx` (`statusLineText`), and the single announcement in `StreamingMessageBubble.test.tsx`.
- Queries for the button named "Show Ask IRIS" in `PaperChatDock.test.tsx` and `PaperViewPage.test.tsx` now ask for "Ask IRIS: back to the answer", following the rename above.

**Evidence, 2026-10-15.**
- `npm test`: **1101 passed, 3 failed**, the same 3 `Sidebar.test.tsx` failures as unmodified `main`.
- After the browser-driven fixes, the touched files: **184 passed**.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean; lint 0 errors (6 warnings, none in touched files); the impeccable design detector reports no findings.
- Recorded browser checks: headless Chrome against Django and Vite on a throwaway pgvector database seeded by `seed_demo`, signed in as `student@cit.edu`.
  - At **360×780** there is no horizontal scroll, history is not beside the answer, and the composer is visible (top 716, bottom 764).
  - The history drawer takes focus on "Close chat history", and 12 Tabs leave focus in it. Escape returns focus to "Show chat history".
  - With the viewport shrunk to 360×420 as a keyboard would, the composer stays visible (bottom 404).
  - **768×1024** gives the same drawer results.
  - **1280×900** keeps history beside the answer.
- Two defects found in the browser were fixed before commit: focus fell to `<body>` on close, and at 360 px the empty state clipped its top while the placeholder wrapped.
- A two-axis review (standards and spec) followed. It moved the drawer to the type scale and the Paper drawer's shadow, made the delete control 44 px on touch, and dropped `aria-pressed` from toggles that open a dialog. After those fixes: `npm test` **1101 passed, 3 failed** (the same `Sidebar.test.tsx`); `npm run build` passes.
- Not checked:
  - a real phone keyboard: Chromium emulation only, so composer reachability is shown for Chromium, and Safari ignores `interactive-widget`;
  - the sources drawer in a browser: the dev corpus has no indexed records, so no answer carries citations;
  - browser zoom and reduced motion.


## IR-562: Ask IRIS keeps each answer and its sources with the Conversation that asked

**Requirement.** IR-552 user stories 12, 14 and 16. Switching conversations while an answer is still arriving never puts that answer, or its sources, in the wrong conversation, and the sources panel describes the answer the reader selected. Before this, the panel showed the latest answer that had sources: ask A (cited), then B (uncited), and A's papers stayed beside B.

**Implementation.**
- `features/ai/conversation/useConversationClient.ts` is Ask IRIS's one Conversation client. Per conversation, it keeps the transcript, the unsent draft, the selected answer and the answer in flight.
  - Loading goes through IR-548's owned route (`open`), and Stop and Retry through IR-548's run routes. The page keeps the address and nothing else.
- Every answer is bound to the conversation that asked and to a request id minted at send. Its events and result apply only while that request is still the conversation's current one.
  - A late answer lands in its own conversation, even after the reader has moved on.
  - A load is dropped if the reader has since opened another conversation, or if an answer landed in that conversation while it was loading, since the transcript already held is newer.
- **Behaviour change:** moving to another conversation no longer stops following the answer. Stopping cut a request-bound answer off, and the server then stored a fragment. Stop remains the reader's explicit cancellation. Because of this, the composer of another conversation is free while one is still answering.
- The stream's event handling moves out of `useAskStream` into `conversation/answerStream.ts` (`streamAnswer`), a plain function, so several answers can be followed at once. `useAskStream` keeps its interface for Paper Chat, which IR-571 moves onto the client.
- Sources follow the selected answer, which is the latest until the reader picks another with its "N sources" button (`aria-pressed`).
  - The panel and drawer name the answer by its question.
  - An answer that cited nothing says "This answer cited no passages." rather than showing an older answer's sources.
  - The toolbar's Sources toggle is enabled once any answer exists, an ended-run notice included.
- Each conversation keeps its own draft (`ChatInput` takes `draft` and `onDraftChange`). A handed-off draft still rides in router state, so reloading the conversation's address keeps it.
- Activity is unchanged: the one polite status line under the answer (IR-570, IR-596). Each conversation shows only its own.

**Tests.** `RAGChatPage.test.tsx` › "each answer stays with the conversation that asked (IR-562)", 10 page tests on delayed streams:
- An answer still arriving stays out of the conversation the reader moved to, and its stream is not aborted.
- Returning mid-answer shows the question and live line, then the answer, once.
- The live line and Stop show only in the answering conversation, and another conversation's composer is free.
- Each conversation keeps its own unsent draft.
- A newer uncited answer shows "cited no passages" and its own question, never the older passages.
- Selecting an earlier answer's sources shows them and names it.
- A cited answer that lands after a mid-answer switch is selectable with its sources in its own conversation.
- An answer that lands while its conversation is loading is kept rather than replaced by the older copy. With the guard removed, this test fails.
- A handed-off draft survives reloading the conversation's address.
- One conversation's sources never appear in another.

Five failed before the change, and the live-line, sources-carry and reload-draft guards passed. The stale-load test was added after review and fails without its guard.

**Evidence, 2026-10-16.**
- `npm test`: **1130 passed, 3 failed**, the same 3 `Sidebar.test.tsx` failures as unmodified `main`. A full run once also hit the PaperAiOverview polling flake that IR-601 fixes. It passed 3 out of 3 when run alone.
- `tsc --noEmit` is clean, lint has 0 errors (no warning in touched files), and `npm run build` passes. The impeccable design detector reports no findings.
- A two-axis review (standards and spec) followed. It moved loading into the client, bound loads to the conversation's answer revision, carried a selection across a reload by Turn, renamed the composer props to match Paper view, and added the mid-answer sources and stale-load tests.
- Not checked: a browser run. The dev corpus has no indexed records, so no answer carries citations there.


## IR-578: Back to the answer after following a citation, even after a reload

**Requirement.** IR-552 user stories 17–20. A reader follows a citation into the Manuscript, checks it, and returns to the same answer. This holds after a reload and across Records. It changes IR-434's page-citation links (a linked follow-up; IR-434 is not reopened).

**Design basis.** No ADR covers it. It follows the IR-578 acceptance criteria and reuses ADR-019's owned Conversation model and the IR-299 re-resolution rule. No backend code changes: the owner-scoped `GET /ai/conversations/<id>/` already re-resolves every stored citation against the reader (`apps/ai/conversations.py`, `_resolve_citation`).

**Implementation.**
- A citation link names its answer by ids only (`lib/citedPage.ts`, `citationHref`, `useCitationLink`): `/records/<id>?page=N&conversation=C&turn=T&cite=M`, plus `from=paper` from Paper Chat. The question and the quote never go in an address.
  - `CitationConversationContext` (set by the Ask IRIS page and Paper Chat) and `CitationTurnContext` (set by each answer bubble and the sources panel) supply the ids, so every citation link (chip, passage link, comparison page link) builds them the same way.
- Router state is trusted only when this page load wrote it for the answer the address names (`trustedCitation`). A browser reload keeps router state, so a stamp from another load is treated as stale.
- Otherwise the paper view re-reads the citation through the reader's own Conversation (`paper-view/useCitationReference.ts`):
  - **passage**: the page and regions as they read now win over the address's page.
  - **changed** (re-chunked): it highlights nothing and says so. It lands on the page the citation was stored with, which the note offers as where the passage was when the answer was written, not as the passage.
  - **missing** (no longer in the answer) and **elsewhere** (a citation into another Record): each highlights nothing and says which.
  - **gone** (someone else's or a deleted Conversation, which reads like a missing one): it highlights nothing and offers no Back to answer.
  - A Record the reader lost is a 404 page before any of this, and the backend drops that citation anyway.
- The reader waits for the re-read before it lands. It lands once per navigation, so landing on the address's page would not move again to the page the re-read gives.
- **Back to answer** is in the shared reading header (`ReadingHeader`, `onBackToAnswer`).
  - From Ask IRIS it goes to `/ai/conversations/C?turn=T`. The page selects that answer, opens its sources, and opens the transcript at it (`ChatMessageList`, `anchorTurn`).
  - From Paper Chat it opens the panel on that Conversation, about the paper it belongs to, and scrolls the transcript to the Turn (`PaperChatPanel`, `returnTo`). Arriving after a reload still pins nothing (IR-355); the button is the way back.
- Overview citations are page context. The chip's name ends ", page context", the link carries `context=page`, and the paper marks only regions on the stated page, with a note saying so. An Overview page cannot be re-read from its address, so after a reload its kept router state is dropped: the page lands as page context with nothing marked, and the note does not claim a mark. Passage citations keep every region, so a passage over two pages stays highlighted on both.
- A comparison's page links name their answer but no `cite`: a comparison's markers are not the answer's citation markers, so re-reading one could highlight the wrong passage.
- A citation landing still overrides the reader's kept place: there is no persisted reading position, and `useReadingPosition`'s in-memory place yields to every new landing (IR-354). No test targets this separately.
- "Restores the scroll position" is met by opening the transcript at the answer (its top), not by restoring the exact pixel offset the reader left.

**Tests.**
- `lib/citedPage.test.ts` › "the way back to an answer (IR-578)", 10 tests: ids-only links, `from=paper`, `context=page`, plain links unchanged, address parsing, router state trusted only from this load and for this answer, and an Overview page trusted only from this load.
- `PaperViewPage.test.tsx` › "back to the answer after following a citation (IR-578)", 12 tests:
  - Re-read after a reload: the page reads 14, not the address's 12, with both regions of a two-page passage.
  - It lands only once the re-read is in.
  - Reload-kept router state is not trusted.
  - A citation followed in this load needs no fetch.
  - Re-chunked, no-longer-cited, other-Record and not-the-reader's cases each degrade with their own note, and only the last offers no Back to answer.
  - Back to answer reaches the Ask IRIS address.
  - A paper reached without an answer offers none.
  - Overview page context marks only its page, and after a reload marks nothing and says only that it is page context.
- `ComparisonTable.test.tsx`, 1 test: a page chip links back to its answer with no `cite`.
- `PaperViewPage.test.tsx` › IR-355 block, 2 tests: Paper Chat citations carry their conversation and answer, and Back to answer after a reload on the other paper reopens X's conversation, pinned.
- `RAGChatPage.test.tsx` › "back to the answer from a citation (IR-578)", 2 tests: `?turn=` selects that answer, shows its sources and scrolls to it, and every citation link names its conversation and Turn.
- **Changed deliberately:** `PaperAiOverview.test.tsx` expected `/records/7?page=12`. An Overview link now carries `&context=page` and its name says page context.
- Six tests were checked red with their fix removed: reload trust, re-resolution, page-context filtering, the Ask IRIS anchor, the Paper Chat return and the wait before landing.
- **HTTP citation resolution** is the backend's existing suite, unchanged by this ticket: `apps/ai/tests/test_conversations_http.py` covers dropped invisible citations, tombstoned chunks degrading to a record-level link, and someone else's conversation as a 404. `test_citation_regions_http.py` covers a replayed turn re-resolving regions. They were not re-run locally (no backend change); CI runs them. Two cases this now depends on have no explicit backend test, and are a follow-up for `apps/ai`'s owner: a degraded pointer keeps `page` and carries no `regions`, and a chunk in a superseded (inactive) ChunkSet degrades the same way as a tombstoned one.

**Evidence, 2026-10-30.**
- `npm test`: **1305 passed, 0 failed** (after `npm ci`, as `main`'s dependencies had moved).
- `tsc --noEmit` is clean, `npm run lint` has 0 errors (its warnings are all on `main` already), and `npm run build` passes.
- A two-axis review (standards and spec) followed. It added the elsewhere case, the reload rule for Overview pages, the honest page-context and re-chunked notes, the comparison link without `cite`, one parser named for what it reads (`addressNumber`), one `AnswerReturn` type, one page-context signal, and an accessible-tree assertion in place of an attribute query.
- Not checked: a browser run. The dev corpus has no indexed records, so no answer carries citations there.


## IR-597 — Controller recovery, budgets and source safety

Authority: ADR-042 and general-tools specification sections 7/10, scenarios A11, A12 and A15.
See [controller recovery runbook](../engineering/CONTROLLER_RECOVERY.md) for the criterion-to-test map,
recovery/rollback procedure, conservative accounting rules and safe operational signals.

The added suites exercise immutable controller bounds and cumulative pre-work charges on redelivery;
shared paid ANSWER/repair reserve; stale attempt, terminal/deleted/deadline admission fences;
unsupported version bundles before preparation; active source-version and metadata reauthorization;
revoked facts during composition; malicious source instructions and closed capabilities; and
independent-interpreter crash, supersession, cancellation and duplicate-delivery races on real Postgres.
Existing authenticated journal/reconnect/public-history, conversation-revision, cache and store-failure
suites are reused. No source-bearing checkpoint is restored: evidence and handles are rebuilt.

Execution evidence (2026-10-16): the stale-chunk regression failed before the source-version fix.
The fresh full backend run completed with **4,187 passed, 5 failed, 1 xpassed**. Four failures were
legacy history fixtures lacking current chunk provenance; their setup was corrected without weakening
assertions. The fifth was Windows CRLF conversion of `proxy_starter.json`: its committed LF bytes
match the pinned audit, and restoring those exact bytes fixed the check without changing the audit or
file content. The subsequent affected-area run passed **240 tests**, including all five prior failures;
the new controller/recovery/budget/metadata/paid-reserve suites also passed in the earlier **91-test**
focused run. Backend static checks pass. Django's configuration check reports only the existing
`axes.W004` deprecation. New process tests use real Postgres and deterministic vendors, not paid calls.

Deliberate existing-test changes: synthesis fixtures now issue actual current chunks instead of invented
IDs/hashes; history, permission, public-summary and scoped-planner fixtures cite current chunks so their
assertions test permission/scope loss rather than fail earlier on missing provenance. The answer-reserve
exhaustion expectation now requires unavailable rather than an unfinished search reported as no matches.
Missing/versionless citation rejection remains explicitly covered by new tests. Standards and spec reviews
found source/metadata/history and recovery-evidence gaps; fixes and regressions were reviewed again with
no outstanding findings. Full final-commit CI status is recorded in the PR. No model-quality,
paid-capacity or production-readiness claim.

## IR-619 — assignment deadlines and extensions

**Authority.** [ADR-043 §§1–6](../adr/043-review-turnaround-reminders-and-escalation.md), accepted. The ticket's reference to Proposed is stale; implementation follows the accepted ADR.

**Implemented.** Admin-maintained Turnaround and Holiday tables; assignment clocks fixed at opening, counting working days in Manila and due at 23:59; no backfill for existing assignments. Own overlapping document requests share a pause and record system Extensions on resumption. New Versions restart only the requesting parties' clocks. Reassignment and nomination into an active assignment preserve its date. The office's coordinators alone may extend later with a reason; Adviser manual extension is refused. Every Extension and its mandatory audit event commit together. Tracker, seats, My Reviews and assignment capabilities project deadlines and retain earlier-round history. The extension route is declared in IR-615's generic matrix (`apps/tests/access/table.py::REVIEW_DEADLINES`, a partial `reviews` declaration that leaves the app pending for IR-616), and its configured-success cases in `apps/tests/test_authorization_matrix.py::ASSIGNMENT_EXTENSION_ACCESS`. Details: [deadline contract](ASSIGNMENT_DEADLINES.md).

**Evidence (2026-10-29).** Rebased on main after IR-166 and IR-632; `audit/0006` merges IR-166's `0005_auditevent_insert_only` with this branch's `0005_alter_auditevent_event_type`, and a redundant local import in `submit_new_version` that shadowed IR-632's module-level `RecordAssignment` was removed. Against PostgreSQL, `pytest apps/reviews apps/audit apps/documents apps/records/test_seed_demo.py apps/tests` ran **948 tests: 934 passed, 14 skipped (container lacks the repo root), 0 failed**; `makemigrations --check` reported no changes; scoped flake8 passed. On a `pg_dump` copy of the development database, reviews/0017 and audit/0005–0006 were unapplied and reapplied: all **28** existing assignments stayed undated and the Turnaround and Holiday tables stayed empty. No frontend files belong to IR-619. No CI pass or human approval is claimed here; CI status is recorded in the PR.

The new integration and migration tests are `test_deadlines.py`, `test_deadline_migration.py` and `AssignmentExtensionMatrixTests`. They cover pauses, date preservation, fresh rereview clocks, coordinator authority, audit rollback, history and deadline-independent acts. The older cutover fixture helper now temporarily removes only the retired-party constraint, instead of rewinding away later columns; its expected outcomes remain unchanged. Two independent static reviews found issues in test drivers, historical-schema fixtures and extension-history projection; those findings were addressed. Reminders and escalation remain sibling tickets.

## IR-622 — My Reviews sorts and filters by due date, and shows overdue plainly

**Authority.** [ADR-043 §§1–6](../adr/043-review-turnaround-reminders-and-escalation.md) (Manila working days, holidays excluded). IR-374's [redesign spec](../frontend_redesign_spec.md) story 72 (the view kept in the URL, so back and bookmarks work), which this extends to sort and filter. [ui-ux/16 §5](../ui-ux/16-paper-view-and-review.md) is updated to match.

**Implemented.** Every My Reviews row carries the deadline fields; a review row with no seat carries the empty payload, and a done seat is never overdue, even on an assignment still active for a colleague. The rows also carry `overdue_days`: Manila working days late, counted by the server because the browser cannot know the holiday table. `GET /reviews/mine/` takes `sort=due|waiting` (soonest due by default, undated rows last) and `overdue=1` on To review and In review. Done refuses both with 400, and `overdue` never narrows the counts. The page keeps both in the URL, shows *Due Fri 7 Nov* formatted in Asia/Manila and says overdue in words (*2 working days overdue*, or *Overdue* before a working day has been missed), with the badge's colour only repeating the text. No client code computes a date. Details: [deadline contract](ASSIGNMENT_DEADLINES.md#my-reviews-ir-622).

**Evidence (2026-10-29).** The tests are `test_my_reviews.py::DueDateTests` (REST seam: the working-day count across a weekend and a holiday, both sorts, the overdue filter and its counts, a finished seat beside an open one, the 400s), `myReviews.test.ts` and `MyReviewsPage.test.tsx` (accessible tree and axe). Results:
- Against PostgreSQL, `pytest apps/reviews apps/tests apps/audit`: **554 passed, 11 skipped, 0 failed**. pytest's summary line did not print through the container stream, so this was counted from its progress output. The full backend suite could not run: the shared Docker engine ran out of memory under other sessions' suites. An earlier run of the reviews app showed two failures and two errors in migration tests; all four passed when rerun alone.
- `npm test`: **97 files, 1190 tests passed**. `tsc --noEmit` is clean, and eslint reports 0 errors (7 pre-existing warnings in untouched files). flake8 on the touched backend files is clean.

Five existing request-shape expectations in `MyReviewsPage.test.tsx`, and the `readView` / `viewSearch` expectations in `myReviews.test.ts`, were updated deliberately: the request and the view gained `sort` and `overdue`. The "never clips a long title" test asserts classes (no clamp, no no-wrap), following `entryHeadings.test.tsx`, because jsdom does no layout.

**Rendered check.** On a copy of the development database, at **1280 px** with real server data, the overdue row came first with *2 working days overdue · Due Tue 27 Oct* and the long title wrapped in full. **360 and 768 px were not checked in a browser**: the Docker port proxy kept dropping the dev server. That check is still owed.

A standards review and a spec review ran on the diff. The spec review found that a finished seat on a still-active assignment showed as overdue; that is fixed, with a test. The stale ui-ux/16 §5 is updated. Choices left for a reviewer: switching to Done and back resets sort and filter, and sort values are strings rather than an enum. No CI pass or human approval is claimed here.
## IR-623 — coordinator console

**Authority.** ADR-032 §4 (a coordinator assigns, reassigns and withdraws seats in their own office) and [ADR-043 §6](../adr/043-review-turnaround-reminders-and-escalation.md) (a coordinator extends with a reason). Parent IR-612. No new decision right is added.

**Implemented.** `GET /api/v1/reviews/office/` (`apps/reviews/office_console.py`): one office's unfinished seats and unclaimed pool assignments on records in review, each with the IR-619 deadline projection, ordered overdue first, then soonest due, then undated; and every active member's open and overdue seat count, idle members included. `office` defaults to the office the caller coordinates; anyone who does not coordinate it gets 403, declared in IR-615's matrix (`REVIEW_DEADLINES["reviews-office"]`, still a partial `reviews` declaration). *Reassign* now requires a reason: the new seat stores `reason` and `reassigned_from` (`reviews/0018`), and `SEAT_REASSIGNED`'s metadata carries it (`docs/evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md`). The tracker gains `reassignments`, a `label` on each deadline-history row and `actor_name` on each extension; names and reasons are null to a viewer who may not read the review (IR-479). Frontend: `/review/office` (`OfficeConsolePage`, with `ReassignDialog` and `ExtendDueDateDialog`, and *Assign* through the existing `AssignReviewerDialog`), linked from My Reviews only when `review_access.is_coordinator` is set; the Review timeline shows reassignments and extensions (`timelineEntries.ts`).

**Evidence (2026-10-29).** Frontend, run locally: `npx vitest run` gave 99 files and **1215 tests passed**, including `OfficeConsolePage.test.tsx` (accessible-tree queries and axe), the two My Reviews entry-point tests and the two new `timelineEntries` cases. Each was observed red before its implementation. `tsc --noEmit` was clean, and eslint reported no finding in a touched file. Backend, run locally: `manage.py check` (one existing `axes.W004` warning), `makemigrations --check` (no changes) and flake8 on the touched modules were all clean. **The backend tests were not run locally**: Docker's engine was down and no local PostgreSQL with pgvector was reachable. `test_office_console.py`, the reassign cases in `test_seats.py`, the audit metadata case in `apps/audit/tests/test_workflow_events.py` and the access matrix are left to CI on the PR, and `reviews/0018` has not yet been applied to a copy of a realistic database.

## IR-598 — conversation set, scorer and controlled-pilot documents

Authority: ADR-042 and general-tools specification §9–§11 (scenarios A01–A15).

**Built.** `apps/ai/evaluation/conversations.py` validates a conversation set (schema, per-turn labels, 100-conversation minimum, per-family coverage, development/held-out leakage by paper title and topic, a named reason for any shared item) and computes per-split lock digests. `conversation_scoring.py` scores human reviews per arm and family with Wilson intervals and separate denominators, refuses arm comparisons whose corpus, models, data policy or limits differ, and sets `enablement_blocked` on any access breach. `manage.py eval_conversations` exposes `--validate`, `--lock`, `--write-lock` and `--score`; it collects nothing and calls no model. `docs/evaluation/general-tools/conversation_set_v1.json` holds 105 draft conversations (62 development, 43 held out, seven per family A01–A15) over the 40-paper arXiv proxy corpus, with `conversation_set_v1.lock.json`. The [pilot report](../evaluation/general-tools/pilot-report.md) and [rollout runbook](../evaluation/general-tools/rollout-runbook.md) record what was and was not measured and the open approvals.

**Not built, not measured.** `label_status` is `draft_unreviewed` and `labelled_by` is empty: an AI agent drafted every label, so no score on this set counts until a named person reviews it and the lock is rewritten. No arm was run, no human review, judge calibration, blind preference, latency, cost or capacity measurement exists (capacity unverified). `supported_unambiguous` (the 90% completion denominator, 36 of 105) is an agent drafting choice flagged for review. `--score` needs `--lock`, refuses draft labels without `--allow-draft` and needs run manifests; capacity counts as verified only with real providers, capacity fit, at least 100 distinct cold conversations and a campaign approval id. The proposed targets (90% completion, 95% claim, citation and page support, zero breaches, blind preference) are owner-review proposals, not approved gates. The runbook records one conflict between the ticket's "cohort" wording and spec §10's no-switch rule for Jive Tyler Revalde to settle. No deployment, vendor, data-retention, institutional-transmission or paid-campaign approval is granted.

**Evidence (2026-10-16).** `eval_conversations --validate` on the set: 105 conversations, 36 supported and unambiguous, no coverage gap, no leakage or topic-overlap warning (exact-string checks, evidence titles included; near-synonyms not caught), `valid`; `--write-lock` then `--validate --lock` both `valid`. The three new test files pass (24 tests). `python -m pytest apps/ai/evaluation`: 299 passed, 1 failed; the failure is the known Windows-only `test_the_proxy_audit_pins_the_proxy_set_it_audited` (CRLF checkout changes the proxy file digest) and predates this ticket. The scorer has only seen invented reviews.

## IR-606 — replay conversations and save run evidence

Authority: IR-606 and ADR-042's authenticated public reader boundary. `apps/ai/evaluation/replay.py` collects each development case into a fresh Conversation through `/conversations/` and `/ask/stream/`, then reads `/conversations/<id>/` after every attempted turn. It records public answer, citation/source IDs, saved Turn, reopened transcript, outcome, latency and explicit usage status. The `eval_replay_conversations` command validates the split lock, limits launch to selected development cases, supports a one-case deterministic smoke launch, and requires approval plus admission enforcement for real providers. Its manifest captures set, code, engine, protocol, prompt, corpus, permission, model, budget, cache and physical-attempt provenance without credentials or provider artifacts. `test_replay.py` exercises the authenticated API seam with deterministic providers; `test_replay_contract.py` covers order, isolation, cap stop and incomplete attempts; `test_replay_command.py` covers the scorer-readable smoke file and held-out rejection. A local run and its limits are recorded in the IR-606 implementation report; no paid or institutional collection is claimed.

## IR-607 — review conversations and score supported task completion

Authority: IR-607 under IR-605, ADR-042, and the general-tools evaluation contract's review rubric. Additive to IR-598's set and scorer contract.

**Built.** The lock now pins the corpus snapshot and rubric version as well as each split, records `locked_at`, and refuses to be written while A09 lacks distinct `missing_field`, `no_match` and `outage` cases (`a09_case`). `check_label_review` gates a named person's label review against the current set and corpus. `eval_review_packets` writes one reviewer's packet: rendered answers and citations checked through `Record.objects.visible_to`, a turn citing an unreadable record withheld whole, and no diagnostics, usage or other verdicts. `score_reviews` keeps two independent reviews and an adjudication in `review_audit`. It refuses a disagreement without an adjudication, an adjudication that is unfinished, made by one of the two reviewers or lacking two reviews, one person reviewing twice, any non-human reviewer, and claims without explicit support judgments. It drops `not_reviewed` records from every denominator. `--score` reports `counted` with reasons and, with `--counted`, refuses draft labels, a missing or fixture label review, no packets, fake providers, a run on another corpus, single-reviewed conversations and reviews with no stated `reviewer_kind`. It always refuses evidence outside a reviewer's packet and a label review changed after locking. It scores held-out reviews only from runs stamped after the lock with the locked rubric, and `eval_review_packets` will not package held-out output before then. The collector records a private per-turn stage from `ResearchRun` rows; the scorer reports stage counts beside verdicts.

**Not built, not measured.** No person has reviewed a label or a conversation. The draft set's A09 cases are untagged and its lock is stale under the new rules, so it can be neither locked nor counted. No real-provider run exists. Diagnostic stages exist only for the controller engine; the legacy engine writes no `ResearchRun`, so it reports `not_traced`.

**Evidence (2026-10-30).** New tests: `test_replay_diagnostics.py` (4, real Postgres, controller at the HTTP boundary: hand-off, no candidate and provider failure told apart; reader view identical with tracing on or off), `test_research_stage.py` (5, pure: recovery, model outage, budget stops, non-paper tools), `test_label_review.py` (7), `test_review_resolution.py` (9), `test_review_campaign.py` (10, committed fixtures at the run-file boundary, numbers counted by hand), `test_review_packets.py` (6, real Postgres, a private draft withheld from another reader and shown to its owner, held-out freeze). The stage classifier is only as precise as `ResearchStep` rows, which record a status per step but no item count. `python -m pytest apps/ai/evaluation`: the new tests and the IR-598/IR-606 tests pass; the known Windows-only `test_the_proxy_audit_pins_the_proxy_set_it_audited` fails as before. The CLI was run on the fixtures for `--validate --label-review --write-lock`, `--score` (uncounted, reasons printed) and `--score --counted` (refused).


## IR-620 — deadline reminders from an hourly beat task

**Authority.** [ADR-043 §7–§8](../adr/043-review-turnaround-reminders-and-escalation.md), accepted. The ticket says no `celery-beat` service exists; it did exist in both Compose files, with nothing scheduled. This change gives it its only entry rather than adding a second service.

**Implemented.** `CELERY_BEAT_SCHEDULE` runs `apps.reviews.tasks.send_deadline_notifications` at minute 0 hourly on `default`. `apps/reviews/reminders.py` computes the one moment each running, unpaused, dated assignment owes now (`due_soon` from 2 Manila working days before, `due_today` on the date), so catch-up sends the latest and skips a superseded one. `DeadlineMoment` (reviews/0018) is unique on (assignment, due date, kind, step); the insert is the idempotency, taken under the record-then-assignment lock with the assignment rechecked. Recipients are live Seat holders that are not done, the office's coordinators for a pool with no live Seat, or the record's Adviser for an unseated Adviser assignment, each filtered through `Record.objects.visible_to`. Notifications are in-app only (`notifications/0007` seeds three types); a failed one is logged and the batch continues. Nothing reads a due date to decide.

**Evidence (2026-10-29).** Against PostgreSQL in a backend container: `test_reminders.py` **13 passed**; `test_document_requests.py` 46 passed; `makemigrations --check` reported no changes; scoped flake8 passed; `celery -A config beat -l debug` loaded `deadline-notifications` on `0 * * * *`. A run of `apps/reviews apps/notifications apps/audit apps/documents apps/tests` reported no failures, but later full runs were cut short by Docker Desktop running out of memory (`Errno 12`, engine 502s), so the complete totals are CI's Backend job, recorded on the PR. No migration was applied to a copy of a realistic database: both migrations only add a table and seed three rows. No human approval is claimed here.

`test_reminders.py` covers: T-2 and due-today moments, re-runs sending once, an unclaimed pool going to coordinators with no duplicate for a later claim, done/withdrawn Seats and completed assignments, catch-up, Extension re-arming, a paused clock, no Turnaround, the Adviser, an unreadable recipient, a failed notification, two simultaneous runs (`TransactionTestCase`, two threads) and the beat entry naming a registered task. A deliberate mutation (dropping the visibility filter, the due-today branch and the lock recheck) failed five of them.

**IR-620 CI follow-up (2026-10-29).** `backend/entrypoint.sh` is now tracked as executable (`100755`). The development bind mount replaces `/app`, hiding the image-layer `chmod`, so the source checkout must carry execute permission for Compose to start Celery Beat. Script contents, the non-root user and the existing Beat startup/schedule smoke test are unchanged. Updated Compose CI is recorded on PR #321.

## IR-621 — overdue assignment escalation

**Authority.** [ADR-043 §1, §7–§8](../adr/043-review-turnaround-reminders-and-escalation.md). The original ticket said per Seat and once only; its description was corrected to assignment deadlines and repeats every five working days. Adviser assignments never escalate.

**Implemented.** The existing hourly deadline job sends the latest overdue moment to all active office coordinators, with unfinished reviewer names or an unclaimed Pool, and falls back to RDCO coordinators with a warning when the office has none. Each recipient passes `Record.objects.visible_to`. The existing ledger's assignment/due-date/kind/step key deduplicates concurrent ticks; an Extension or document pause changing the date re-arms it. `ASSIGNMENT_ESCALATED` (audit/0007) commits with the ledger, with metadata documented in [AUDIT_EXPORT.md](../evaluation/AUDIT_EXPORT.md). Notification delivery remains best effort. No workflow, clearance or Seat is changed.

**Evidence (2026-10-30).** `apps/reviews/test_reminders.py`: **28 passed** against PostgreSQL, including simultaneous escalation, repeat/catch-up, weekend/holiday, extension/pause, Adviser exclusion, RDCO fallback, visibility, unchanged workflow data and audit rollback/retry. The repeat regression failed on the original scheduler and passed with escalation implemented. Scoped flake8 and Django migration consistency passed; Django check reports only the existing Axes deprecation warning. Audit migrations through `0007` applied successfully to a disposable `pg_dump`/restore copy of the development database; `sqlmigrate audit 0007` confirms a SQL no-op (choices only), and the copy was removed afterwards. Full-suite and CI evidence are recorded on the PR. No human approval is claimed.

**IR-623 CI follow-up (2026-10-29).** The workload count field is renamed from `open` to `open_count` in the backend response, TypeScript type, renderer and HTTP/UI fixtures. It counts unfinished seats; it is not `DocumentRequestState.OPEN`. The shared vocabulary guard is unchanged. The HTTP fixture changes deliberately to pin the new field name. Focused checks and updated CI results are recorded on PR #322.
